The sixteen steps, starting from the section's own 0.000 km at Temple 23: 77.547, 6.686,
3.849, 28.547, 38.365, 9.211, 7.079, 6.539, 6.074, 8.192, 6.344, 9.847, 14.529, 58.069,
82.712, 54.911 km. They sum to 418.501, the whole line.

Three properties of that list carry most of the narratives below:

- **Five steps run over 30 km** — 77.547, 38.365, 58.069, 82.712 and 54.911 — and together
  they carry **311.604 km, 74.5%** of the section. `metadata.json`'s `description` declares
  the five and the longest of them, Temple 37 → 38 at 82.7 km.
- **Exactly one step falls inside the 25–30 km band the day cut looks in**, Temple 26 → 27 at
  **28.547 km**. That is why ten of the fifteen days end at a lodging or a town: the second
  arm of the rule is not an exception here, it is the normal case.
- **The narrowest step is Temple 25 → 26 at 3.849 km**, and Temples 24, 25 and 26 stand
  inside 10.535 km — the closest any three consecutive temples come in this section, against
  12.613 km for the next-tightest triple, Temples 30 to 32.

**Places.** `waypoints.geojson` holds **372** features. Per stage, by `stageIndex`: 22, 37,
61, 56, 47, 26, 20, 8, 6, 21, 19, 14, 20, 6, 9. Of the 372, **124 are bus stops** and 108 of
those fall on stages 1, 2 and 3 — which is why those three days are described by their bus
stops and no later day is. 38 are train stations, 39 toilets, 27 convenience stores, 33
accommodation, 16 temples, 8 towns (3 `town`, 4 `city`, 1 `village`), 5 viewpoints, 1
`water_source` and 1 `cultural_site`.

### What Tosa refused, on top of the shared list above

Everything under "What was refused" binds here unchanged, including **"discipline" and
"ascetic training" as what Tosa means** — both sit verbatim in this section's own
`description` and `cultural.historicalSignificance`, and both are refused on the reasoning
that refused "awakening" for Awa. No narrative below names the dōjō or its meaning. Beyond
that list, this section offered five things Awa did not:

- **`description` fields on two temple waypoints.** `temple-24` carries "Cape Muroto — where
  Kukai achieved enlightenment." and `temple-38` "Cape Ashizuri — southernmost point of
  Shikoku." Both are in this section's own `waypoints.geojson`, and both are refused: the
  first is the Kūkai claim the shared list already refuses, the second a geographic
  superlative that is not a temple, shrine, pass, town, distance or climb. Neither cape is
  named below as a place. The words "Cape Ashizuri" appear once, on stage 12, as the **name
  of a bus stop** — `node/9444928826`, `transport`/`bus_stop`, name "Cape Ashizuri", `ja`
  足摺岬 — in the same way Awa named the stations Yuki, Tainohama and Kiki.
- **`tags` on four temple waypoints** — `temple-24` `["temple","cape","historic"]`,
  `temple-27` `["temple","mountain"]`, `temple-38` `["temple","cape","remote"]`, `temple-39`
  `["temple","phase-boundary"]`. Refused. `mountain` and `remote` are terrain and character
  claims with no profile and no measurement behind them, and this is exactly the kind of
  sentence the no-elevation rule exists to stop.
- **`elevation` on three temple waypoints** — `temple-24` 160, `temple-27` 380, `temple-31`
  140. This qualifies the shared paragraph above: **"No elevation, anywhere in this
  pilgrimage" is true of the lines, the stage fields and the shipped packages, but not of
  every waypoint.** Awa has the same three (Temples 12, 20, 21), Iyo five and Sanuki seven,
  and none of the four sections' drafters has used them. They are point heights, not climb —
  a single number says nothing about what the day between two of them does — so nothing below
  cites one, and a reviewer should read any future sentence built on them as an error of the
  same kind as one built on `gainMeters: 0`.
- **The station named Awa.** `node/8383580512`, `train_station`, name "Awa", `ja` 安和, at
  252.730 km on stage 8. It is a real waypoint and it is left unnamed in the narrative,
  which calls it "a station": in a document about a pilgrimage whose first dōjō is Awa, the
  name would read as an error rather than a fact. Recorded here so the omission is visible.
- **Any account of the six waypoints that do not reach the line.** See below.

### The six that passed enrichment and failed the build

Six waypoints filed on stage 12 sit **1.75–2.08 km** from `route.main.geojson` and are
dropped by `build-ways` as off route, yet all six passed the 300 m enrichment corridor —
because that corridor measures against `route.geojson`, which is 727.397 km against the
walked line's 418.501 and carries the variants this section leaves out. Measured against
`route.geojson` the same six are **12 to 137 m** from a line. Three more, on stage 13, behave
the same way: 870, 1,120 and 1,486 m from the walked line, 39, 199 and 102 m from
`route.geojson`. **None of the nine is named in any narrative below**, and stage 13's entry
says in as many words that three of its six places never reach the day.

All sixteen boundaries snapped; none went proportional. The six bolded rows are the
misfilings, and the sign of Δ decides every one of them: a day-end that projects past its own
boundary vertex by any margin lands on the day that begins there. The widest is Tosa at
+17.30 m and the narrowest Henro House Maaru at +0.56 m; on the other side 民宿あわの里 sits on
its vertex to within a floating-point residual and is filed on the arriving day because that
residual is negative. It is the mirror of the Kanjizai-ji (Temple 40) case `metadata.json`'s
`provenance` records, where the residual is positive and the day-end is filed on the day that
begins there.

**Measurement.** As for Awa: every along-line kilometre and off-line metre below is the
perpendicular projection of the place's own coordinate onto the committed
`routes/shikoku-88-tosa/route.main.geojson`, computed with `projectOnLine` from
`scripts/ways/geo.ts`; boundary vertices come from `stageBoundaries` on the same line. The
sixteen temple waypoints carry no OSM id and are cited as `temple-24` … `temple-39`; every
other id is an OpenStreetMap node. Each **Distance** line gives the span between the two
boundary vertices, the figure `stages.json` declares, and `ways/report.json`'s `sliceKm`,
`ratio` and `passedGate` for the slice actually shipped.

  `node/11535055772` (`accommodation` / `guesthouse`), 19 m off the line, at 32.079 km.
  Second arm: no temple falls 25–30 km past 0.000 km, and the fallback is not confined to the
  band, so the day runs 32.08 km
- **Distance** 32.071 km between the boundary vertices against **32.1 km** declared. Shipped
  slice 31.889 km, ratio 0.9934, `passedGate: true`, `boundaryMode: snap`. Previously
  declared: **none** — this section's stages were cut in Task 5b and have never carried

    Hotsumisaki-ji at **77.547 km**, 22 m off the line; the section's own 0.000 km is Temple 23
  - "this day walks 32.1 of them" — the 32.071 km span, 32.1 declared
  - "Nothing on it carries a temple number" — none of the 22 features with `stageIndex: 0`
    carries a `templeNumber`. The feature at 0.000 km is
    `wp-osm-sacred_site-node369741886` (`sacred_site` / `church`, name "23 Yakuo-ji",
    `ja` 第23番札所 薬王寺, 55 m off) — one of the church-subtype twins `metadata.json`'s

```
theme:      Seventy-seven kilometres to the first temple
narrative:  Tosa's first temple stands 77.5 km from the section's first step, and this day walks 32.1 of them. Nothing on it carries a temple number. What the record holds instead is a railway — six stations, Hiwasa in the first half-kilometre, then Yamagawachi, Hegawa, Mugi, Sabase and Kaifu at 28.8 km — and, at 8.6 km, a ruined restaurant. No temple falls 25 to 30 km ahead of the section's start, so the day ends at a bed rather than a gate: the Haryugetu Guesthouse, 32.1 km on.
reflection: Hotsumisaki-ji is still 45.5 km ahead when this day ends — what does a day hold when nothing on it is a gate?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: the six stations project at **0.493 / 5.723 / 11.402 / 15.308 / 20.550 / 28.750 km**
at 49 / 159 / 204 / 90 / 46 / 84 m off, all `train_station`, all inside 300 m, and they are the
only six on the stage ✓. The ruins node is at **8.585 km / 24 m**, the section's **only**
`cultural_site` of 372 features ✓. None of the 22 features carrying `stageIndex: 0` has a
`templeNumber` ✓. `temple-24` **77.54710**, so 77.547 − 32.079 = **45.468** → 45.5 ✓. Span
**32.07072** (vertices 0 → 1145, `snap`) against 32.1 declared; no drops.

- **Ruling on "gate" and "bed", for the whole section.** They stand, and the ruling is the
  section's not the stage's. I checked all fifteen usages of "gate" and all three of "bed": in
  every one the referent is a temple, or an `accommodation` waypoint, that the same sentence or
  the same stage has already named or fixed unambiguously — "the day ends at its gate"
  (Kongōchō-ji), "59 m short of the gate" (Kongōfuku-ji), "sixteen gates" (the 16 `templeNumber`
  features). Neither word ever introduces a referent of its own. That makes them metonyms for a
  thing already grounded, not added facts: unlike Awa's struck "ordinary", which asserted
  something about a place that no field supported, "gate" asserts nothing about a temple that the
  sentence has not already said. Awa's "the first bed the record holds" and "Fifty-three
  kilometres between beds" cleared in Task 7a on the same reading, and consistency across the
  pilgrimage points the same way. **Eleven entries do not change.**
- **"What the record holds instead is a railway" stands.** Six `train_station` waypoints in
  order along 28.8 km, and the sentence names them all and attributes the claim to the record.
  Awa named three stations and cleared; the only step beyond that is calling the six of them a
  railway, which is a reading of their ordering and nothing more. It reaches for no terrain.
- **The ruined restaurant stands, on the Bandō precedent** the previous reader confirmed: the
  node's own `name` and its ruined state, and nothing else.

**Edited in Task 8b. One dataset phrase out, two figures out; the railway hedge kept, moved into the verb.**
Nine figures down to seven. This day begins at the section's own 0.000 km, so **none of its figures is a
cumulative section kilometre** — 77.5, 28.8 and 8.6 are all measured from where the walker stands on the
morning of day one, which is why 77.5 stays where stage 5's 153.3 could not.

```
theme:      Seventy-seven kilometres to the first temple
narrative:  Tosa's first temple stands 77.5 km from the section's first step, and this day walks 32.1 of them. Nothing on it carries a temple number. What is mapped instead is a railway — six stations, Hiwasa in the first half-kilometre, then Yamagawachi, Hegawa, Mugi, Sabase and Kaifu — and, 8.6 km in, a ruined restaurant. No temple falls 25 to 30 km ahead of the section's start, so the day ends at a bed rather than a gate: the Haryugetu Guesthouse.
reflection: Hotsumisaki-ji is still 45.5 km ahead when this day ends — what does a day hold when nothing on it is a gate?
```

- **"What the record holds instead is a railway" → "What is mapped instead is a railway".** Task 7b cleared
  this sentence on the ground that "the sentence names them all and attributes the claim to the record": six
  `train_station` waypoints are what the file holds, and *a railway* is a reading of their ordering. The
  attribution had to survive and only its noun changed. *Is mapped* carries the same hedge in the verb Awa's
  stage 3 and stage 4 now use — an unmapped railway would be a railway still, and neither version asserts
  anything about the ground. The six names follow immediately in both.

  consecutive pairs.** 3.849 km is the smallest of the sixteen steps, and the minimum distance
  between *any* two of the section's temples is necessarily the smallest consecutive step, so the

  them *could* have been named; none is, and the narrative is a count plus the temple spacing.

  on the day the section's refused `description` calls Temple 24 "Cape Muroto — where Kukai
  achieved enlightenment" would have put the refused sentence's own words on the page. **The
  omission is right and should stay.**

**Edited in Task 8b. No dataset word to remove; one inventory sentence out and two step figures with it.**
Ten figures down to seven. This narrative was already clean of the dataset's own vocabulary — one of only
three in the fifteen. What made it read as a survey was a single sentence, and it is the same sentence Awa's
stage 3 carried.

```
theme:      The first temple, and then two more
narrative:  Two days and 62.9 km have passed with no temple on them; this day reaches the first 14.7 km in. Hotsumisaki-ji, then Shinshō-ji, then Kongōchō-ji after the shortest step between any two temples in Tosa, and the day ends at its gate. More places stand on this day than on any other in the section, and forty-four of them are bus stops. Three temples inside 10.5 km, after seventy-seven and a half kilometres with none.
reflection: Seventy-seven kilometres for the first gate and ten and a half for the next two — which of those two rhythms is the pilgrimage?
```

- **"Sixty-one places stand on this day, more than on any other in the section, and forty-four of them are
  bus stops" → "More places stand on this day than on any other in the section, and forty-four of them are
  bus stops."** Awa's move in structure as well as in effect: the superlative is the fact, the inventory was
  the arithmetic behind it (61 against 56 / 47 / 37 / 26 / 21 / 20 / 20 / 19 / 14 / 9 / 8 / 6 / 6). **Left
  unhedged deliberately, for Awa's reason:** this is not an absence claim but a comparison between fifteen
  days counted the same way out of one file, and a reader who takes it as "this day has more on it than the
  others" is not misled by anything. *Forty-four of them are bus stops* is kept because a count of a subset
  is a positive existence claim — 44 bus stops do stand on the day — where a proportion of everything on it
  would not have been.
- **"then Shinshō-ji 6.7 km on, then Kongōchō-ji 3.8 km after that — the shortest step …" → "then Shinshō-ji,
  then Kongōchō-ji after the shortest step …"** Awa's stage 2 ruling on 1.1 km applied here: the figure was
  doing nothing the superlative was not. **3.849 km** against a next-shortest of **6.074**. 6.7 goes with it
  because the run is already bounded by "Three temples inside 10.5 km" in the closing sentence, and
  **6.686 + 3.849 = 10.535**.
- **"the shortest step between any two temples in Tosa" is carried word for word.** Task 7b found that here,
  unlike Awa's stage 0, the idiom is literally true of *any* two and not merely of consecutive ones, because
  the smallest distance between any two of a section's temples is necessarily its smallest consecutive step.
  The clause was **moved, not reworded** — it now follows "then Kongōchō-ji" rather than "3.8 km after that" —
  and the step it names is the same one.
- **The reflection stands**, and carries "Seventy-seven" and "ten and a half", both of which the narrative
  also keeps.

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

- **The superlative was moved and not reworded, and the move is safe.** "the shortest step between
  any two temples in Tosa" now follows "then Kongōchō-ji" rather than "3.8 km after that", and it
  names the same step: `temple-25` **84.2330** → `temple-26` **88.0818** = **3.849 km**, the
  smallest of the sixteen. Task 7b's finding that the idiom is here literally true of *any* two —
  because for points ordered along a line the smallest distance between any two is necessarily the
  smallest consecutive step — travels with the clause because the clause did not change.
- **"More places stand on this day than on any other in the section" is not an edit-introduced
  problem.** The drafter's own sentence carried the same unhedged comparative — "Sixty-one places
  stand on this day, more than on any other in the section" — and Task 7b cleared it; the edit
  dropped only the count. Re-counted here: **61 of 372**, against 56 / 47 / 37 / 26 / 22 / 21 /
  20 / 20 / 19 / 14 / 9 / 8 / 6 / 6. The general question this raises is recorded under
  `shikoku-88-awa` stage 3, where an edit *did* remove a hedge from the same shape of claim, and it

- **"forty-four of them are bus stops" keeps its antecedent.** With "Sixty-one places" gone,
  *them* attaches to "places … on this day", which is the set the 44 were counted out of.

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: 116.62895 − 88.08181 = **28.547 km**, and of the sixteen steps it is the **only**
value in [25, 30] — next below is 14.529, next above 38.365 ✓. Nahari **107.01902 / 99 m**,
18.937 km past the day's start anchor, and the earliest of the section's eight `type: town`
features (107.019 · 130.193 · 153.339 · 205.287 · 247.552 · 280.358 · 318.350 · 406.681) ✓.
56 filed, **40** `bus_stop` ✓. 神峯神社 projects at **116.62895**, the same point as `temple-27`,
and "27 Kōnomine-ji" at **116.61475**, **14.20 m** earlier — both inside 15 m ✓. Span
**28.52732**, `snap`; no drops.

- **"Tosa's sixteen temple-to-temple legs" is the right count.** Seventeen temples stand from
  Temple 23 to Temple 39 inclusive, so sixteen legs, and the first of them (T23 → T24, 77.547 km)
  runs from this section's own start anchor. The step list sums to **418.501 km**, the whole line,
  which is the check that no leg is missing or double-counted.
- **Describing the cut is not a §6 problem.** §6 governs what may be asserted about the *world*;
  "with nothing for the rule to choose between" asserts something about the dataset, and it is
  true. Awa's cleared stage 3 does the same three times ("the cut had nowhere nearer to stop",
  "because of where the record let it end", "the record holds"). The machinery is visible in this
  section more than in Awa's, but visibility is not ungroundedness, and the alternative — a day
  whose 28.5 km length is stated with no reason — would be less honest, not more.
- **The two-waypoints-for-one-place clause stands and is worth keeping.** It is a fact about the
  file, stated as a fact about the file ("two more waypoints"), and it is the exact case
  `metadata.json`'s `provenance` cites for why the cut's "temple" means a `templeNumber`. A walker
  whose app draws two marks on one gate is better served by the sentence than by its absence.

to six.


- **"two more waypoints sit within 15 m of Kōnomine-ji itself" → "two more entries sit within 15 m of
  Kōnomine-ji itself".** Task 7b defended this clause in as many words — "It is a fact about the file, stated
  as a fact about the file ('two more waypoints')" — and *waypoint*, the one word on the vocabulary list, is
  the word its defence rests on. Carrying it verbatim would have left the section's most technical noun in a
  walker's stage text; deleting the clause would have thrown away a fact that entry judged worth keeping
  ("A walker whose app draws two marks on one gate is better served by the sentence than by its absence").
  **So the noun was swapped and the argument reconciled here rather than silently detached**, on the
  precedent Awa's stage 3 set for "the record holds". *Entry* is a word for a line in a file exactly as
  *waypoint* was; it is the drafter's own word for this same object in the clause's second half; and the
  sentence still states a fact about the file as a fact about the file. It does **not** say two more *places*
  stand there, which would assert that a duplicate entry is a building. `node/408839221` 神峯神社 at
  **116.62895** and `node/408839421` "27 Kōnomine-ji" **14.20 m** earlier are the same two features, and both
  are still inside 15 m.
  - "a second entry carrying the temple's own name" → "another carrying the temple's own name", so the noun
    is not used twice in one clause. Same referent, one figure less.
- **"the first town the section records" → "the first mapped town in the section".** *First* asserts none
  before it, so this is an absence claim and the hedge is kept — in Awa stage 3's own construction, "the
  first mapped bed is 0.6 km on". Nahari at **107.019 km** is still the earliest of the section's eight
  `type: town` features.
- **"Fifty-six places stand on the day and forty of them are bus stops" → "Forty bus stops stand on it".**
  The inventory goes, as on stage 2; what survives is a positive existence claim over the same 40 features.
- **"the 25 to 30 km band the day cut looks in" → "the band the day cut looks in".** No claim moves. The
  sentence's content is that **exactly one** of the sixteen legs falls inside the band, and 28.547 is printed
  four words earlier. The band's edges are printed twice more in this section — stages 6 and 12 — where a
  margin against them is load-bearing; here nothing rests on them.
- **"Tosa's sixteen temple-to-temple legs" and "with nothing for the rule to choose between" are carried word
  for word**, so Task 7b's count check (seventeen temples, sixteen legs, summing to 418.501 km — the whole
  line) and its ruling that describing the cut is not a §6 problem still attach to the sentences they were
  written about.
- **The reflection stands.**

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared. This is one of the two places on the
branch where a Task 7 argument could have been detached, and it was not.**

- **"two more waypoints" → "two more entries" is the right resolution of a genuine collision.**
  Task 7b defended the clause in exactly the words "It is a fact about the file, stated as a fact
  about the file ('two more waypoints')", so the defence rests on the noun; and *waypoint* is on
  the vocabulary list this pass exists to remove. The three available moves were carry the banned
  word, delete a clause Task 7b judged worth keeping, or swap the noun and say so. **The third was
  taken and the argument is reconciled in the entry above rather than left hanging off a word the
  text no longer uses** — which is the only one of the three that keeps both the fact and its
  reasoning. *Entry* names a line in a file exactly as *waypoint* did, it is the drafter's own word
  for the same object in the clause's second half, and crucially it does **not** say two more
  *places* stand there, which would assert that a duplicate record is a building.
- **"at the end" is new and it is exact.** 神峯神社 (`node/408839221`) projects at **116.62895 km**,
  the same point as `temple-27` and the day's own end; "27 Kōnomine-ji" (`node/408839421`) is
  **14.20 m** earlier. Both are still inside 15 m.
- **"the first town the section records" → "the first mapped town in the section" keeps the hedge
  where it is needed.** *First* asserts none before it, so this is an absence claim, and it is
  hedged in the verb in Awa stage 3's own construction. Nahari at **107.019 km** is still the
  earliest of the section's eight `type: town` features.
- **Dropping "25 to 30 km" from the band costs nothing here and is consistent.** The sentence's
  claim is that exactly one of the sixteen legs falls inside the band, and **28.547** is printed
  four words earlier; the edges survive on stage 6, where the 457 m margin against them is the
  whole day. Some readability is lost — a walker meeting "the band" cold on this page has no
  definition for it — but no claim moved.

```
theme:      Thirty-eight kilometres between two temples
narrative:  Kōnomine-ji to Dainichi-ji is 38.4 km and this day takes 35.8 of it, so no temple stands on it at all. What the record holds changes character: eleven railway stations, twelve public toilets and ten convenience stores, against the bus stops that carried the three days before — two bus stops here, against forty on the day before. The city of Aki comes at 130.2 km and a viewpoint at 137.6. The day ends at a hotel at 152.4 km, 2.6 km short of the temple it has been walking toward.
reflection: Two temples thirty-eight kilometres apart, and neither of them on this day — what is a day worth that is only the space between?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands, with one vocabulary slip recorded and not struck.**

Re-measured: `temple-28` 154.99438 − `temple-27` 116.62895 = **38.365 km** ✓, span **35.78138**
✓, no `templeNumber` on the stage ✓. The eleven carried stations project at **120.755 · 123.135
· 127.804 · 131.825 · 134.458 · 138.731 · 140.246 · 142.024 · 146.483 · 148.356 · 149.768** ✓,
and the twelfth, Aki General Hospital, is at **131.257 / 311.9 m** — dropped, and not among the
eleven ✓. **12** toilets and **10** convenience stores, all carried ✓. **2** bus stops here
against **40** on stage 3, **44** on stage 2 and **24** on stage 1 ✓. Aki **130.19320 / 120 m**
✓, the viewpoint **137.56125 / 35 m** ✓, the hotel **152.38904 / 57 m** and 154.994 − 152.389 =
**2.605** ✓.

- **"the three days before" is exactly right.** Bus-stop counts run 5 / 24 / 44 / 40 / 2 across
  stages 0–4, so the three days that were carried by bus stops are 1, 2 and 3, not four days and
  not two. This is the species of claim the previous reader struck on Awa ("nearly twice the next
  day's count" meant the day *before* and the ratio was 5.6×), and here both the span and the two
  figures quoted against it are correct.
- **Recorded, not struck: "What the record holds … eleven railway stations."** This section keeps
  a real discipline — it says *reaches* where a drop matters (stage 7's "one thing reaches the
  day", stage 11's "Thirteen places reach it") and *record* where none does — and **stage 4 is the
  one place that breaks it**: the record holds twelve stations on this day and the package carries
  eleven. I have not struck it, for three reasons. Eleven is the number the walker's package
  actually draws, so nothing the reader can check is wrong. The qualifier is protective — deleting
  "What the record holds" would leave "eleven railway stations" reading as a claim about the road,
  which is worse. And no rank, ratio or superlative rests on it; the sentence's point is the
  change of kind, which twelve would not alter. **It is a one-word fix for a later editorial pass
  ("What reaches the day changes character"), not a grounding failure.**
- **Cross-stage reference: I decline to re-open it, and it is not this section's to settle.**
  Task 7a ruled on Awa's "yesterday" and let it stand because the day-for-stage substitution is
  already the pilgrimage's own vocabulary in the *files* — `stages.json`'s notes say "the previous
  day's end", `provenance` says "a day ends at a temple", `overview.estimatedDays` counts days.
  Every cross-stage figure in Tosa's eight such sentences is exact and section-local, and I
  re-measured each one. If the pilgrimage later settles against day-adjacency language, that is a
  pilgrimage-wide edit touching Awa and Sanuki too, not a Tosa review finding.

**Edited in Task 8b. The one-word fix Task 7b asked a later editorial pass to make, made — plus three
cumulative kilometres and three counts out.** Twelve figures down to seven.

```
theme:      Thirty-eight kilometres between two temples
narrative:  Kōnomine-ji to Dainichi-ji is 38.4 km and this day takes 35.8 of it, so no temple stands on it at all. What reaches the day changes character: railway stations, public toilets and convenience stores, against the bus stops that carried the three days before — two here, against forty on the day before. The city of Aki comes 13.6 km in, and a viewpoint after it. The day ends at a hotel, 2.6 km short of the temple it has been walking toward.
reflection: Two temples thirty-eight kilometres apart, and neither of them on this day — what is a day worth that is only the space between?
```

- **"What the record holds changes character" → "What reaches the day changes character".** This is Task 7b's
  own replacement, quoted from its entry above and adopted verbatim rather than invented here. It removes the
  dataset noun *and* closes the discipline slip that entry recorded: the record holds twelve stations on this
  day and the package carries eleven, because あき総合病院前 is **311.9 m** off and dropped. *Reaches* is the
  verb this section uses wherever a drop matters — stages 7 and 11, and now 8 and 13 — and under it the
  sentence is exactly true. **This was the section's one breach of its own discipline and it is closed.**
- **"eleven railway stations, twelve public toilets and ten convenience stores" → "railway stations, public
  toilets and convenience stores".** The sentence's point is the change of kind, which Task 7b noted twelve
  would not have altered either. Bare plurals claim less than counts do — at least two of each, against
  11 / 12 / 10 — and all three counts stay in the grounding bullets above.
- **"two bus stops here, against forty on the day before" → "two here, against forty on the day before"**,
  elliptical on the "bus stops" three words earlier. Both figures stay because they *are* the contrast the
  sentence exists for. **"the three days before" is carried word for word**, so Task 7b's check — bus-stop
  counts 5 / 24 / 44 / 40 / 2 across stages 0–4, so the three carried by bus stops are stages 1, 2 and 3, not
  four days and not two — still defends the phrase it was written about.
- **"The city of Aki comes at 130.2 km and a viewpoint at 137.6. The day ends at a hotel at 152.4 km" → "The
  city of Aki comes 13.6 km in, and a viewpoint after it. The day ends at a hotel".** Three cumulative
  kilometres, all measured from Temple 23 four days back. Aki is **130.193 − 116.629 = 13.564 km** past this
  day's start anchor, on the anchor convention Task 7b established as this section's throughout and which no
  printed digit here turns on. The viewpoint's position ranked nothing and is given as an ordering; the
  hotel's is the day's own end, and the day's distance is already in the first sentence.
- **"2.6 km short of the temple it has been walking toward" stays.** It is the whole shape of the day and the
  reflection turns on it.
- **The reflection stands.**

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared, and this is the one stage of the twenty
where the edit made the text *more* accurate rather than only more readable.**

- **"What the record holds changes character" → "What reaches the day changes character" closes a
  discipline slip Task 7b found and could not fix.** That entry recorded that the record holds
  twelve stations on this day and the package carries eleven, because あき総合病院前 is **311.9 m**
  off and dropped, and it proposed this exact wording as "a one-word fix for a later editorial
  pass". The pass adopted it verbatim rather than inventing one, and under *reaches* the sentence
  is exactly true where under *the record holds* it was one station wrong.
- **"eleven railway stations, twelve public toilets and ten convenience stores" → bare plurals
  claims less, not more.** A bare plural asserts at least two; the counts were 11 / 12 / 10 and are
  all still in the grounding bullets. The sentence's point — the change of kind from bus stops —
  is untouched, and both figures of the contrast that carries it ("two here, against forty on the
  day before") are kept.
- **"The city of Aki comes at 130.2 km" → "comes 13.6 km in" is a re-origin, not a re-measure, and
  I checked it.** 安芸市 projects at **130.1932 km**; the day's start anchor `temple-27` is at
  **116.6290**; the difference is **13.564 km** → 13.6. The viewpoint at **137.5613** is after it,
  so "a viewpoint after it" holds.
- **"the three days before" is word for word**, so Task 7b's check of the bus-stop run
  (5 / 24 / 44 / 40 / 2 across stages 0–4) still defends the phrase it was written about.

Re-measured: `temple-28` **154.99438 / 33 m**, `temple-29` **164.20465 / 34 m**, `temple-30`

- **Ruling on all four theme roundings, together as the drafter asked.** All four stand.
  22.829 → "twenty-three", 82.712 → "eighty-three", 33.557 → "thirty-four" and 35.738 →
  "thirty-six" are each **ordinary rounding to the nearest whole number** — none of them rounds up
  from below the halfway point, so none is a licence at all, and 33.557 → 34 is not "the loosest"
  but simply correct. Every one is a spelled-out word in a theme or reflection with the file's own
  decimal standing in the same stage's narrative, which is the pattern Task 7a cleared on Awa
  (stage 0's "twenty-eight" for 28.263, stage 2's "the last eight kilometres" for 8.142 — that one
  rounding *down* past 142 m). Nothing here is weaker than what already cleared.
- **The Konan sentence is the best-handled rounding in the section and should be left alone.**
  153.339 is 0.950 km past the day's start anchor and 0.929 km past its boundary vertex — the two
  conventions round to 1.0 and 0.9 respectively. Rather than pick one and print a digit that the
  other convention contradicts, the drafter gave the absolute position and the containing
  kilometre, and both readings agree that Konan is inside the day's first kilometre. This is the
  precise trap Awa's stage 2 fell into at 18.75 km, and Tosa stepped around it.

**Edited in Task 8b. The largest cut of the fifteen — thirteen figures down to six — with two cumulative
kilometres out and the Konan construction reduced to the half Task 7b actually defended.** No dataset word
stood in this narrative; what stood in it was arithmetic.

```

narrative:  After 38.4 km with none, four temples fall inside this one day. Dainichi-ji stands 2.6 km in, and Tosa Kokubun-ji, Zenraku-ji and Chikurin-ji follow, the last closing the day 22.8 km on from the first. The city of Konan stands inside the day's first kilometre, and five convenience stores stand on the day.
reflection: Four gates in twenty-three kilometres after thirty-eight with none — how quickly can attention change its pace?
```

- **"Tosa Kokubun-ji 9.2 km after it, Zenraku-ji 7.1 km after that, and Chikurin-ji 6.5 km further, closing

  Chikurin-ji follow, the last closing the day 22.8 km on from the first."** Awa's stage 0 shape: name the
  temples in order, place the first, bound the run. The three steps **9.211 / 7.079 / 6.539** sum to exactly
  the **22.829 km** the sentence keeps, so nothing is asserted that was not asserted before and nothing is
  lost a reader cannot recover; all three are in the grounding bullets above. 177.8 is a cumulative kilometre
  and goes with them.
- **"The city of Konan stands at 153.3 km, inside the day's first kilometre" → "The city of Konan stands
  inside the day's first kilometre."** Task 7b called this "the best-handled rounding in the section and
  should be left alone", and **the half of it that was doing the work is exactly the half that is kept.**
  That entry's argument was that Konan is 0.950 km past the day's start anchor and 0.929 km past its boundary
  vertex, that the two conventions round to 1.0 and to 0.9, and that rather than pick one and print a digit
  the other contradicts the drafter gave the absolute position *and* the containing kilometre. Dropping the
  absolute position prints **no digit either convention contradicts**, and both conventions agree Konan is
  inside the day's first kilometre. The trap Task 7b praised the drafter for stepping around is still stepped
  around; what leaves is the cumulative kilometre that was the price of stepping around it.
- **"Twenty-six places in all, four of them temples" → nothing.** The inventory, and its second half is
  already the narrative's own first clause: "four temples fall inside this one day".
- **"After 38.4 km with none", "four temples", "Dainichi-ji stands 2.6 km in" and "five convenience stores
  stand on the day" are unchanged.** 38.4 is the Temple 27 → 28 step this day answers, 2.6 places the first
  gate inside the day, and the convenience stores are a positive existence claim over five features.
- **The reflection stands**, and had to: it carries "twenty-three" and "thirty-eight", the two figures the day
  turns on, and Task 7b ruled on the first of them with the other three theme roundings under this entry.

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared. This is the second place where a Task 7b
argument was at risk, and the argument came out ahead.**

- **The Konan cut serves Task 7b's reasoning better than the sentence it praised did.** That entry
  praised the drafter for printing the absolute position **and** the containing kilometre rather
  than picking a convention and printing a digit the other contradicts — Konan is **0.950 km** past

```
theme:      Four hundred and fifty-seven metres out of the band
narrative:  Three temples come early — Zenjibu-ji 6.1 km in, Sekkei-ji 8.2 km after it, Tanema-ji 6.3 km after that — and then the day has to find somewhere to stop. Kiyotaki-ji, the next one, stands 30.5 km from where this day began: 457 m past the 30 km edge of the band the cut ends days in. So the day ends at Tosa instead, the city, at 205.3 km. Twenty places are recorded on it, four of them shrines and four of them hospitals.
reflection: Four hundred and fifty-seven metres decided where this day ends. How much of any day is decided that finely?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands, with one soft word recorded and not struck.**

Re-measured: `temple-32` **183.89728 / 52 m**, `temple-33` **192.08887 / 35 m**, `temple-34`
**198.43307 / 14 m**; steps **6.075 / 8.192 / 6.344** → 6.1 / 8.2 / 6.3 ✓. `temple-35`
**208.28041**, so 208.28041 − 177.82276 = **30.45765 km** — **457.65 m** outside [25, 30], and
the stage's committed `end.note` is the fact's own source ✓. Tosa **205.28657 / 107 m**,
`town`/`city` ✓. 20 filed; **4** `hospital` and **4** `church` whose names all end 神社 —
妙見宮星神社, 石土神社, 住吉神社, 嶋宮神社 ✓. Span **27.44651**, `snap`; no drops.

- **The theme being a number from the rule is not a §6 objection, and the trade was made
  correctly.** 457 m is a measurement on the committed line, re-derivable from two temple
  positions and the band's own edge, and it is the reason this day ends where it does. The
  alternative the drafter names — three temples and nothing else — would have left the day's shape
  unexplained, and §6 lists distance among what a narrative may stand on. It stands on distance.
- **"ends at Tosa" is the anchor's wording and the honest one.** The node is `town`/`city` 土佐市
  at 107 m off the line — the walked line passes through the city's mapped point's neighbourhood,
  and calling it the day's end is what `stages.json` does. A town node 107 m off is not a place
  the walker fails to reach.
- **Recorded, not struck: "Three temples come early."** The three stand 6.1, 14.3 and 20.6 km
  into a 27.4 km day, so the third is at 75% of it and "early" is soft for the group. I have not
  struck it: the word is relative and the sentence supplies its frame in the same breath ("and
  then the day has to find somewhere to stop" — the last 6.8 km carry no temple), all three exact
  distances follow it immediately so a reader can check it in the same sentence, and it cannot be
  removed by deletion without breaking the clause, which would make the fix a rewrite. Unlike
  Awa's struck "ordinary", it characterises position rather than a place, and the positions are
  right there.

**Edited in Task 8b. One dataset word and one cumulative kilometre out — and the smallest figure cut of the
fifteen, eleven down to nine.**

**This is one of two stages that would not come down to Awa's middle, and the reason is Task 7b's own defence
of a word it declined to strike.** Nine figures is Awa's own worst — its stages 0 and 1 both carry nine — so
this narrative reaches the register's edge rather than falling outside it. That entry recorded "Three temples come early" as soft — the three stand 6.1,
14.3 and 20.6 km into a 27.4 km day, so the third is at 75% of it — and let it stand because "all three exact
distances follow it immediately so a reader can check it in the same sentence". **Cutting 6.1, 8.2 and 6.3
would strip the defence off the word without striking the word**, which is the one thing an editing pass may
not do. The obvious substitute was drafted and rejected: "Zenjibu-ji 6.1 km in, then Sekkei-ji and Tanema-ji,
all three inside the day's first 21 km" carries four figures too, saves nothing, and makes "early" harder to
check rather than easier. **The clause is carried word for word.**

```
theme:      Four hundred and fifty-seven metres out of the band
narrative:  Three temples come early — Zenjibu-ji 6.1 km in, Sekkei-ji 8.2 km after it, Tanema-ji 6.3 km after that — and then the day has to find somewhere to stop. Kiyotaki-ji, the next one, stands 30.5 km from where this day began: 457 m past the 30 km edge of the band the cut ends days in. So the day ends at Tosa instead, the city. Four shrines stand on it, and four hospitals.
reflection: Four hundred and fifty-seven metres decided where this day ends. How much of any day is decided that finely?
```

- **"Twenty places are recorded on it, four of them shrines and four of them hospitals" → "Four shrines stand
  on it, and four hospitals."** The inventory goes and *recorded* goes with it. Both halves become positive
  existence claims over the same eight features — `node/5369299652`, `node/1085588477`, `node/5447091866`,
  `node/5447091847` and the four `subtype: hospital` nodes — so neither asserts a total, which is what
  *recorded* had been hedging. Task 7b's finding that "shrines" is read from the names ending 神社 and not
  from the `church` subtype is untouched, because the word and its four referents are.
- **"the day ends at Tosa instead, the city, at 205.3 km" → "the day ends at Tosa instead, the city."** A
  cumulative kilometre measured from Temple 23, seven days back. **"ends at Tosa" is carried word for word**,
  so Task 7b's ruling on it — that a `town`/`city` node 107 m off the walked line is not a place the walker
  fails to reach, and that calling it the day's end is what `stages.json` does — still defends the phrase it
  was written about.
- **The 457 m, the 30.5 km and the 30 km band edge all stay.** They are the day, the theme and the
  reflection: **208.280 − 177.823 = 30.457 km**, **457.65 m** outside [25, 30]. This is the one place in the
  section where the band's own edge is load-bearing, which is why stages 3 and 12 could drop it and this
  stage cannot.
- **The reflection stands.**

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared. Ruling on the nine figures: a reason, not
an excuse.**

Task 7b declined to strike "Three temples come early" — it is soft, since the third of the three
stands at 75% of the day — and the *only* ground it gave for letting it stand was that "all three
exact distances follow it immediately so a reader can check it in the same sentence". **Cutting
6.1, 8.2 and 6.3 would therefore have stripped the defence off a word that was left standing
because of it**, which is the exact failure this pass was sent to look for: an argument detached
from the sentence it defends. The choice was keep the figures or re-open the word, and re-opening
a word a reader deliberately let stand is not an editing pass's to do. **The clause is carried word
for word and the trade is correct.**

Two further checks confirm it is not cover for having done nothing. The pass did cut what it could
here — the twenty-place inventory, the dataset word *recorded* with it, and the cumulative 205.3 —
so the figures that remain are 6.1 / 8.2 / 6.3, then 30.5, 457 and 30 (the day's whole reason, and
the theme and reflection both turn on 457), then four shrines and four hospitals. **None of those
is droppable without either breaking a defence or removing the day's explanation.** And nine is
Awa's own worst, matched by two of its five stages, so this narrative sits at the edge of the
register rather than outside it.

- **"Twenty places are recorded on it, four of them shrines and four of them hospitals" → "Four
  shrines stand on it, and four hospitals" is weaker, not stronger.** Both halves become positive
  existence claims over the same eight named features and neither asserts a total, which is what
  *recorded* had been hedging. Task 7b's finding that "shrines" is read from the four names ending
  神社 rather than from the `church` subtype is untouched, because the word and its referents are.

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged, and it is the best-disciplined entry in the section.**

Re-measured: `temple-35` **208.28041 / 71 m**, so 2.993 km into the day → 3.0 ✓; `temple-36`
**222.80943 / 7 m**, 208.280 → 222.809 = **14.529** ✓; `temple-37` 280.878 − 222.809 = **58.068**

dropped ✓. Span **21.15518**, the shortest of the eight cut so far (32.071 / 30.808 / 25.223 /

is why "so far" is load-bearing and correct. 8 filed less one drop = **7** carried, against 22 /
37 / 61 / 56 / 46 / 26 / 20 ✓.

- **"reaches the day" is right and should not be replaced with "within 300 m of the route".**
  The distinction is real: 民泊汐風 is a lodging in the file that the shipped package does not
  carry, and this is the one day in Tosa where naming it would have changed a count from seven to
  eight and "one thing" to "two". Spelling the 300 m out would put a build parameter into a
  walker's stage text; "reaches the day" says the same thing in the walker's own terms and is
  exactly true. Stage 11 uses it again for the same reason. **This is the vocabulary stage 4
  should have used.**
- **The second church-subtype twin is correctly not cited.** `node/3919866480` "35 Kiyotaki-ji"
  projects onto **208.28041**, the same point as `temple-35`, 4.6 m off. Naming it would have made
  the day look as though it held three sacred places between its two temples rather than one.

**Edited in Task 8b. One figure out of the narrative and nothing else; two dataset words out of the
reflection.** Seven figures down to six — the smallest change in the fifteen, because Task 7b called this the
best-disciplined entry in the section and it was right.

```
theme:      Two gates, and almost nothing between them
narrative:  Kiyotaki-ji stands 3.0 km into the day and Shōryū-ji 14.5 km beyond it, and between them one thing reaches the day: Shōnen-ji. Seven places reach it in all, the fewest so far in Tosa. Past Shōryū-ji the next temple is 58.1 km away, and no temple falls where a day beginning here would end, so it stops at the Ohenro Guest House Lilian — 21.1 km, the shortest day the section has cut so far.
reflection: Twenty-one kilometres, two gates, and one mapped thing between them — what fills a day the map leaves blank?
```

- **"between them one thing reaches the day: Shōnen-ji, 10.3 km after the first gate" → "…: Shōnen-ji."** The
  position ranked nothing the sentence needed. What the sentence is for is that exactly one thing reaches a
  21 km day between two gates, and **10.307 km** stays in the grounding bullets above.
- **The rest of the narrative is carried word for word, including both uses of *reaches*.** Task 7b ruled on
  that verb at length — "'reaches the day' is right and should not be replaced with 'within 300 m of the
  route' … says the same thing in the walker's own terms and is exactly true" — and **this pass adopts it as
  the section's house verb rather than editing it**: stages 4, 8 and 13 now use it too, and stage 4's is that
  entry's own suggested wording. "the fewest so far in Tosa" and "the shortest day the section has cut so
  far" keep their "so far", which Task 7b showed is load-bearing because stages 12 and 14 come in shorter
  later.
- **Reflection: "one marked thing between them — what fills a day the record leaves blank?" → "one mapped
  thing between them — what fills a day the map leaves blank?"** Two swaps, neither a change of claim.
  *Marked* → *mapped* is the verb Awa's rewrite settled on. *The record* → *the map* is Awa stage 3's
  reflection edit, made for the same reason and with the same effect: the question still names the boundary
  of what the data can settle rather than crossing it, and it still asserts nothing about the road. The count
  of one is unchanged — 正念寺 is the only carried place between the two temples, because 民泊汐風 at
  **350.7 m** is dropped, which is the fact the whole reflection rests on.

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

- **The narrative lost one position and nothing else, and both uses of *reaches* survive.** That
  verb is the section's whole answer to the drop problem — 民泊汐風 at **350.7 m** is a lodging in

  unchanged words. **The pass then took it as the section's house verb rather than editing it**,
  which is why stages 4, 8 and 13 now use it too; adopting a reader's own preferred wording across
  a section is the opposite of detaching that reader's argument.
- **The reflection's two swaps change no claim.** *One marked thing* → *one mapped thing* is the
  same count of the same one feature; *what fills a day the record leaves blank* → *the map leaves
  blank* is Awa stage 3's own reflection edit. The question still names the boundary of what the

```
narrative:  This day walks 19.4 km before the record marks a single place — the longest such opening anywhere in Tosa. Six places on 26.6 km, the fewest of any day here so far, and no temple on it: the next stands 54.5 km beyond where this one began.

```

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: the day's first carried place after its start anchor is `node/5568013724` 7-Eleven
at **245.82678 / 273 m**, so **19.405 km** ✓. I checked the superlative against **every** stage
rather than against the drafter's summary: the openings run 0.274 / 2.532 / 0.009 / 1.466 /
0.014 / 0.421 / 0.348 / 0.172 / **19.405** / 6.424 / 0.521 / 0.265 / 0.424 / 0.007 / 2.768 km, so
the next-largest is stage 9's **6.424** and the claim holds by a factor of three ✓. Dawson
**247.13752 / 151 m**, Susaki **247.55187 / 44 m**, the station **252.72952 / 79 m**, the toilet
**252.85663 / 53 m**, the day's end **252.97834** ✓ — and those six are in the order the narrative
gives them ✓. 6 filed and 6 carried against stage 7's 7 ✓. `temple-37` 280.878 − 226.422 =
**54.456** → 54.5 ✓. Span **26.55389**, `snap`; no drops.

- **The reflection stands, on the precedent Task 7a set for Awa's stage 3.** That reader accepted
  "is it long because the way is, or because of where the record let it end?" because a question
  that names the boundary of what the data can settle is the opposite of an overclaim — an error
  would *assert*. Apply the same test here: an error would assert the road is quiet, and this
  sentence asserts nothing. Its premise is doubly qualified ("nothing **on the map** to mark
  them"), and the narrative above it has already stated the fact in the declarative and attributed
  it to the record twice ("before **the record** marks a single place"). It offers the walker two
  readings of a mapped absence and takes neither.
- **The unnamed station is the right call.** `node/8383580512` is named "Awa" (安和). In a
  fifteen-stage section of a pilgrimage whose first dōjō is Awa, printing it would read as an
  error in the data rather than a fact about the day, and the sentence loses nothing by saying "a
  station" — the day names Susaki and counts the rest. The refusal is recorded above where a
  future editor will find it, which is the correct place for it.

**Edited in Task 8b. Two dataset phrases out of the narrative, one out of the theme, two out of the
reflection; two cumulative kilometres out.** Eight figures down to five — the joint-lowest of the fifteen,
with stages 13 and 14.

```
theme:      Nineteen kilometres before anything is mapped
narrative:  This day walks 19.4 km before anything at all is mapped on it — the longest such opening anywhere in Tosa. What comes after comes quickly: two convenience stores, the city of Susaki, a station, a toilet, and the day's end. Six places reach it in 26.6 km, the fewest of any day here so far, and no temple on it: the next stands 54.5 km beyond where this one began.
reflection: Nineteen kilometres with nothing mapped along them — is that an absence, or a kind of quiet?
```

- **Theme: "Nineteen kilometres before anything is marked" → "Nineteen kilometres before anything is
  mapped".** One word, the same claim, in the verb the narrative below now carries. **This is one of only two
  themes this pass touched**, and it was touched because *marked* is dataset vocabulary and nothing else in
  it is.
- **"before the record marks a single place — the longest such opening anywhere in Tosa" → "before anything
  at all is mapped on it — the longest such opening anywhere in Tosa."** The absence is the whole stage and
  it stays hedged, in the verb. *Such* was already carrying the qualifier into the superlative and is
  untouched. **19.405 km** to `node/5568013724` is unchanged, and so is Task 7b's check of the superlative
  against every one of the fifteen openings — next-largest **6.424 km**, a factor of three.
- **"the city of Susaki at 247.6 km … and the day's end at 253.0 km" → "the city of Susaki … and the day's
  end."** Both cumulative, measured from Temple 23 nine days back. The six places still come in the order
  Task 7b verified them in, and the order is what the sentence is for.
- **"Six places on 26.6 km" → "Six places reach it in 26.6 km".** *Reach* is **added** rather than removed.
  The bare form asserted that six places are on the day; the section's own verb says only that six reach it.
  Six are filed and six are carried here, so the two readings coincide on this stage — but the weaker one is
  the one that generalises, and it is the verb stages 7, 11 and 13 use. **26.6 km is the day's own distance
  and stays.**
- **Reflection: "Nineteen kilometres and nothing on the map to mark them" → "Nineteen kilometres with nothing
  mapped along them".** Task 7b cleared this question on the precedent Task 7a set for Awa's stage 3, noting
  that its premise is doubly qualified. It still is, in one word instead of two: *mapped* carries what *on
  the map* and *to mark* carried between them, and **two hedges on one clause are not a stronger claim than
  one.** The question still offers the walker two readings of a mapped absence and takes neither.
- **The unnamed station is untouched**, so "What Tosa refused" above still describes this text exactly:
  `node/8383580512` is named "Awa" (安和) and the narrative still calls it "a station".

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

- **The stage's whole claim is an absence and it keeps its hedge in all three fields.** Theme
  "before anything is **mapped**", narrative "before anything at all **is mapped** on it",
  reflection "with nothing **mapped** along them". The 19.405 km opening to `node/5568013724` is
  unchanged, and so is Task 7b's check of the superlative against every one of the fifteen
  openings — next-largest **6.424 km**, a factor of three.
- **"Six places on 26.6 km" → "Six places reach it in 26.6 km" adds a hedge rather than removing
  one.** Six are filed and six are carried here, so the two readings coincide on this stage; the
  weaker one is the one that generalises, and it is the verb Task 7b ruled on twice.
- **Two hedges became one on the reflection and the reduction is legitimate.** *Nothing on the map
  to mark them* → *nothing mapped along them* is the same claim written once. Task 7b's clearance

  and it still does; the half that carried that finding — "is that an absence, or a kind of quiet?"
  — is untouched.
- **The two cumulative kilometres that went, 247.6 and 253.0, were both measured from Temple 23
  nine days back.** The six places still arrive in the order Task 7b verified, which is what the

**Reviewed in Task 7b. One clause struck; the rest stands.**

Re-measured: `temple-37` **280.87768 / 21 m**; 280.878 − `temple-36` 222.809 = **58.068 km**, and
the sixteen steps rank 82.712 · 77.547 · **58.068** · 54.911 · 38.365 — **third**, checked against
the whole list and not against a per-stage summary ✓. Span **27.89934** ✓. 21 filed, **6**
`train_station` and **5** `restaurant` ✓. The six late places project at **280.231 · 280.275 ·
280.358 · 280.393 · 280.637 · 280.794**, the earliest **0.647 km** before the temple, all inside
300 m ✓; no drops.

- **Struck: "and most of what the day holds arrives in its last six hundred metres."** The
  reflection read "Fifty-eight kilometres between two gates, **and most of what the day holds
  arrives in its last six hundred metres.** What does nearness do to distance?" It is **six of
  twenty-one** — 29%, or 33% counting the temple — and the narrative directly above it prints the
  true fraction in as many words: "the last 0.6 km takes **six of the day's twenty-one places**".
  This is the species the previous reader struck on Awa, where "nearly twice the next day's count"
  was 5.6× and named the wrong day: a determinate quantity word that the section's own count
  refutes. Unlike this section's soft words, **there is no reading of "most" on which 6 of 21 is
  most** — the only rescue would be an unstated reweighting that discards a station and a toilet
  as not being "what the day holds", and nothing in the files ranks a waypoint's worth. Deleting
  the clause leaves a grammatical reflection carrying its one measured fact (58.068 km), and
  "nearness" keeps its referent in the narrative the reader has just read.
- **The theme stands, and the distinction is the point.** "The last six hundred metres hold the
  day" makes no fraction claim. What sits behind it is real and checkable: 6 places in 647 m
  against 15 across the other 27.25 km — a **17× density** — plus the day's only town, both its
  lodgings and the temple that closes it. A figure of weight with that behind it is not the same
  object as a quantifier the count contradicts, and the rule I applied throughout this section is
  **strike what is false under every available reading, record what is false only under a loose
  one**.
- **"strung along a railway" stands.** Six `train_station` waypoints in order along the day, and
  the sentence attributes it to the record ("the record runs thin and strung along a railway").
  Stage 0 makes the same move from six stations and clears on the same ground.

**Edited in Task 8b. One dataset noun out, one cumulative kilometre out, two counts out — and the
six-of-twenty-one deliberately kept.** Eleven figures down to eight.

Then the last 0.6 km takes six of the day's twenty-one places at once — two Kubokawa stations, the town of Shimanto, two places to stay, a toilet — and the temple after them.
```
reflection: Fifty-eight kilometres between two gates. What does nearness do to distance?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: `temple-38` 363.58977 − `temple-37` 280.87768 = **82.712 km**, the largest of the
sixteen steps by 5.165 km over the next (77.547) ✓, and the 82.7 km `metadata.json`'s
`description` declares ✓. The three day-ends at **309.04985 · 342.60708 · 363.53099** all lie
inside the gap, and 363.58977 − 363.53099 = **58.78 m** ✓. Span **28.17161** ✓, and
28.172 / 82.712 = **0.3406** → "a third" ✓. 19 filed; **5** `train_station`, **3** `church` whose
names carry 神社 or "Shrine", **3** `restaurant` ✓. The fountain **303.16210 / 31 m**, the
section's **only** `type: water_source` of 372 features ✓. 363.590 − 309.050 = **54.540** ✓.
No drops.

- **The superlative is correctly limited to the section, and that is the load-bearing thing
  here.** "the widest gap between two temples **in this section**" — Temple 37 → 38 is also the
  widest leg on the whole circuit, and the section's own `description` says so, but this section
  does not claim it. That is the ruling the previous reader settled on Temple 66's 911 m applied
  correctly and unprompted: a rank across the 88 attaches to the pilgrimage, not to a day. I
  checked all four stages that cross this gap — 10, 11, 12 and the first 59 m of 13 — and **none
  of them makes the leg remarkable beyond its measured length**. None calls it remote, hard, a
  crossing or a trial.
- **The water sentence carries its qualifier and I accept the drafter's judgement.** It says
  "the only water source **recorded**", the subject of the whole clause is what the record holds,
  and the fact is a genuine curiosity — one `water_source` feature in 372. Against a walker
  reading it as a warning: this section also records **9 vending machines** and 27 convenience
  stores, and `logistics.waterNotes` says vending machines are ubiquitous, so the record itself
  does not support an inference of scarcity and the sentence does not draw one.
- **"a henro house" is read off the node's own name** ("Henro House Maaru", `subtype: guesthouse`)
  — the same move stage 11 makes with 民宿いさりび and stage 14 with Guest House Kurousagi.

**Edited in Task 8b. The worst narrative in the section by figure count — fourteen down to eight — with one
dataset word out, one inventory and three counts out, and two cumulative kilometres out.**

```
theme:      The first of three days in one gap
narrative:  Iwamoto-ji to Kongōfuku-ji is 82.7 km, the widest gap between two temples in this section, and the cut spends three whole days and the first 59 m of a fourth crossing it. This is the first of the three, 28.2 km. Railway stations, shrines and places to eat stand on it — and the only water source mapped anywhere in Tosa, a fountain. It ends at a henro house, with 54.5 km of the gap still ahead.
reflection: Eighty-three kilometres between one gate and the next, and this day covers a third of it — how do you hold a distance you cannot finish?
```

- **"Nineteen places stand on it — five railway stations, three shrines, three places to eat —" → "Railway
  stations, shrines and places to eat stand on it".** The inventory and three counts at once, which is what
  made this the densest of the fifteen. Bare plurals claim less than 5 / 3 / 3 do, and all four counts are in
  the grounding bullets above. Task 7b's note that "shrines" is read from the names — 天満宮, 八坂神社,
  熊野神社 — rather than from the `church` subtype survives with the word.
- **"the only water source recorded anywhere in Tosa" → "the only water source mapped anywhere in Tosa".**
  *Recorded* is on the vocabulary list, and it is also the hedge Task 7b accepted the sentence on: "It says
  'the only water source **recorded**', the subject of the whole clause is what the record holds". **The
  hedge is not removed; it is re-verbed**, into the word Awa's rewrite settled on and this section now uses
  throughout. An unmapped spring is a spring still, and the sentence says only that one water source is
  mapped — `node/10810968860`, 1 of 372 features. Task 7b's second point is untouched and still holds: the
  section also records nine vending machines and 27 convenience stores and `logistics.waterNotes` calls
  vending machines ubiquitous, so the record does not support an inference of scarcity and this wording draws
  none.
- **"at 303.2 km" → nothing, and "It ends at a henro house at 309.0 km" → "It ends at a henro house".** Two
  cumulative kilometres, measured from Temple 23 ten days back. The fountain's position ranked nothing; the
  day's end is the day's end, and **54.5 km of the gap still ahead** is the figure that places it — measured
  forward, from where the walker will be standing.
- **"the widest gap between two temples in this section" is carried word for word.** Task 7b called the
  section-limit "the load-bearing thing here": Temple 37 → 38 is also the widest leg on the whole circuit and
  the section's own `description` says so, and this text deliberately does not claim it. Rewording it was
  never available.
- **"the cut spends three whole days and the first 59 m of a fourth crossing it" and "a henro house" are
  carried word for word too**, the second because Task 7b showed it is read off the node's own name.
- **The reflection stands.**

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

- **"the only water source recorded anywhere in Tosa" → "the only water source mapped anywhere in
  Tosa" is the branch's cleanest re-verbing of a hedge.** This is a genuine absence claim — *only*
  asserts none besides — and Task 7b cleared it precisely because it said *recorded*. The hedge is
  not removed; it changes word class and stays inside the clause. An unmapped spring is a spring
  still, and `node/10810968860` is still 1 of 372. Task 7b's second point survives untouched: the
  section also records nine vending machines and 27 convenience stores and `logistics.waterNotes`
  calls vending machines ubiquitous, so neither the record nor this wording supports an inference
  of scarcity.
- **"the widest gap between two temples in this section" is word for word**, which Task 7b called
  the load-bearing thing on this stage: Temple 37 → 38 is also the widest leg on the whole circuit,
  and the text deliberately does not claim it. **A rewrite that dropped "in this section" would
  have been the single largest strengthening available anywhere in these twenty**, and none was

- **The inventory and three counts that went ranked nothing**, and the day's shape survives in the
  figures that stayed: 82.7, 28.2, the 59 m, and 54.5 km measured *forward* from where the walker

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: spans **28.17161 / 33.55676 / 20.91824** for stages 10, 11 and 12, so the middle day
is the longest of the three ✓. 14 filed less the **331.3 m** drop = **13** carried ✓. The four

empty ✓. 342.607 − `temple-37` 280.878 = **61.729** ✓ and `temple-38` 363.590 − 342.607 =

- **The two blank spans are exact and that is what carries the sentence.** Both bracket
  measurements are true against **all 372 features**, not just the stage's own — I checked with
  the off-line limit removed, so a misfiled or dropped waypoint could not have hidden inside
  either. The four places between them are listed by kind rather than in line order (325.657
  store, 329.205 toilet, 335.062 café, 335.126 toilet), which the entry declares; the sentence
  claims only that they fall between the two blanks, and they do.
- **The theme's rounding is ordinary rounding, not a licence.** 33.557 → 34 is the nearest whole
  number. Ruled with the other three under stage 5.
- **The skipped bus stop breaks nothing.** `node/9470907587` (unnamed, **318.32648 / 33 m**) sits
  *before* Kuroshio and therefore outside both blank spans; omitting it from a run-through that
  never claims completeness is selection.

  record holds fourteen and the package carries thirteen.

**Edited in Task 8b. Four dataset phrases out and two cumulative kilometres out — but only two figures, and
this is the one narrative in the fifteen still above anything Awa carries.** Twelve figures down to ten,
against Awa's worst of nine.

**Why it stays high.** This day *is* a sequence of measured blanks: four stations in the first 7.4 km, then
**7.307 km** with nothing in it, then four places, then **7.480 km** with nothing in it. Task 7b verified both
brackets against all 372 features with the off-line limit removed, so a misfiled or dropped waypoint could not
have hidden inside either, and **the two spans are the day's whole shape**. A version without them describes a
day with no shape at all, and the figures that would have gone are the ones a walker most needs.

```
theme:      Thirteen places over thirty-four kilometres
narrative:  The middle day of the 82.7 km gap, and the longest of the three at 33.6 km. Thirteen places reach it. Four railway stations come in the first 7.4 km, then a viewpoint and the town of Kuroshio — and after that the map almost gives out: 7.3 km with nothing on it, a convenience store, a café and two toilets, then 7.5 km more with nothing, and the day ends at a minshuku. No temple has stood on the way for 61.7 km.
reflection: Sixty-two kilometres since the last gate and twenty-one still to the next — what keeps a day like this from being only transit?
```

- **"the record almost stops" → "the map almost gives out".** This clause is the attribution both blank claims
  hang from — it governs them, as *the record* did — and only its noun changed, to the one Awa's stage 3
  reflection settled on. Nothing about the road is asserted in either version.
- **"7.3 km with nothing marked … then 7.5 km more with nothing marked" → "7.3 km with nothing on it … then
  7.5 km more with nothing".** *Marked* appeared twice underneath a governing hedge that already says whose
  absence this is. That is one claim written three times, which Awa's stage 4 reduced on the same reasoning.
  Both spans are unchanged: Kuroshio **318.350** → スリーエフ **325.657**, and the last toilet **335.126** →
  the day's end boundary **342.606**.
- **"the town of Kuroshio at 318.4 km" → "the town of Kuroshio", and "the day ends at a minshuku at 342.6 km"
  → "the day ends at a minshuku".** Both cumulative, measured from Temple 23 eleven days back. Both places
  keep their position in the ordering, which is what the sentence uses them for, and "a minshuku" keeps the
  name's own word.
- **"No temple has stood on the line for 61.7 km" → "No temple has stood on the way for 61.7 km".** *The line*
  is this repository's name for `route.main.geojson` and no walker has a definition for it; *the way* is what
  Awa's rewrite put in its place, and it is the vaguer of the two and therefore not the stronger.
  **342.607 − 280.878 = 61.729 km** is unchanged. The absence needs no mapping hedge because temples are an
  exhaustively known set here — sixteen `templeNumber` features from the official-site source — which is the
  same reason stages 2, 4 and 8 can say "no temple" flatly.
- **"Thirteen places reach it" is carried word for word, and cutting it was considered and rejected.** It
  duplicates the theme's "Thirteen places", so cutting it would have saved a figure — but the theme states
  the count with **no verb**, and *reach* is the whole of its hedge. Removing the sentence would have left the
  number in the theme unqualified, which is a stronger claim than the drafter made. Task 7b's ruling on the
  verb ("the record holds fourteen and the package carries thirteen") still attaches to the sentence it was
  written about.
- **The reflection stands.**

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands and the flag is cleared — but this narrative carries the one
hedge on this branch that now reads as a claim about the ground, and a later pass should fix it.**

**The concern.** "and after that the map almost gives out: 7.3 km with nothing on it, a convenience
store, a café and two toilets, then **7.5 km more with nothing**". Both blanks are the day's
load-bearing absences, and both are the only absences in these twenty whose hedge sits *outside*
the clause it protects. Everywhere else on this branch the mapping is in the verb of the clause
itself — "nothing is mapped for 9.4 km" (stage 1), "before anything at all is mapped on it"
(stage 8), "past it nothing is mapped for 35.7 km" (stage 13), "Nothing is mapped along the first
11.2 km" (Awa stage 4). Here it is four words upstream in "the map almost gives out", and what
stands in the clause is *nothing on it*, whose natural antecedent is the 7.3 km of ground rather
than the map. The second blank is a further eleven words from the attribution and carries no
qualifier at all.

**Why it stands.** The governing clause is explicit, names *the map*, sits in the same sentence,
and is joined by a colon whose only function is to elaborate it — and this branch has already
accepted exactly that construction, on the stage where it was argued hardest: Task 8a kept "so the
**day** had nowhere nearer to stop" after "nothing on the way is mapped as a bed or a town",
rejecting the unchained "so there was nowhere nearer to stop". A world-scoped clause chained by an
explicit connective to a map-scoped one is the precedent, and this fits it. **Restoring *marked*
would undo a section-wide vocabulary decision on a case the section's own precedent covers, and
rewriting it in my own words would leave this stage drafted for a wording that is not false.** So
it is recorded rather than acted on: **put the hedge back in the verb — "7.3 km with nothing mapped
on it … then 7.5 km more with nothing mapped" — the next time this text is opened.** Both spans are
unchanged and Task 7b's check of them against all 372 features with the off-line limit removed
still holds.

- **Ruling on the ten figures: a reason, not an excuse.** Task 7b verified both brackets with the
  off-line limit removed precisely because they are what the day is; a version without them
  describes a day with no shape, and the figures that would have gone — 7.4, 7.3, 7.5 — are the
  three a walker most needs. The pass cut what it could (two cumulative kilometres, four dataset
  phrases) and landed one figure above Awa's worst, which is the smallest overshoot available.
- **Keeping "Thirteen places reach it" despite its duplicating the theme is right, and the reason
  is one this pass should be credited with finding:** the theme states the count with no verb, so
  cutting the sentence would have left the number unqualified — a stronger claim than the drafter
  made, in the field a walker reads first.
- **"No temple has stood on the line for 61.7 km" → "on the way" is safe** for the reason it is not
  safe elsewhere: temples here are an exhaustively known set of sixteen `templeNumber` features from
  the official-site source, not an OpenStreetMap sample, so no mapping hedge is owed.
  **342.607 − 280.878 = 61.729 km** is unchanged.

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: the five lodgings project at **342.60708 · 343.03130 · 344.95259 · 345.68078 ·
345.96975**, a span of **3.363 km** ✓; 363.53099 − 345.96975 = **17.561** → 17.6 ✓;

363,589.77 − 363,530.99 = **58.78 m** ✓. The five late places project at **363.35494 · 363.42233
· 363.42772 · 363.51713 · 363.52428**, the earliest **176 m** before the day's end ✓. Span
**20.91824**, `snap`; **6** drops, all of them the Ashizuri cluster.

- **I verified the superlative by brute force rather than trusting it.** Over the section's **28
  carried `accommodation` waypoints**, the tightest five-bed window anywhere is this one at
  **3.363 km**; the next tightest five is **20.500 km**, and **no 3.4 km window outside
  342–347 km holds more than two**. The claim is exactly what the entry says it is — a computed
  superlative over carried lodging waypoints — and it survives the check.
- **None of the six drops is named, and the one that could have slipped through did not.** The
  six project together at **351.54850 km** at 1,751–2,080 m off the walked line, and all six
  measure **12–137 m** from `route.geojson` — I reproduced every figure in the table above. A
  careless draft would have picked up the Lawson or the hospital as places "on" the day, exactly as
  Awa's stage 4 draft picked up a Lawson before it was cut. This one names nothing.
- **"Cape Ashizuri" is inside the line the section drew for itself.** It appears once, as the
  `name` of `node/9444928826`, a `bus_stop` at **363.52428 / 69 m**, introduced as "a bus stop
  named Cape Ashizuri" — the same construction Awa used for Yuki, Tainohama and Kiki. The refused
  `description` on `temple-38` ("Cape Ashizuri — southernmost point of Shikoku") appears nowhere,
  no cape is named as a place, and no superlative attaches. **The refusal of temple `description`
  fields held.**
- **Narrating the rule is right here.** A 20.9 km day that stops 59 m short of the gate it was
  walking to is not explicable by anything except the rule, and §6 lists distance among what a
  narrative may stand on. The alternative is a day whose shape looks arbitrary.
- Noted: the package a walker reads carries no sign of the town 2 km off the line. That is a
  property of the walked line and the 300 m build limit, not of this text, and it is not the
  prose's to fix.

**Edited in Task 8b. One dataset noun out and one band figure out; all five of the day's load-bearing
measurements stay.** Ten figures down to eight.

```
theme:      Fifty-nine metres short of a gate
narrative:  Five places to stay stand in the first 3.4 km of this day, the tightest run of beds anywhere in Tosa; then it goes 17.6 km to its end. Kongōfuku-ji is 21.0 km from where the day began — too near for the band the cut ends days in — so no temple qualified, and the day stops 59 m short of the gate, at a lodging that carries the temple's own name. In the last 200 m before it the day gathers two viewpoints, two toilets and a bus stop named Cape Ashizuri.
reflection: Fifty-nine metres from the gate, and the day ends anyway, because the rule measured from somewhere else. Is arriving a place, or a decision?
```

  sentence shape ("the day gathers what the rest of it withheld: …"). **This is a positive list, not an
  absence claim, so it needs no hedge**: five named features do stand in the last 200 m, and the sentence
  never said they were all that stood there. **"a bus stop named Cape Ashizuri" is carried word for word**,

  anywhere in Tosa", which Task 7b verified by brute force over all 28 carried `accommodation` waypoints

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**
