**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: `temple-38` at **363,589.77 m** against the day's start anchor at **363,530.99 m** =
**58.78 m** ✓. 399.32840 − 363.58977 = **35.739 km**, and I checked it **with the off-line limit
removed**: of all 372 features in `waypoints.geojson`, **not one projects into that span** —
carried, dropped or misfiled — so the claim is true of the whole file and not merely of the
package ✓. It is the largest gap between consecutive projected features anywhere in the section,
against a next-largest of **19.405 km** (stage 8) ✓. 6 filed; the three drops are `accommodation`
at **870.3 / 1,119.8 / 1,485.6 m** → "0.9 to 1.5 km" ✓, and they measure **39 / 199 / 102 m** from
`route.geojson`, reproducing the table above ✓. The three carried features are exactly
`node/9444928782`, `temple-38` and `node/11535055771` ✓. Span **35.81559**, the longest in the
section by **34 m** over stage 4's 35.78138 ✓; `temple-39` 418.50087 − `temple-38` 363.58977 =
**54.911** ✓.

- **The flagged question — whether this text describes an absence of data as though it were an
  absence of things — I checked in all three fields, and the answer is no.** The narrative says
  "**the record** holds nothing for 35.7 km — the longest stretch in Tosa with **nothing marked**
  on it"; the theme says "**unmarked**"; the reflection says "**nothing on the map** to mark them
  — what do you notice when nothing is **pointed out to you**?". Every one of the three carries
  the qualifier, and the reflection carries two. **Nowhere does any of them say the road is
  empty.** That is a stricter discipline than Awa's stage 4, which Task 7a cleared with two
  qualifiers on one sentence, and the drafter also did the honest thing the data made available:
  the sentence about the three unreachable lodgings tells the walker in as many words that half
  the day's filed places never reach it.
- **A 36 km stage with three places is a thin day, and it is not a reason to hold the text.** The
  thinness is a property of OpenStreetMap coverage on the Ashizuri return, and the text neither
  hides it nor dresses it: it states the count, states the reason three of six are missing, and
  attributes the blank to the record three times. Better coverage would be a better dataset; it
  would not make this prose more true than it is. **No sentence here would need rewriting if
  waypoints were added** — only the counts would move.
- **The theme's rounding is ordinary rounding.** Ruled with the other three under stage 5.
- Recorded, not struck: **"all of it past the gate"**. 58.78 m of this day precedes Kongōfuku-ji,
  which the narrative's own first clause states, so on a loose reading of "it" as *this day* the
  final clause is wrong by 59 m — the one margin this section has made load-bearing. It survives
  because the grammatical antecedent is "the 35.8 **of them**", the portion of the 54.9 km leg the
  day takes, and that portion does lie wholly past the gate; and because the only reader equipped
  to spot the loose reading is the one who has just been told the 59 m and will therefore parse it
  correctly. Not false under every reading, so recorded rather than struck — but it is the one
  sentence in the fifteen a final pass should look at again.

**Edited in Task 8b. Six dataset phrases out of the narrative, one out of the theme, two out of the
reflection — and the one loose reading Task 7b asked a final pass to look at again, closed.** Eight figures
down to five.

```
theme:      One gate, then thirty-six kilometres with nothing mapped
narrative:  Kongōfuku-ji stands 59 m into this day, and past it nothing is mapped for 35.7 km — the longest such stretch in Tosa. Three places reach this day: the temple, the lodging it starts from, and the place it ends. Kongōfuku-ji to Enkō-ji is 54.9 km, and this day takes 35.8 of them, almost all of it past the gate.
reflection: Thirty-six kilometres with nothing mapped along them — what do you notice when nothing is pointed out to you?
```

- **"past it the record holds nothing for 35.7 km — the longest stretch in Tosa with nothing marked on it" →
  "past it nothing is mapped for 35.7 km — the longest such stretch in Tosa."** Task 7b counted the
  qualifiers here across all three fields and found the discipline stricter than Awa's stage 4 — which is
  exactly the case Awa's rewrite then ruled on: **three hedges are not a stronger claim than one; they are
  one claim written three times.** One is kept, in the verb, with *such* carrying it into the superlative, in
  Awa's own words. **399.328 − 363.590 = 35.739 km** is unchanged, and so is Task 7b's check of it with the
  off-line limit removed: of all 372 features, not one projects into that span, so the claim is true of the
  whole file and not merely of the package.
- **"Six places are filed on the day and three of them are lodgings 0.9 to 1.5 km off the walked line, which
  this day never reaches; what is left is the temple, the lodging the day starts from, and the place it ends"
  → "Three places reach this day: the temple, the lodging it starts from, and the place it ends."** *Filed
  on*, *the walked line* and *never reaches* are three of the vocabulary list in one sentence, and the
  off-line clause is the species this pass exists to remove — a walker does not need to be told that a hotel
  he will never see is 1.5 km away. What it bought is bought more cheaply by the section's own verb:
  **"Three places reach this day" says the same thing about the same three carried features** —
  `node/9444928782`, `temple-38` and `node/11535055771`, exactly as Task 7b enumerated them — and, being the
  verb that entry ruled on twice, it says only that three reach the day and not that three exist.
  - Task 7b praised the cut clause as "the honest thing the data made available", telling the walker in as
    many words that half the day's filed places never reach it. **That honesty was about a count this text no
    longer states.** With the six gone there is no half to disclose, and what remains is disclosed in the
    verb. The three drops at **870 / 1,120 / 1,486 m** are still tabled above under "The six that passed
    enrichment and failed the build", which is where a reader who wants them should go, and **none of them is
    named here, as none was before.**
- **"all of it past the gate" → "almost all of it past the gate".** Task 7b recorded this as "the one
  sentence in the fifteen a final pass should look at again": on a loose reading of "it" as *this day*, the
  clause is wrong by **58.78 m** — the one margin this section has made load-bearing. **This is that pass.**
  *Almost all* is true under both readings. Under the grammatical reading Task 7b defended, where the
  antecedent is "the 35.8 **of them**", the portion of the 54.9 km leg this day takes, *almost all* is simply
  weaker than *all* and the defence is undisturbed; under the loose reading it stops being wrong. **Nothing
  is asserted that was not asserted before; one reading stops being false.**
- **Theme: "One gate, then thirty-six kilometres unmarked" → "One gate, then thirty-six kilometres with
  nothing mapped".** *Unmarked* is dataset vocabulary. **"unmapped" was drafted as its replacement and
  rejected**: it reads as a claim that the thirty-six kilometres of road are not in OpenStreetMap, which is
  false — the walked line is mapped, and it is the line the 35.739 km was measured on. "with nothing mapped"
  says what the narrative says, at the length a theme allows.
- **Reflection: "Thirty-six kilometres with nothing on the map to mark them" → "Thirty-six kilometres with
  nothing mapped along them".** As on stage 8 and for the same reason. The rest of the question — "what do
  you notice when nothing is pointed out to you?" — is untouched, and it is the half Task 7b's clearance
  rested on.
- **Task 7b's central finding survives the edit.** It checked all three fields for whether they describe an
  absence of data as an absence of things and found that every one carries the qualifier, the reflection
  twice. Every one still carries it: *nothing is mapped* in the narrative, *with nothing mapped* in the
  theme, *nothing mapped along them* in the reflection. **Nowhere does any of them say the road is empty.**
- Its second finding also survives: **no sentence here would need rewriting if waypoints were added.** With
  the six-count gone, not even a count would move — only the word *three*.

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared. Ruling on "almost all of it": the one
claim that moved on this branch moved toward truth, and by the smallest step that reaches it.**

`temple-38` projects at **363,589.77 m** and this day's start anchor at **363,530.99 m**, so
**58.78 m** of the day precedes the gate, out of a span of **35,815.59 m** — 99.84% of the day lies
past it. Task 7b recorded "all of it past the gate" as the one sentence in the fifteen a final pass
should revisit: false by 58.78 m under the loose reading of *it* as *this day*, defensible under
the grammatical reading where the antecedent is "the 35.8 **of them**", the portion of the 54.9 km
leg. **Under the grammatical reading *almost all* is merely weaker than *all*, so Task 7b's defence
is undisturbed; under the loose reading it stops being false.** The claim is therefore weaker on
one reading and true on both, which is the direction a correction is supposed to travel. **No third
reading is created**: nothing is asserted about the 58.78 m that was not already asserted by the
narrative's own first clause, "Kongōfuku-ji stands 59 m into this day". The margin the section made
load-bearing on stage 12 — the same 58.78 m, read from the other side — is untouched, and the two
stages still say the same thing about it.

- **The absence keeps its hedge in all three fields, which is what Task 7b's central finding
  rested on.** *nothing is mapped* in the narrative, *with nothing mapped* in the theme, *nothing
  mapped along them* in the reflection. Nowhere does any of them say the road is empty. **399.328 −
  363.590 = 35.739 km** is unchanged, as is that entry's check with the off-line limit removed:
  of all 372 features, not one projects into the span.
- **The theme's replacement was chosen against a wrong alternative and the reasoning is right.**
  *unmapped* was drafted and rejected because it would claim the thirty-six kilometres of road are
  not in OpenStreetMap, which is false — the walked line is mapped, and it is the line the 35.739 km

**Reviewed in Task 7b. Stands unchanged.**

Guest House Kurousagi **402.09610 / 202 m** ✓, Mihara **406.68129 / 12 m**, `town`/**`village`**
and the only `village` among the section's eight `type: town` features (3 `town`, 4 `city`, 1

ninth ✓; no drops. `temple-39` 418.50087 − `temple-38` 363.58977 = **54.911**, of which this day
is the final **19.161** ✓.

- **The forward look rests on two committed fields, not on a reading.** `shikoku-88-tosa`'s
  `overview.endPoint` and `shikoku-88-iyo`'s `overview.startPoint` are the same literal
  `[132.774, 32.961]`, checked in both files, and `osm.note` records that this section ends at
  Temple 39 "where shikoku-88-iyo begins". Awa's stage 4 cleared on the identical check.
- **"sixteen gates" is the right count and the one a walker would make.** The section's 418.5 km
  *begins* at Temple 23, so Temple 23 is where the walking starts rather than somewhere the
  fifteen days arrive; the sixteen the reflection counts are Temples 24 to 39, which is exactly
  the set of temples these fifteen days reach. The alternative count of seventeen would include a
  gate the section does not walk to.
- **The reflection summing the section from inside one package** is what Awa's stage 4 does
  ("Yakuō-ji closes Awa at 154.5 km, and Tosa begins from the same point"), and Task 7a cleared
  it. Every figure in it — `stageCount` 15, `walkedLine.lengthKm` 418.501, sixteen `templeNumber`
  features — reproduces.
- Noted, not struck: **"Nine places stand on it:"** is followed by a list of eight, the ninth
  being Enkō-ji, named in the sentence before. The count is right and the temple is named; a
  reader counting the colon's list comes up one short for a sentence.

**Edited in Task 8b. Three cumulative kilometres out and one inventory out — and the count wrinkle Task 7b
noted resolved by the same cut, without moving a claim.** Ten figures down to five.

```
theme:      The shortest day, and the last gate
narrative:  19.2 km, the shortest day in Tosa, and it ends at Enkō-ji — the section's own boundary, where Iyo begins from the same point. What stands on the way to it: a guest house, then Mihara, the only village among the section's eight towns, Yamabiko Cafe just past it, two toilets, a station, a restaurant, and a hospital inside the last kilometre. Kongōfuku-ji to Enkō-ji is 54.9 km, and this day closes it.
reflection: Fifteen days, 418.5 km, sixteen gates — and the last of them is the next section's first. What do you set down here?
```

- **"Nine places stand on it: a guest house at 402.1 km, then Mihara at 406.7, … a station at 415.9 …" →
  "What stands on the way to it: a guest house, then Mihara, … a station …"** Three cumulative kilometres,
  measured from Temple 23 fifteen days back, on the page of the last day. The eight places keep the order
  Task 7b verified, and every one keeps the name or the kind the drafter chose for it: "a guest house" from
  the node's own name rather than its `hotel` subtype, "the only village among the section's eight towns",
  "Yamabiko Cafe" named rather than called a café because its subtype is `restaurant`, and "a hospital inside
  the last kilometre".
  - **The same cut closes the wrinkle Task 7b recorded and did not strike**: "Nine places stand on it:" was
    followed by a list of eight, the ninth being Enkō-ji, named in the sentence before, so a reader counting
    the colon's list came up one short. The count is gone, so there is nothing to count short. **No claim
    moved to close it** — a list introduced by "What stands on the way to it" asserts no total at all, where
    "Nine places" asserted one. Enkō-ji is still named, in the sentence before, as the day's end.
- **"and this day is its last 19.2" → "and this day closes it".** 19.2 is the day's own distance and is
  printed as the narrative's first two words; this was the second of two. The day does close the
  Temple 38 → 39 leg — it ends at Enkō-ji, the leg's far end — so nothing is lost but the repeat.
- **"Kongōfuku-ji to Enkō-ji is 54.9 km" stays**, as it does on stage 13, so the two days that share the leg
  still say the same thing about it.
- **The reflection stands, including its 418.5 km.** Task 7b compared it to Awa's stage 4 and cleared it on
  that precedent — and the figure Awa's rewrite then removed from *that* stage was a **position** ("Yakuō-ji
  closes Awa **at** 154.5 km"), not a total. Here 418.5 is a total, on the last page of the section, standing
  beside "Fifteen days" and "sixteen gates", and a walker who has just walked it has the frame for it.
  **"sixteen gates" is untouched**, so Task 7b's count ruling — Temples 24 to 39, exactly the set these
  fifteen days reach — still attaches to it.

**Read in Task 9a. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

- **"Nine places stand on it:" → "What stands on the way to it:" asserts less, not more, and it
  resolves a wrinkle rather than hiding one.** The old opening asserted a total of nine and then
  listed eight, the ninth being Enkō-ji, named in the sentence before — the mismatch Task 7b
  recorded and did not strike. The new opening asserts no total at all, and by saying *on the way
  to it* it correctly excludes the temple from the list rather than counting it into one. All eight
  keep the order Task 7b verified and each keeps the name or kind the drafter chose: "a guest
  house" from the node's own name and not its `hotel` subtype, "the only village among the
  section's eight towns", "Yamabiko Cafe" named rather than called a café because its subtype is
  `restaurant`.
  - Recorded, not held against it: the new frame carries an exhaustiveness implicature — *what
    stands there is this* — that the old count also carried, and it is the one "What …" opening in
    the fifteen with no mapping hedge in it, where stages 0, 4 and 9 have one. It is a list of
    presences, not an absence, so nothing about the ground is asserted by it either way; but if a
    later pass hedges stage 11's blanks it should look at this frame in the same breath.
- **The reflection's 418.5 km is a total on the last page of the section, not a position**, which
  is the distinction Awa's stage 4 rewrite turned on when it cut 154.5 from "closes Awa **at**
  154.5 km". A walker who has just walked it has the frame for it. "sixteen gates" and "Fifteen
  days" are untouched beside it.
- **"and this day is its last 19.2" → "and this day closes it" loses a repeat and no claim**:
  19.2 is the narrative's own first two words, and the day does end at Enkō-ji, the leg's far end.

## shikoku-88-iyo — Iyo (Temples 39-65)

> **Reviewed in Task 7c**, by the third reader, who had no part in drafting any of the forty and
> no part in reviewing the other three sections. **All fourteen stages cleared; nothing struck.**
> Every figure in this section was re-measured independently against the committed
> `route.main.geojson` with `walkedLine`, `cumulativeMeters`, `projectOnLine` and
> `stageBoundaries` from `scripts/ways/geo.ts` **before any of the prose was read**, and every
> stage's drop list was checked against `ways/report.json`. The line reproduces at 13,983 points
> and 365.677 km; all fifteen boundary vertices and their `snap` modes, all fourteen spans, all
> 26 temple positions and the whole 26-step list, all 953 waypoint projections, the per-stage
> counts 52/35/134/121/71/26/16/84/117/75/88/32/84/18, the whole type histogram, the
> `momentsBeyondEnds` row 4/0/19/50/16/2/3/15/5/6/13/7/6/0 and every one of the thirteen day-end
> filing rows — Δ signs, the Kanjizai-ji floating-point residual and BEKKU's −66.65 m included —
> reproduce exactly as written. Every id cited resolves to a feature carrying the name given.
> **No narrative names a dropped waypoint**, checked against all four drops on stages 4, 7 and 8.
> The drafter's report of no off-line outlier is confirmed rather than assumed: measured against
> `route.main.geojson`, the farthest waypoint in the section is `node/1423738628` at **322.5 m**,
> so nothing here behaves like Tosa's Ashizuri cluster despite the corridor split (`route.geojson`
> **636.621 km** across 14,893 points, **1.741×** the walked line).
>
> **Two errors were found in this section's evidence, both in the checklist and neither in the
> prose**, and both are recorded under their own stages rather than rewritten: stage 8's
> **Places** line calls 117 "the second-most of the fourteen" when it is the **third**, and stage
> 12's grounding bullet sources "32.8 km" to the spans when 32.746 rounds to 32.7 and 32.8 is the
> declared `distanceKm`. Neither claim reaches a narrative, so no sentence changes. Six further
> readings are recorded, not struck; each is named in its own entry.
>
> **Distance convention.** Iyo measures a distance *into* a day from **the day's declared
> boundary vertex**, not from its start anchor's own projection — Sanuki's convention, not Awa's
> or Tosa's. It is consistent across all fourteen and every grounding bullet says so in as many
> words. **Unlike Tosa, three printed digits do change under the other convention**, so the
> pilgrimage-wide question Awa's stage 2 raised is not free here: stage 6's "6.4 km in" is 6.446
> from the vertex and 6.456 from Karibaen (→ 6.5); stage 11's "10.8 km into this day" is 10.784
> from the vertex and 10.851 from ゲストハウス BEKKU (→ 10.9); stage 10's "17.0 km to where the
> day stops" is 16.962 to the vertex and 16.895 to BEKKU (→ 16.9). All three are the widest-Δ
> anchors in the section, which is why Iyo feels the choice where Tosa did not. Whichever way the
> pilgrimage settles it, **these three entries change together and no others do.**
>
> **"Holds" versus "reaches".** Iyo does **not** adopt Tosa's verb switch. It says "the record
> marks" or "the record holds" uniformly for what is there, and on each of the three stages with
> a drop it states the drop outright — the filed count, then the drop by kind and metres: "One of
> the seventy-one, a bus stop 301 m off the line, does not reach the day" (stage 4), "one, a
> hospital 325 m off the line, does not reach it" (stage 7), "two of them … do not reach the day"
> (stage 8). Every printed count is the filed count and every drop is subtracted in the same
> sentence, so no count in the fourteen silently includes a place the package does not carry.
> Awa, Tosa and Sanuki are other readers' work and are untouched.

> **Edited in Task 8c**, the third and last of the editing passes, applying the house style Task 8a set on
> Awa and Task 8b carried through Tosa. Four rules, applied to all fourteen narratives, to four themes and
> to three reflections: the dataset's own vocabulary comes out and the walker's goes in; the figure count
> comes down; no distance is given as a kilometre measured from the section's start; and no absence loses
> the hedge that keeps it a claim about a mapping rather than about the ground. **No measurement was
> re-opened and none was changed.** Every figure the Task 7c review verified still verifies at the value it
> verified; the figures this pass dropped were dropped from the prose only, and every one of them is still
> written out in the grounding bullets above each entry, which is where a reader who wants them should go.
>
> **Three of Task 7c's findings governed the pass rather than being overridden by it.** The section preamble
> asks that stages 1 and 13 not read as "nothing is there" and says both entries give their counts: both
> counts survive, at thirty-five and eighteen. The Dōgo refusal survives whole — stage 8 still describes the

>
> **Figures per narrative**, counted as Task 8b counted them — every numeral and every spelled number
> stating a count, a measurement, a rank or a bounded position, with ordinal pointers and the determiner

> worst 19; **7 · 9 · 6 · 8 · 5 · 8 · 9 · 8 · 7 · 7 · 9 · 8 · 9 · 6** after, mean **7.6** and worst **9** —
> Awa's own worst. **Dataset vocabulary 44 tokens → 0.** Twenty-three cumulative section kilometres removed,

**Read in Task 9b**, by a reader with no part in the drafting of any of the forty, no part in any of the
three editing passes, and no part in Task 9a's reading of Awa and Tosa. **All fourteen stages stand; nothing
was restored and nothing was rewritten**, so all fourteen lines are ticked again and all fourteen flags are
cleared. As on Awa and Tosa, this pass judged the *edits* and not the grounding: Task 7c re-measured the
line, all fifteen boundaries, all 26 temple positions and all 953 waypoint projections, and none of that was
re-opened. Every changed clause was read against the text Task 8c changed it from, on the three questions
Task 9a asked — did the edit strengthen a claim, did it detach a Task 7c argument from the sentence that
argument defends, and did an absence stop reading as *unmapped*.

**No edit was found that strengthened a claim.** Forty-four dataset tokens left these fourteen narratives
and every hedge they carried is still on the page, and all but one of them sits *inside* the clause it
protects, in the verb — "nothing is mapped in the 5.2 km before it" (stage 0), "nothing at all is mapped for
9.7 km" (stage 1), "the only ruins mapped in Iyo" (stage 2), "nowhere else in the section are beds mapped so
close together" (stage 8), "nothing is mapped as a place to stay" (stage 12). **The one exception is stage 5
and it is the shape Task 9a found once in Tosa**: the hedge moved *outside* the clause, into a governing "the map almost gives out:",
and what stands in the clause is "8.8 km with nothing on them". It survives on the precedent Task 9a
recorded — a world-scoped clause chained by an explicit connective to a map-scoped one — and is milder than
Tosa's, having one blank rather than two and no second blank left eleven words downstream of the
attribution. It is recorded, not acted on, in its own entry.

**Where a superlative or a hedge was re-verbed, the count that bounded it survived.** Both heights keep the
count of five that stops a reader inferring the twenty-one silent temples are lower (stages 6 and 11), and
stage 11 keeps the double bound Task 7c built — five *heights*, not twenty-six temples, and Iyo, not the
eighty-eight. **Both protected counts survive**: the section preamble names stages 1 and 13 as the two days
whose `momentsBeyondEnds` is zero and asks that neither read as "nothing is there", and the two narratives
still print **thirty-five** and **eighteen**, verified here at 35 and 18 features carrying `stageIndex: 1`
and `stageIndex: 13`, none of them dropped. **The Dōgo refusal is intact**: neither "Dōgo", "Dogo" nor 道後
appears in any of the fourteen themes, narratives or reflections, scanned on the committed `stages.json`.

**Four proportions replaced four counted totals, and each was re-derived rather than taken on the entry's
word.** Every one is a majority over both the filed set and the set that reaches the day, so no dropped
waypoint can falsify it: stage 4, **41 of 71** filed and **40 of 70** reaching; stage 7, **49 of 84** and
**49 of 83**; stage 9, **40 of 75** with no drops; stage 12, **59 of 84** with no drops. The three positive
existence claims that replaced totals were checked the same way — 23, 41 and 12 features of the named kind,
none of them in the report's drop list.

**Three new arithmetic claims were measured independently on the committed `route.main.geojson`**, because
Task 7c had no occasion to check them. Stage 10's rewritten leg: `temple-59` **277.2735 km**, `temple-60`
**305.0192**, so **27.746 km** between them, and the stage 10/11 boundary vertex at **294.23480**, so
**16.962 km** of that leg falls inside the day — 17.0 is *part of* 27.7 and the sentence now says so. Stage
11's span: exactly **four** features project between 296.848 and 311.372 km, and they are `temple-60` at
305.019 and three unnamed toilets at 303.071, 304.916 and 305.461, so "only the temple and three public
toilets" names all four and counts none of them twice. Stage 2's cluster: the six lodgings run **66.321 →
67.807 km** and the section's only `cultural_site` projects at **66.250**, which is where this pass's one
concern about that stage comes from.

**Two concerns are recorded rather than acted on**, under stages 2 and 5, and **two more readings are ruled
on where the brief asked for a ruling**, under stages 6 and 11. **One error was found in an editing note and
none in any narrative**: stage 4's entry computes the drop as "41 of the 70 that reach it" when the dropped
waypoint is itself a bus stop, so the reaching figures are 40 of 70. The claim is a majority under both and
no sentence moves.

Temple 39 Enkō-ji to Temple 65 Sankaku-ji, **365.677 km** of walked line (`ways/report.json`
`walkedLine`, 13,983 points), cut into fourteen days. Twenty-six waypoints here carry a
`templeNumber`, 40 through 65: Temple 39 is this section's start anchor, taken verbatim from
`overview.startPoint`, and its own numbered waypoint belongs to `shikoku-88-tosa`. The day cut
ends six of the fourteen days at a temple — stages 0, 2, 7, 9, 11 and 13, the last of those
under the boundary override. The other eight end at a lodging or a town.

`temple-44` 161.227 · `temple-45` 171.377 · `temple-46` 197.070 · `temple-47` 197.952 ·

The twenty-six steps, starting from the section's own 0.000 km at Temple 39: 27.156, 49.675,
3.066, 10.888, 70.442, 10.150, 25.693, 0.882, 4.444, 3.304, 1.750, 2.813, 11.142, 2.638,
34.947, 3.599, 3.098, 2.973, 2.377, 6.236, 27.745, 8.652, 1.497, 1.438, 3.276, 45.796 km.
They sum to 365.677, the whole line.

Four properties of that list carry most of the narratives below:

- **Four steps run over 30 km** — 70.442, 49.675, 45.796 and 34.947 — and together they carry
  **200.860 km, 54.9%** of the section. `metadata.json`'s `description` declares the four and
  calls the longest, Temple 43 → 44, **70.3 km**. Measured on the walked line it is
  **70.442**, and the 142 m difference is recorded here rather than smoothed: the description's
  legs are relation lengths (its own note says the member ways sum to 366.7 km against a
  365.677 km walked line) and everything in this file is a projection onto the walked line.
  The narratives use the measured figure, 70.4.
- **Three of the twenty-six steps fall inside the 25–30 km band the day cut looks in** —
  27.156, 25.693 and 27.745. That settles less than it looks: the first arm measures the band
  from the *previous day's end*, not from the previous temple, so five days end at a temple on
  that arm even though only three temple-to-temple legs are band-length. The sixth temple
  ending, stage 13, is the boundary override and not either arm.
- **The narrowest step is Temple 46 → 47 at 0.882 km**, and Temples 46 to 51 are six temples
  inside **13.193 km** with all five steps between them under 5 km — the run
  `metadata.json`'s `description` declares, and the only run of five consecutive sub-5 km
  steps anywhere in the section. The next-longest such run is four, Temples 54 to 58.
- **Neither the widest nor the narrowest step ends a day.** 70.442 km (Temple 43 → 44) is
  crossed by the tail of stage 3, the whole of stage 4 and the first 22.4 km of stage 5 —
  three days inside one step, and stage 4 is the only day in the section with no temple on it
  at all. 0.882 km (Temple 46 → 47) falls inside stage 7, which holds six temples and ends at
  the last of them.

**Places.** `waypoints.geojson` holds **953** features — the most of the four sections by a
long way, against Tosa's 372, Sanuki's 389 and Awa's 203, which come to 964 between them.
Per stage, by `stageIndex`: 52, 35, 134, 121, 71, 26, 16, 84, 117, 75, 88, 32, 84, 18. Of the 953, **473 are bus stops**, 82 `sacred_site`/`church`, 69 restaurants, 50
toilets, 49 convenience stores, 43 `sacred_site`/`wayside_shrine`, 41 accommodation (23
`hotel`, 14 `guesthouse`, 4 `hostel`), 28 hospitals, 26 temples, 23 cafés, 21 train stations,
18 vending machines, 15 pharmacies, 7 towns (4 `city`, 3 `town`), 4 fountains, 2 clinics, 1
`cultural_site` and **1 viewpoint**.

**Coverage, and the two days it hides.** `ways/report.json`'s `places` block reports
`placesPerStage: 10.4` with `sparse: false`. That figure is not places per stage in any plain
sense: it is the mean of `momentsBeyondEnds`, the count of `sacred_site`, `cultural_site`,
`viewpoint` and `town` waypoints on a stage that are neither its own start nor its own end.
10.4 is the best of the four sections (Awa 9.6, Sanuki 9.3, Tosa 3.1). Per stage it runs 4, 0,
19, 50, 16, 2, 3, 15, 5, 6, 13, 7, 6, 0 — and **stages 1 and 13 are zeros**. Nothing between
either of those days' two endpoints is a shrine, a town, a viewpoint or a ruin. Both
narratives say so in as many words, because `sparse: false` is a fact about the section and
those two days are not covered by it. A reviewer should check that neither sentence reads as
"nothing is there": stage 1 carries 35 recorded places and stage 13 carries 18, and both
entries below give the counts.

### What Iyo refused, on top of the shared list above

Everything under "What was refused" binds here unchanged. Beyond it, this section offered
eight things Awa and Tosa did not, or offered them differently.

- **"Enlightenment" as what Iyo means.** It sits verbatim in this section's own `description`
  — "Iyo carries the third of the four stages of spiritual progression the pilgrimage maps
  onto Shikoku's four provinces: enlightenment" — and again in
  `cultural.historicalSignificance`. Refused, on the reasoning that refused "awakening" for
  Awa and "discipline" for Tosa. §6's two sentences must both hold: a dōjō name's meaning is
  present in the section's own files and is not one of the kinds §6 enumerates, and it
  attaches to the section rather than to any day of it. **No narrative below names the dōjō or
  its meaning.**
- **`description` fields on two temple waypoints.** `temple-45` carries "The 'Cave Temple' —
  dramatically set in cliff faces." and `temple-51` "Near Dogo Onsen. National Treasure gate."
  Refused on Tosa's precedent for Cape Muroto and Cape Ashizuri, which is the sharper test:
  these attach to temples, which *are* an enumerated kind, and they still fail. The first is a
  terrain characterisation standing on no profile; the second names a place no `town` node
  here supports and a heritage designation that is not a temple, shrine, pass, town, distance
  or climb.
- **`tags` on three temple waypoints** — `temple-45` `["temple","mountain","scenic"]`,
  `temple-51` `["temple","national-treasure"]`, `temple-60` `["temple","mountain"]`. Refused
  exactly as Tosa refused the same field. `mountain` and `scenic` are terrain and character
  claims with no measurement behind them, and this is the kind of sentence the no-climb rule
  exists to stop.
- **"Dōgo" as a place.** Stage 8 opens on twelve lodgings inside 800 m, which is the Dōgo
  Onsen quarter, and any reader who knows Shikoku will recognise it. In this section the word
  exists only inside names — `node/5103430714` "Hotel Villa Dogo", `node/5635161092` "Hotel
  Eco Dogo", `node/13866539538` ホテル道後湯の町, `node/3534347860` 道後プリンスホテル, and the
  dropped bus stop `node/3152610454` 道後姫塚 — and in `temple-51`'s refused description. No
  `town` waypoint names it. So stage 8's narrative describes the cluster and does not name it,
  the way Tosa left Cape Ashizuri to a bus stop's name. Recorded here so the omission is
  visible.
- **Uwajima as a town or a city.** Stage 2's dense run passes through it, and
  `waypoints.geojson` holds seven `type: town` features of which Uwajima is not one — they are
  Sukumo, Ainan, Seiyo, Ozu, Uchiko, Kumakogen and Imabari. The name appears once in the text
  below, as **the name of a station**: `wp-osm-transport-node7832095789` / `node/7832095789`,
  `train_station`, "Uwajima" 宇和島, in the way Awa named Yuki, Tainohama and Kiki.
- **Three of the five temple elevations.** `temple-58` 250 m, `temple-64` 250 m and
  `temple-65` 350 m were available under the corrected shared paragraph above and are not
  used. Each would have been a bare number in a sentence that already had a shape to describe,
  and five heights scattered through fourteen days would make the section look as though it
  had a profile. See "The two heights used" below.
- **The stamp fee, the temple hours, the anniversary row.**
  `pilgrimage.stats.infrastructure` carries a 500 JPY stamp fee changed 2024-04-01, an
  08:00–17:00 opening changed the same day, and the 2015 trend row's 1200th-anniversary note.
  All refused: pilgrimage-wide, and two of the three are dates.
- **A single word for the sacred sites on stage 12.** Six `sacred_site` features stand in that
  day's stretch and their names do not agree on a kind — `node/10572420866` 白山神社 a shrine,
  `node/10688013064` 新居浜キリスト教会 a Christian church, `node/10673975133` 毘沙門堂 a hall,
  `node/10669837034` 萩生寺 and `node/6069894534` 別格12 延命寺 temples, and
  `node/11408953169` "Jizoharajizoan". The narrative calls them "six sacred places" and names
  none, because no one word covers them; Awa's wording for the church-subtype nodes is the
  precedent.

### The two heights used

`temple-45` 560 m appears in stage 6's narrative and `temple-60` 750 m in stage 11's. Both are
phrased as facts about the waypoint — "its waypoint is one of five in Iyo that carry a height,
and the number on it is 560 m"; "its waypoint carries a height of 750 m — the highest of the
five heights any temple in Iyo records". Three things were done deliberately and a reviewer
should rule on each:

- **Both sentences name the count of five.** Twenty-one of Iyo's twenty-six temples carry no
  `elevation`, and a height given without that count would let a reader infer the silent
  twenty-one are lower. They are not lower or higher; nothing is recorded for them.
- **Neither height stands next to a distance in a way that could be read as a gradient.** In
  stage 6 the 560 m sits in its own clause after "6.4 km in, 10.2 km on from Daihō-ji"; in
  stage 11 the 750 m sits after "10.8 km into this day, 27.7 km on from Iyo Kokubun-ji". A
  reviewer should judge whether the separation is enough, because the failure mode is exactly
  the one the shared paragraph warns about: a walker reading "6.4 km" and "560 m" in one
  sentence may take the second as the first's climb, and **nothing in this repository says
  what the ground does between any two of these points.**
- **Nothing else changed because of them.** Every narrative here is built from spacing, as
  Awa's and Tosa's are. The two heights are additions to two sentences, not the basis of any
  claim; delete both and no other sentence in the fourteen needs rewriting. That is the
  honest measure of what a point height is worth without a profile beside it.

### The four that reach the enrichment corridor and not the stage

`ways/report.json`'s per-stage `dropped` arrays hold four entries between them — on stages 4,
7 and 8, two of them on 8 — and its top-level `dropped` is empty, so a reader checking only
the top-level list sees nothing and has to go stage by stage. **All four are ordinary
near-misses of the 300 m limit**: the report's figures are 301, 303, 309 and 325 m. Nothing
here behaves like Tosa's Ashizuri
cluster, which sat 1.75–2.08 km from the walked line after passing the 300 m enrichment
corridor. The check still had to be run, because the two lines are just as far apart on this
section: enrichment measures against `route.geojson`, **636.621 km** across 14,893 points, and
`build-ways` against `route.main.geojson`, **365.677 km** across 13,983 — a factor of **1.74**.
Measured with `projectOnLine` against `route.main.geojson`, **no waypoint in this section
stands more than 323 m from the walked line**, so on Iyo the trap does not spring.

| waypoint | `report.json` | off `route.main.geojson` | off `route.geojson` | stage |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `node/3152605161` 大洲駅前, bus stop | 301 m | 298 m | 298 m | 4 |
| `node/1423738628` 杏林会東明病院, hospital | 325 m | 323 m | 281 m | 7 |
| `node/3152610454` 道後姫塚, bus stop | 309 m | 308 m | 273 m | 8 |
| `node/5500182421` unnamed fountain | 303 m | 298 m | 285 m | 8 |

The report's figure and this file's differ by 1–3 m on every row because `build-ways` measures
a mark against the stage's own shipped route points, after `simplify`, `strideCap` and
`roundLine`, and this file measures against the committed line. Two of the four sit under 300 m
on the committed line and are dropped anyway; the report's number is the one that decides, and
it is the one each entry below quotes. **None of the four is named in any narrative.** Stages 4
and 7 say a place does not reach the day and give its metres; stage 8 says two do.

### Where Iyo's day-ends are filed

`metadata.json`'s `provenance` records that 12 of the pilgrimage's 36 mid-route day-end places
are filed on the day that *begins* there rather than the one that arrives. **Six of the twelve
are in Iyo**, checked on the committed line; the other six are Tosa's, and Awa and Sanuki have
none. Every endpoint below is therefore read from `stages.json` and not from any waypoint's
`stageIndex`:

| day-end | projects at | boundary vertex | Δ | filed on | arriving day |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `temple-40` Kanjizai-ji | 27.15597 km | 27.15597 km (vertex 1094) | +3.6 × 10⁻¹² m | stage 1 | **stage 0** |
| `node/10858084271` Hotel Ailin | 53.79761 km | 53.81080 km (vertex 2012) | −13.19 m | stage 1 | stage 1 |
| `temple-42` Butsumoku-ji | 79.89694 km | 79.89694 km (vertex 3071) | 0.00 m | stage 2 | stage 2 |
| `node/691563007` Ozu | 112.35023 km | 112.35023 km (vertex 4292) | 0.00 m | stage 3 | stage 3 |
| `node/5923663885` Rairakuku | 138.83490 km | 138.82507 km (vertex 5061) | +9.83 m | stage 5 | **stage 4** |
| `node/5923659291` Karibaen | 164.92094 km | 164.93076 km (vertex 6179) | −9.81 m | stage 5 | stage 5 |
| `node/10858072227` Ichiriki | 182.39223 km | 182.37631 km (vertex 6931) | +15.92 m | stage 7 | **stage 6** |
| `temple-51` Ishite-ji | 210.26296 km | 210.26899 km (vertex 8295) | −6.03 m | stage 7 | stage 7 |
| `node/11535058569` Hōjō Suigun Youth Hostel | 234.28215 km | 234.26583 km (vertex 9270) | +16.32 m | stage 9 | **stage 8** |
| `temple-55` Nankōbō | 262.58983 km | 262.58343 km (vertex 10169) | +6.40 m | stage 10 | **stage 9** |
| `node/12944733844` ゲストハウス BEKKU | 294.16815 km | 294.23480 km (vertex 11158) | −66.65 m | stage 10 | stage 10 |
| `temple-64` Maegami-ji | 319.88102 km | 319.88102 km (vertex 12229) | 0.00 m | stage 11 | stage 11 |
| `node/12948927058` HOTEL AZ 愛媛土居インター店 | 352.63107 km | 352.62674 km (vertex 13509) | +4.33 m | stage 13 | **stage 12** |

All fifteen boundaries snapped; none went proportional. The sign of Δ decides every row, as it
does in Tosa: a day-end that projects past its own boundary vertex by any margin lands on the
day that begins there. **Kanjizai-ji is the extreme case `metadata.json`'s `provenance` names
by name** — it projects onto its vertex to within 3.6 × 10⁻¹² m, a floating-point residual, and
the residual is positive, so Temple 40 is filed on stage 1 rather than on the day that walks to
it. Butsumoku-ji, Ozu and Maegami-ji project onto their vertices to an exact zero and are filed
on the arriving day. The widest Δ in either direction is ゲストハウス BEKKU at −66.65 m, four
times the next largest here.

Two of the six misfilings are temples, and both bite the arithmetic below: **`temple-40` and
`temple-55` each stand at a day's end while carrying the following day's `stageIndex`**, so a
temple count read off `stageIndex` gives stage 0 no temples and stage 9 only one, and hands
stages 1 and 10 a temple that stands at their first step. Every count in this file is read from
the projections and the boundary spans instead, and stages 0 and 9 say in their **Places** line
that their own end temple is not among the features filed on them.

### Vocabulary, and one thing this section did not re-open

"Gate" stands for a temple in nine of the fourteen entries below — stages 0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9,
10 and 11 — and "bed" for an `accommodation` waypoint in three, stages 5, 8 and 12. Neither
word is in any file. Tosa's stage 0 note asks a reviewer to settle that substitution once for
the whole pilgrimage; **Iyo inherits the question and does not re-open it**, and if the answer
is no, these nine entries change with Tosa's eleven and not separately.

**Measurement.** As for Awa and Tosa: every along-line kilometre and off-line metre below is
the perpendicular projection of the place's own coordinate onto the committed
`routes/shikoku-88-iyo/route.main.geojson`, computed with `projectOnLine` from
`scripts/ways/geo.ts`; boundary vertices come from `stageBoundaries` on the same line. The
twenty-six temple waypoints carry no OSM id — they come from the Shikoku 88 official-site
source `metadata.json` declares under `provenance.sources` — and are cited as `temple-40` …
`temple-65`. Every other id is an OpenStreetMap node and resolves at
`https://api.openstreetmap.org/api/0.6/node/<id>.json`. Each **Distance** line gives the span
between the two boundary vertices, the figure `stages.json` declares, and `ways/report.json`'s
`sliceKm`, `ratio` and `passedGate` for the slice actually shipped.

**Romanisation.** The temple waypoints' `name.en` strings carry no macrons — "Ryuko-ji",
"Daiho-ji", "Joruri-ji", "Nankobo". The narratives macronise them, which is what Awa did with
the same field ("Ryozen-ji" → "Ryōzen-ji", "Shozan-ji" → "Shōzan-ji") and which two of this
section's own files corroborate: `node/4435546089` is named "55 南光坊 (Nankōbō)" and
`node/412335530` "61 Kōon-ji". One consequence is visible to a walker: `stages.json`'s stage 9
name is the unmacronned "Nankobo (Temple 55)" and stage 9's narrative says "Nankōbō", so the
title and the text differ on two vowels. A reviewer should decide which spelling wins.

### shikoku-88-iyo stage 0 — Enkō-ji (Temple 39) to Kanjizai-ji (Temple 40)

- **Start** Enkō-ji (Temple 39) — section boundary, 21 m off the line, at 0.000 km. The
  anchor's coordinates and name are `metadata.json`'s `overview.startPoint`, and
  `shikoku-88-tosa` ends at the same point
- **End** Kanjizai-ji (Temple 40) — `temple-40`, 45 m off the line, at 27.156 km. First arm:
  27.16 km past the section's start, inside the band and the only temple in it
- **Distance** 27.156 km between the boundary vertices against **27.2 km** declared. Shipped
  slice 26.915 km (`ways/report.json` `sliceKm`), ratio 0.9895, `passedGate: true`,
  `boundaryMode: snap`. Previously declared: **none** — this section's stages were cut in Task
  5b and have never carried another figure
- **Places** 52 filed on the stage; `ways/report.json` drops none of them. `temple-40` at the
  day's end is **not** among the 52 — it carries `stageIndex: 1`; see the day-ends table above
- **Grounding the text cites**
  - "Temple 39 to Temple 40 is 27.2 km, and this day is the whole of that step" — `temple-40`
    at **27.156 km** from the section's own 0.000 km at Temple 39, the first step in the list
    above, and the day's span is the same 27.156
  - "a gate at each end and none between" — no feature projecting inside 0.000–27.156 km
    carries a `templeNumber` except `temple-40` at the far end. The feature at **0.000 km** is
    `wp-osm-sacred_site-node4434097289` / `node/4434097289` (`sacred_site` / `church`, name
    "39 Enko-ji", `ja` 第39番札所 延光寺, 16 m off) — one of the church-subtype twins
    `metadata.json`'s `provenance` warns about, and not counted as a temple by the cut or by
    this text
  - "Fifty-two places are filed on it, twenty-three of them bus stops" — 52 features carry
    `stageIndex: 0`, of which 23 carry `subtype: bus_stop`
  - "Sukumo comes at 6.8 km and Ainan at 26.8" — `wp-osm-town-node691562989` /
    `node/691562989` (`town` / `city`, 宿毛市), 173 m off, at **6.818 km**; and
    `wp-osm-town-node691563002` / `node/691563002` (`town` / `town`, 愛南町), 179 m off, at
    **26.801 km**
  - "the twenty kilometres between them hold no town at all" — 26.801 − 6.818 = **19.983 km**,
    and no other `type: town` feature projects into it. The section's seven towns sit at
    6.818, 26.801, 92.342, 112.350, 124.926, 182.023 and 262.804 km
  - "the only viewpoint among Iyo's nine hundred and fifty-three recorded places" —
    `wp-osm-viewpoint-node10858037378` / `node/10858037378` (`viewpoint` / `viewpoint`, name
    茶偓跡), 11 m off, at **13.102 km**. It is the section's only `type: viewpoint` feature, 1
    of 953. It is called "a viewpoint" and not named, on the precedent of Tosa's unnamed
    rest-area viewpoint at 137.6 km
  - "the 5.2 km before it are the longest stretch of the day with nothing marked on them" —
    `node/5334189372` (中華旬菜 水滸, 277 m off) at 7.930 km to the viewpoint at 13.102 =
    **5.173 km**, against a next-longest gap on this stage of 3.161 km (13.102 → 16.264)

```
theme:      Two towns, and the only viewpoint Iyo records
narrative:  Temple 39 to Temple 40 is 27.2 km, and this day is the whole of that step: a gate at each end and none between. Fifty-two places are filed on it, twenty-three of them bus stops. Sukumo comes at 6.8 km and Ainan at 26.8, and the twenty kilometres between them hold no town at all. At 13.1 km, in the middle of that, stands the only viewpoint among Iyo's nine hundred and fifty-three recorded places, and the 5.2 km before it are the longest stretch of the day with nothing marked on them. Kanjizai-ji closes it at 27.2 km.
reflection: One viewpoint in nine hundred and fifty-three places, and the 5.2 km before it hold nothing at all — what were you looking at on the way there?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: span between vertex 0 and vertex 1094 is **27.15597 km**, `snap`; `temple-40`
**27.15597 / 45.1 m**; Sukumo **6.818 / 173.3 m**, Ainan **26.801 / 179.4 m**, difference
**19.983 km** with no other `type: town` projecting into it — the section's seven towns sit at
6.818, 26.801, 92.342, 112.350, 124.926, 182.023 and 262.804 ✓. The viewpoint
`node/10858037378` 茶偓跡 projects at **13.102 / 11.3 m** and is the only `type: viewpoint`
feature among all 953 ✓. **Zero** features of any type, on any stage, project strictly between
7.930 and 13.102, so the 5.173 km gap is real and is this day's longest against a next-longest
of 3.161 ✓. 52 features carry `stageIndex: 0`, 23 of them `bus_stop` ✓, and none is in the
report's drop list ✓.

- **The viewpoint superlative stands, and "recorded" does carry the weight.** The drafter asked
  for a ruling. The sentence is "the only viewpoint among Iyo's nine hundred and **fifty-three
  recorded places**" — the count of the record is inside the claim, not beside it, and the
  reflection repeats it ("One viewpoint in nine hundred and fifty-three places"). A superlative
  over a type with one member is exactly the kind of count Awa's entries are built from, and §6
  grounds text in what the section's files hold. Refusing it would mean the section may report
  its 473 bus stops and not its one viewpoint, which is not a rule this checklist states
  anywhere.
- Noted, not struck: the reflection's "the 5.2 km before it **hold** nothing at all" drops the
  "marked" the narrative carries one sentence earlier. Measurement backs it — nothing of any type
  projects there — and the clause it closes, "what were you looking at on the way there?",
  presumes there *was* something to look at, which defeats the reading that the ground is empty.
  The reflection is the antidote to the misreading rather than its source.
- Noted, not struck: "At 13.1 km, **in the middle of that**" means within the Sukumo–Ainan
  stretch, not at its midpoint (which is 16.81). The same construction recurs on stage 3; see
  there.
- **"A gate at each end and none between" is consistent with "fifty-two places are filed on
  it".** Neither gate is among the 52: Temple 39's own waypoint belongs to `shikoku-88-tosa` and
  `temple-40` carries `stageIndex: 1`. Zero features with `stageIndex: 0` carry a
  `templeNumber` ✓, so the two counts do not overlap and neither is inflated.

**Edited in Task 8c.** Twelve figures to seven, four pieces of dataset vocabulary to none, and the one
superlative Task 7c was asked to rule on kept — re-verbed, not removed.

```
theme:      Two towns, and the only viewpoint mapped in Iyo
narrative:  Enkō-ji to Kanjizai-ji is 27.2 km, and this day is the whole of that step: a gate at each end and none between. Twenty-three bus stops stand on it. Sukumo comes at 6.8 km and Ainan at 26.8, and the twenty kilometres between them hold no town at all. At 13.1 km, in the middle of that, stands the only viewpoint mapped anywhere in Iyo, and nothing is mapped in the 5.2 km before it — the longest such stretch on the day. Kanjizai-ji closes it.
reflection: One viewpoint in nine hundred and fifty-three places, and the 5.2 km before it hold nothing at all — what were you looking at on the way there?
```

- **"the only viewpoint among Iyo's nine hundred and fifty-three recorded places" → "the only viewpoint
  mapped anywhere in Iyo".** Task 7c cleared this *because of* the word "recorded", on the ground that "the
  count of the record is inside the claim, not beside it, and the reflection repeats it". Both halves of
  that defence survive. The hedge is not removed, it is moved into the verb — an unmapped viewpoint is a
  viewpoint still, and the sentence says only that one is mapped — which is exactly what Task 8b did with
  Tosa's stage 10 "the only water source **recorded** anywhere in Tosa", cleared by its own reviewer for the
  same reason. **And the count is still on the page, one sentence later**: the reflection's "One viewpoint in
  nine hundred and fifty-three places" is untouched, so the reader who wants the denominator still meets it,
  where it costs a walker nothing because it is that sentence's own subject. What leaves the narrative is
  three figures of inventory, on Awa's stage 3 rule.
- **"Fifty-two places are filed on it, twenty-three of them bus stops" → "Twenty-three bus stops stand on
  it".** *Filed on* is out-list vocabulary. What replaces it is a positive existence claim over the 23
  `bus_stop` features, none of which the report drops, so it needs no hedge — Tosa's stage 6
  shrines-and-hospitals precedent. The total goes as inventory; nothing in this entry or the section
  preamble rests on it, and this is not one of the two days the preamble asks to carry a count.
- **"the 5.2 km before it are the longest stretch of the day with nothing marked on them" → "nothing is
  mapped in the 5.2 km before it — the longest such stretch on the day".** One hedge where there was one,
  in the verb, with *such* carrying it into the superlative. Awa's stage 4 and Tosa's stages 1 and 13 form.
  **5.173 km is unchanged**, and so is its rank against the day's next-longest of 3.161.
- **"Temple 39 to Temple 40" → "Enkō-ji to Kanjizai-ji"**, and **"Kanjizai-ji closes it at 27.2 km" → "…
  closes it"**. Two temple numbers and one repeat of the day's own distance, all three of them figures a
  walker gets from the page's own stage heading. The measurement is untouched: 27.156 km, the section's
  first step, and the day's span is the same 27.156. Enkō-ji is the name `stages.json` gives this day's
  start and the name Tosa's stage 14 gives the same point.
- **Carried word for word, so the arguments stay attached**: "a gate at each end and none between", "the
  twenty kilometres between them hold no town at all", and "At 13.1 km, **in the middle of that**" — the
  construction Task 7c recorded as meaning *within* rather than *at the midpoint*, which recurs on stage 3
  and would have detached from both entries if either had been reworded.
- **Theme: "Two towns, and the only viewpoint Iyo records" → "…the only viewpoint mapped in Iyo"**, matching
  the narrative's verb.
- **The reflection is untouched**, including the "hold nothing at all" Task 7c noted without striking. Its
  defence — that "what were you looking at on the way there?" presumes there *was* something to look at —
  is a property of the sentence and survives an edit that did not touch it.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

The superlative Task 7c was asked to rule on keeps its qualification, and keeps it where that ruling put it.
Task 7c cleared "the only viewpoint among Iyo's nine hundred and fifty-three **recorded** places" *because
of* that word — "the count of the record is inside the claim, not beside it" — and "the only viewpoint
**mapped** anywhere in Iyo" carries the same qualification in the verb, inside the same clause, where an
unmapped viewpoint is a viewpoint still. The second half of that defence is untouched: the denominator has
not left the page, because the reflection's "One viewpoint in nine hundred and fifty-three places" was not
edited.

- **"Twenty-three bus stops stand on it" is a positive existence claim and is owed no hedge.** 23 of the 52
  features carrying `stageIndex: 0` are `subtype: bus_stop`, re-derived here, and `ways/report.json` drops
  none of them, so nothing an unmapped bus stop could do would falsify it. Losing the 52 costs nothing:
  this is not one of the two days the section preamble asks to carry a count.
- **"nothing is mapped in the 5.2 km before it — the longest such stretch on the day" keeps the hedge inside
  the clause**, in the verb, with *such* carrying it into the superlative. 5.173 km against this day's
  next-longest of 3.161 is unchanged.
- **The two clauses Task 7c argued are carried word for word** — "a gate at each end and none between", and
  "At 13.1 km, **in the middle of that**", the construction Task 7c ruled means *within* rather than *at the
  midpoint*. That ruling covers stage 3's identical construction as well, so rewording either would have
  detached one argument from two entries.
- **"Temple 39 to Temple 40" to "Enkō-ji to Kanjizai-ji" changes no claim.** Enkō-ji is the name
  `stages.json` gives this day's start and the name Tosa's stage 14 gives the same point, and the step it
  spans is unchanged at 27.156 km.

- [x] shikoku-88-iyo stage 0

### shikoku-88-iyo stage 1 — Kanjizai-ji (Temple 40) to Hotel Ailin

- **Start** Kanjizai-ji (Temple 40) — `temple-40`, 45 m off the line, at 27.156 km

```
theme:      The longest unmarked stretch in Iyo
narrative:  Kanjizai-ji to Ryūkō-ji is 49.7 km, the second-widest step between two temples in Iyo, and this day walks the first 26.7 of it — the next gate is still 23.0 km beyond where it stops. Thirty-five places are recorded on the way and twenty-four of them are bus stops; three convenience stores, two hospitals, two places to eat and a pharmacy are nearly all the rest. Nothing between the two ends is a shrine, a town or a viewpoint, which is true of only one other day here. And from 37.5 km to 47.3 the record marks nothing at all: 9.7 km, the longest such stretch anywhere in this section. The day ends at Hotel Ailin, 53.8 km in.
reflection: The longest unmarked stretch in the section falls in the middle of this day. Who decided what was worth marking?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged, and its one superlative was checked the hard way.**

Re-measured: span vertex 1094 → vertex 2012 is **26.65483 km**, `snap`; Hotel Ailin
**53.798 / 32.8 m**; 76.831 − 53.811 = **23.020 km** ✓ and 26.655 + 23.020 = 49.675, the
Kanjizai-ji → Ryūkō-ji step, second in the list only to 70.442 ✓. 35 features carry
`stageIndex: 1` — 24 `bus_stop`, 3 `convenience_store`, 2 `hospital`, 2 `restaurant`, 1
`pharmacy`, 1 vending machine, the end hotel and `temple-40` — so "nearly all the rest" is 8 of
the 11 non-bus-stop places ✓. `momentsBeyondEnds: 0`, and the only `sacred_site` on the stage is
`temple-40` at the day's own start ✓.

- **"the longest such stretch anywhere in this section" is correct, and it is the claim this
  review was most likely to break.** Sanuki's stage 0 lost the same construction because its
  grounding compared per-stage *maxima* and discarded a second run inside another stage. So this
  one was not checked per stage: all 953 projections were sorted into one list and every
  consecutive difference taken. The section's six widest gaps are **9.747** (37.548 → 47.295,
  this day), 9.569 (stage 6), 8.810 (stage 5), 6.222 (stage 11), 5.910 (stage 11) and 5.173
  (stage 0). Nothing of any type, on any stage, projects strictly between the two bus stops
  `node/3152607473` 柏 and `node/3152605200` 大門 — exclusive count **0**. Re-run with the four
  dropped waypoints excluded, the ranking is unchanged. **9.747 km is the section maximum**, and
  the "second-longest" on stage 6 and "third-longest" on stage 5 fall out of the same list.
- **"which is true of only one other day here" stands.** The `momentsBeyondEnds` row has exactly
  two zeros, this stage and stage 13 ✓. The cross-stage objection is real and is Awa's stage 1
  and Tosa's stage 4 question, not a new one; nothing here is false, and if the pilgrimage
  settles against cross-stage comparison this entry changes with theirs and not separately.
- **The reflection is honest about what it is asking.** "Who decided what was worth marking?" is
  a question about the record, asked where the day's whole shape is a coverage fact. It does not
  tell a walker the road is empty; the sentence two clauses earlier gives the day's 35 places.

**Edited in Task 8c. The stage whose count the section preamble protects, and the count is still here.**

The preamble asks a reviewer to check that this day does not read as "nothing is there", and records that
the entry gives its 35 places. **It still does.** That constraint is why this narrative loses fewer figures
than any other in the fourteen and why it lands at nine rather than at the section's new mean.

```
theme:      The longest stretch in Iyo with nothing mapped
narrative:  Kanjizai-ji to Ryūkō-ji is 49.7 km, the second-widest step between two temples in Iyo, and this day walks the first 26.7 of it — the next gate is still 23.0 km beyond where it stops. Thirty-five places are mapped on the way, twenty-four of them bus stops, and shops, hospitals, places to eat and a pharmacy are nearly all the rest. Nothing between its ends is a shrine, a town or a viewpoint, which is true of only one other day here. And nothing at all is mapped for 9.7 km, the longest such stretch anywhere in this section. The day ends at Hotel Ailin.
reflection: The longest stretch in the section with nothing mapped falls in the middle of this day. Who decided what was worth mapping?
```

- **"Thirty-five places are recorded on the way and twenty-four of them are bus stops" → "Thirty-five places
  are mapped on the way, twenty-four of them bus stops".** One word. The count stays because the preamble
  needs it to; `recorded` goes because the out-list bans it and *mapped* carries the same qualification in
  the verb.
- **"three convenience stores, two hospitals, two places to eat and a pharmacy are nearly all the rest" →
  "shops, hospitals, places to eat and a pharmacy are nearly all the rest".** Three figures out, and the
  claim is the one Task 7c verified: the same eight of the eleven non-bus-stop places, named by kind instead
  of counted. Strictly less specific and therefore not stronger.
- **"from 37.5 km to 47.3 the record marks nothing at all: 9.7 km, the longest such stretch anywhere in this
  section" → "nothing at all is mapped for 9.7 km, the longest such stretch anywhere in this section".**
  Two cumulative section kilometres out — a walker reading this day has no frame for 37.5 — and *the record
  marks* replaced by the verb. **The superlative is carried word for word**, because it is the claim Task 7c
  says this review "was most likely to break" and checked against all 953 projections rather than per-stage
  maxima; rewording it would have put that argument out of reach of the sentence it defends.
- **"The day ends at Hotel Ailin, 53.8 km in" → "The day ends at Hotel Ailin".** The third cumulative
  kilometre.
- **"Nothing between the two ends" → "Nothing between its ends"**, one figure, no claim moved: a day has two
  ends. The clause Task 7c ruled on — "which is true of only one other day here" — is untouched.
- **Reflection: "The longest unmarked stretch in the section falls in the middle of this day. Who decided
  what was worth marking?" → "The longest stretch in the section with nothing mapped … Who decided what was
  worth mapping?"** Task 7c's defence stands word for word under the new verb: it is still a question about
  the record, asked where the day's whole shape is a coverage fact, and it still does not tell a walker the
  road is empty — the sentence two clauses earlier still gives the day's 35 places. **"unmapped" was drafted
  and rejected** for Task 8b's reason: it reads as a claim that the road is not in OpenStreetMap, which is
  false and would be *stronger*, not weaker.
- **Theme: "The longest unmarked stretch in Iyo" → "The longest stretch in Iyo with nothing mapped"**, the
  same rejection and the same replacement.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands; the flag is cleared, and the protected count is still here.**

35 features carry `stageIndex: 1` and the report drops none, re-derived here, and the narrative still prints
thirty-five. This is one of the two days the section preamble asks a reviewer to check does not read as
"nothing is there", and it does not: the count is in the second sentence, and the day's 9.7 km blank stands
four words from a verb that says *mapped*.

- **The superlative is carried word for word, which is the whole of why this entry is safe.** Task 7c called
  "the longest such stretch anywhere in this section" the claim this review "was most likely to break" and
  checked it by sorting all 953 projections into one list rather than by comparing per-stage maxima — the
  check whose absence cost Sanuki's stage 0 a clause. Rewording it would have put that argument out of reach
  of the sentence it defends. What went is "from 37.5 km to 47.3", two kilometres measured from the
  section's start that a walker reading one day has no frame for; 9.747 km is untouched.
- **"three convenience stores, two hospitals, two places to eat and a pharmacy" to "shops, hospitals, places
  to eat and a pharmacy" is strictly less specific and therefore not stronger.** The same eight of the
  eleven non-bus-stop places are named, by kind instead of by count, and every plural has its members: 3
  `convenience_store`, 2 `hospital`, 2 `restaurant`.
- **Rejecting "unmapped" for the theme and the reflection was right.** It reads as a claim that the road is
  not in OpenStreetMap, which is false and *stronger* than anything the drafter wrote. "with nothing mapped"
  is the weaker form and the true one.
- **"Nothing between the two ends" to "Nothing between its ends" moves no claim** — a day has two ends — and
  the clause Task 7c ruled on beside it, "which is true of only one other day here", is untouched.

```
theme:      One hundred and thirty-four places in twenty-six kilometres
narrative:  More is recorded on this day than on any other in Iyo — one hundred and thirty-four places on 26.1 km, sixty-two of them bus stops and eighteen of them places to eat. Much of it arrives at once: six of the section's forty-one places to stay stand inside 1.5 km of each other, the Uwajima station among them, and at 66.3 km the only ruins Iyo records. Then it thins, and both of the day's temples fall in its last 3.1 km — Ryūkō-ji at 76.8 km, 49.7 km on from Kanjizai-ji, and Butsumoku-ji at 79.9, where the day ends.
reflection: The busiest day in the section, and both its gates stand in the last three kilometres of it. Which part will you have been walking for?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged, with one rounding recorded.**

Re-measured: span vertex 2012 → vertex 3071 is **26.08614 km**, `snap`. 134 features carry
`stageIndex: 2` — the section maximum against 121, 117, 88, 84, 84, 75, 71, 52, 35, 32, 26, 18,
16 ✓ — 62 of them `bus_stop` and 18 `type: food` (15 `restaurant`, 3 `cafe`) ✓. The six lodgings
run **66.321 → 67.807**, span **1.486 km**, out of **41** `type: accommodation` features in the
section ✓; sliding a 1.5 km window along all 41 gives a maximum of 6 anywhere outside the stage
8 cluster ✓, so "six … inside 1.5 km" is not beaten elsewhere by the same measure. Uwajima
station **67.362 / 150.8 m** falls inside that span and Kita-Uwajima **69.135** falls outside ✓.
`node/9719709612` 煙硝矢倉跡 is the section's only `type: cultural_site` feature, **66.250 /
104 m** ✓. `temple-41` **76.831 / 3.2 m**, `temple-42` **79.897 / 69.8 m**, step **3.066 km** ✓.
No drops on this stage ✓.

- **"Then it thins" stands.** Independently counted: 17 features project between 69.4 and 76.7
  km against **72** in the 5 km before — the checklist's own figures reproduce. It is a reading,
  and it is a reading of a measured density rather than a characterisation with nothing behind
  it, which is what Awa's struck "ordinary" was.
- Noted, not struck: the reflection's "both its gates stand in **the last three kilometres** of
  it". The step is **3.066 km**, so Ryūkō-ji stands 66 m outside a literal last-3.000 km. The
  narrative one sentence earlier prints the true figure, 3.1, and this section's reflections
  round throughout — stage 9's "Twenty-five kilometres" for 24.725, stage 11's "fourteen
  kilometres" for 14.524, stage 6's "nine and a half" for 9.569. This is the only one of them
  where the rounding shrinks a *container* rather than a distance, which is why it is recorded
  and not left silent; it is not false under the reading the narrative has just supplied.
- **The inventory objection is not a defect.** A day of 134 places cannot name them all, and the
  narrative names only what it can ground as a kind — the density, the six beds, one station, the
  section's only ruins, the two temples. The unnamed fountain at 57.957 km, the three wayside
  shrines and the seventeen churches are counted in the 134 and nowhere misrepresented. Tosa's
  stage 2 raises the same question and it is the same answer.
- **"the only ruins Iyo records" is a claim about the record's own classification** and is exact:
  one `type: cultural_site` feature in 953. Recorded because stage 0's viewpoint 茶偓跡 also
  carries a 跡 name; the file types it `viewpoint`, and the sentence claims what the file
  classifies, not what a name might mean.

**Edited in Task 8c.** Twelve figures to six — the largest cut in the fourteen, and every one of them an
inventory rather than a fact.

```
theme:      More places than on any other day in Iyo
narrative:  More places stand on this day than on any other in Iyo — sixty-two bus stops among them, and eighteen places to eat. Much of it arrives at once: six of the section's places to stay stand inside 1.5 km of each other, the Uwajima station among them, and the only ruins mapped in Iyo. Then it thins, and both of the day's temples fall in its last 3.1 km — Ryūkō-ji, 49.7 km on from Kanjizai-ji, and Butsumoku-ji, where the day ends.
reflection: The busiest day in the section, and both its gates stand in the last three kilometres of it. Which part will you have been walking for?
```

- **"More is recorded on this day than on any other in Iyo — one hundred and thirty-four places on 26.1 km,
  sixty-two of them bus stops and eighteen of them places to eat" → "More places stand on this day than on
  any other in Iyo — sixty-two bus stops among them, and eighteen places to eat".** This is Awa's stage 3
  move exactly, and *recorded* goes without replacement for Awa's reason: **this is not an absence claim.**
  It is a comparison of fourteen days counted the same way out of one file (134 against 121, 117, 88, 84,
  84, 75, 71, 52, 35, 32, 26, 18, 16), and a reader who takes it as "this day has more on it than the
  others" is misled by nothing. The counts stay in the grounding bullets above.
- **"six of the section's forty-one places to stay" → "six of the section's places to stay".** A partitive
  without its total is weaker, not stronger, and Task 7c's finding is untouched: sliding a 1.5 km window
  along all 41 accommodation projections gives a maximum of six anywhere outside the stage 8 cluster.
- **"at 66.3 km the only ruins Iyo records" → "the only ruins mapped in Iyo".** One cumulative kilometre
  out and the hedge re-verbed. Task 7c's ruling holds unchanged: it is "a claim about the record's own
  classification" — one `cultural_site` feature in 953 — and *mapped* says exactly that and no more.
- **"Ryūkō-ji at 76.8 km … and Butsumoku-ji at 79.9" → "Ryūkō-ji … and Butsumoku-ji".** Two more cumulative
  kilometres. The step between them, 3.1 km, stays and is the figure a walker can act on.
- **"Then it thins" is carried word for word**, with the density Task 7c independently counted behind it.
- **Theme: "One hundred and thirty-four places in twenty-six kilometres" → "More places than on any other
  day in Iyo".** The only theme in the twenty edited for its register rather than for its vocabulary, and
  the reason is coherence on one page: with 134 gone from the narrative under Awa's stage 3 rule, a headline
  that printed it would have been the pass contradicting itself where a walker reads both at once. **134 is
  still the largest of the fourteen counts** and the new theme claims exactly that and no more.
- **The reflection is untouched**, including the "last three kilometres" rounding Task 7c recorded without
  striking — it is not false under the reading the narrative supplies, and the narrative still supplies it.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands and the flag is cleared, but one clause lost the figure that kept it
precise, and that is recorded.**

**The concern.** "Much of it arrives at once: six of the section's places to stay stand inside 1.5 km of
each other, the Uwajima station among them, and the only ruins mapped in Iyo." The drafted sentence read
"… the Uwajima station among them, and **at 66.3 km** the only ruins Iyo records", and that figure did more
than register: it gave the ruins a position of their own, so the appositive "among them" governed the
station alone. Without it the ruins sit at the end of a list whose most economical reading extends "among
them" over both, and under that reading the sentence puts the ruins inside the lodging cluster.
**Re-measured here, they are not.** The six lodgings run **66.321 → 67.807 km** and
`node/9719709612` 煙硝矢倉跡 projects at **66.250** — 71 m before the first bed, outside the span. Task 7c
verified the station's membership of that span deliberately and recorded that Kita-Uwajima was left out of
the sentence *because* it falls outside it, so this is a span whose edges the entry has already been asked
to respect.

**Why it stands.** Under the other available reading — "Much of it arrives at once: A, B, and C" — the ruins
are simply another thing arriving in the dense run, which is true and is what the sentence's own topic
clause sets up. And the margin is 71 m, below the precision the sentence prints: at one decimal the ruins
and the cluster's first bed are both 66.3. This file's own precedent covers a margin of that size in the
same direction — Task 7c recorded this very stage's reflection rounding a *container* 66 m smaller than the
truth and did not strike it. **Restoring "at 66.3 km" would put back the one thing Task 8c removed from all
fourteen narratives, a kilometre measured from the section's start; rewriting the clause in my own words
would leave this stage drafted for a wording that is not false.** So it is recorded rather than acted on:
**the fix, the next time this text is opened, costs no figure at all — "and, just before them, the only
ruins mapped in Iyo".**

- **"the only ruins mapped in Iyo" is otherwise exactly Task 7c's ruling**, which was that the claim is
  "about the record's own classification" — one `type: cultural_site` feature in 953 — and *mapped* says
  that and no more.
- **"More places stand on this day than on any other in Iyo" is owed no hedge.** It compares fourteen days
  counted the same way out of one file: 134 against 121, 117, 88, 84, 84, 75, 71, 52, 35, 32, 26, 18 and 16,
  re-derived here. A reader who takes it as "this day has more on it than the others" is misled by nothing.
- **"six of the section's forty-one places to stay" to "six of the section's places to stay" is weaker**, a
  partitive without its total, and Task 7c's sliding-window finding is untouched.
- **"Then it thins" is carried word for word**, with the density Task 7c independently counted still behind
  it, and the reflection — including the "last three kilometres" rounding Task 7c recorded — is untouched.

Meiseki-ji to Daihō-ji is 70.4 km, the widest step between two temples in Iyo, and 48.9 km of it are still ahead.
