**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged, with two readings recorded.**

Re-measured: span vertex 3071 → vertex 4292 is **32.45329 km**, `snap`, the second-longest of
the fourteen, **292.4 m** under stage 12's 32.74572 ✓. `temple-43` **90.785 / 14.0 m**, 10.888 km
past `temple-42`, and the only feature carrying a `templeNumber` inside the span ✓. The series
runs `node/7398047551` **90.786** to `node/7398047582` **92.442**, span **1.656 km**, **28**
features ✓; 43 `subtype: wayside_shrine` features in the section, 31 of them on this stage, the
other three at 87.661, 90.241 and 100.633 ✓, and three more at 79.312 / 79.894 / 79.897 filed on
stage 2 within 585 m of Butsumoku-ji ✓. Seiyo **92.342 / 150.8 m**, Ozu **112.350 / 98.7 m**,
112.350 − 92.342 = **20.008 km** ✓, 161.227 − 112.350 = **48.877 km** ✓. No drops ✓.

- **"the record does something it does nowhere else in Iyo" is heard as being about the record,
  and it has to be.** The subject of the clause is "the record" and the colon that follows counts
  features, not shrines encountered. Nothing in the sentence says these are the only such shrines
  on the ground, and the count it draws — 28 of 43, more than half — is meaningless except as a
  count of the file. Verified against all 43.
- Recorded, not struck: **one of the twenty-eight is not numbered.** `node/7398047556` is named
  坪ヶ内新四国**番外** — the "outside-the-numbering" station of such a circuit — and the other 27
  end in a numeral. The narrative's "twenty-eight numbered wayside shrines **of a single
  series**" is true of the series and of 27 of its members. **The grounding bullet above
  overstates it**: it says the 28 are "features whose `ja` name begins 坪ヶ内新四国 and ends in a
  number", and one ends in 外. The bullet is left as its drafter wrote it and corrected here, on
  the convention this file uses for corrections. Nothing downstream moves — 27 or 28, the count
  is still more than half of 43, and 1.656 km still rounds to 1.7.
- Recorded, not struck: **"Seiyo comes at 92.3 km, in the middle of them" means amongst them, not
  at their midpoint.** 25 of the 28 project before Seiyo and **3** after; the series midpoint is
  91.614. Under the midpoint reading it is false by 728 m; under the "amongst" reading it is true,
  and true in the way that matters — three shrines still come after the town, so the town is
  genuinely inside the run and not at its edge. The grounding bullet says "between", which is the
  narrower and safer word. Stage 0 uses the same construction for the same sense.
- **The series is left unexplained, correctly.** What the numbers count, what circuit they
  belong to and how it relates to the 88 is nowhere in this repository, so it is outside the
  sentence — Awa's Bandō precedent exactly.

**Edited in Task 8c. One of the four collisions, resolved in the open.**

```
theme:      Twenty-eight numbered shrines in less than two kilometres
narrative:  Meiseki-ji stands 10.9 km into this day and is the only temple on it. Just past it the map does something it does nowhere else in Iyo: twenty-eight numbered wayside shrines of a single series inside 1.7 km — more than half of all the wayside shrines mapped in the section. Seiyo comes in the middle of them. After that the day runs 20.0 km to Ozu and stops there. Meiseki-ji to Daihō-ji is 70.4 km, the widest step between two temples in Iyo, and 48.9 km of it are still ahead.
reflection: Twenty-eight numbered shrines inside 1.7 km, and one temple in 32.5 — which of those two counts is the day?
```

- **"Just past it **the record** does something it does nowhere else in Iyo" → "Just past it **the map**
  does something it does nowhere else in Iyo".** Rule against the out-list says *the record* goes; the rule
  that an argued clause is carried word for word says it stays, because **Task 7c's defence is about that
  very noun**: "The subject of the clause is 'the record' and the colon that follows counts features, not
  shrines encountered." Resolved by swapping the noun for the one the corpus has already sanctioned in the
  same role — Awa's stage 3 reflection says "where **the map** let it end" — so the subject is still a word
  for the record, the colon still introduces a count of the file, and the defence transfers with one word
  changed. It is not "twenty-eight shrines stand there", which would be the claim about the ground the
  sentence has always refused.
- **"all between 90.8 and 92.4 km — 1.7 km holding more than half of the forty-three wayside shrines the
  whole section records" → "inside 1.7 km — more than half of all the wayside shrines mapped in the
  section".** Two cumulative kilometres and one inventory out; *the whole section records* becomes *mapped*.
  **28 of 43 is unchanged**, and so is the rounding: 1.656 km still prints 1.7.
- **"twenty-eight numbered wayside shrines of a single series" is carried word for word.** Task 7c recorded
  that one of the twenty-eight is 坪ヶ内新四国**番外** and ruled the phrase true of the series and of 27 of
  its members. Rewording it would have detached that ruling from the sentence it settles.
- **"Seiyo comes at 92.3 km, in the middle of them" → "Seiyo comes in the middle of them".** The cumulative
  kilometre goes; **"in the middle of them" is carried word for word**, because Task 7c ruled on exactly
  those words — amongst them, not at their midpoint — and the same construction on stage 0 is carried for
  the same reason.
- **"stops there, 32.5 km in" → "stops there".** The day's own distance, which every consumer prints beside
  the text. No figure was added anywhere to replace it.
- The reflection is untouched.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

Task 7c's defence of this narrative's central clause is about a noun — "The subject of the clause is 'the
record' and the colon that follows counts features, not shrines encountered" — so swapping *the record* for

```
theme:      Twenty-two kilometres, and then the temple
narrative:  Daihō-ji stands 22.4 km into this day and closes the 70.4 km that have run since Meiseki-ji. Before it the map almost gives out: 8.8 km with nothing on them, the third-longest such run in Iyo. Six places to stay stand on the day — three of them inside its last 700 m — and seven public toilets. Karibaen, the last of the beds, ends the day.
reflection: Twenty-two kilometres to one gate, then three beds inside seven hundred metres. The day's shape is all at its ends — where does its middle go?
```

- **"Before it the record almost stops: 8.8 km from 145.8 km with nothing marked on them" → "Before it the
  map almost gives out: 8.8 km with nothing on them".** Task 7c cleared "the record almost stops" because
  "It says 'the record', so it makes no claim about the ground", and asked that it be judged with Tosa's
  stage 11 — which carried the identical phrase and which Task 8b rewrote to **"the map almost gives out"**.
  The two are judged together, as their drafter asked, and land on the same words. The governing clause
  carries the attribution for the span beneath it, so *marked* was the same claim written twice. **8.810 km
  and its third place in the section-wide gap list are unchanged**; 145.8 was a cumulative kilometre and is
  out.
- **"Twenty-six places are recorded on the whole day, and six of them are places to stay — three of those
  inside the last 700 m — while seven are public toilets" → "Six places to stay stand on the day — three of
  them inside its last 700 m — and seven public toilets".** The total goes as inventory; what remains is two
  positive existence claims over eight named-by-kind features, none of which the report drops, so neither
  needs a hedge — Tosa's stage 6 precedent, and Awa's stage 4 closing list.
- **"ends the day at 164.9 km" → "ends the day".** The cumulative kilometre.
- **"Karibaen, the last of the beds" is carried word for word**, and so is the reflection, whose "The day's
  shape is all at its ends" Task 7c defended on the exactness of both halves of the sentence before it —
  both of which this pass leaves standing.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands and the flag is cleared, and this is the one narrative in the fourteen
whose hedge sits outside the clause it protects.**

**The concern.** "Before it the map almost gives out: 8.8 km with nothing on them, the third-longest such
run in Iyo." Everywhere else in these fourteen the mapping is in the verb of the clause itself — "nothing is
mapped in the 5.2 km before it" (stage 0), "nothing at all is mapped for 9.7 km" (stage 1), "nothing is
mapped as a place to stay" (stage 12). Here it is four words upstream, and what stands in the clause is
*nothing on them*, whose natural antecedent is the 8.8 km of ground rather than the map. It is the shape
Task 9a found once in Tosa's stage 11, reached by the same substitution.

**Why it stands.** On the precedent Task 9a recorded, and on two things that make it the milder of the pair.
The governing clause is explicit, names *the map*, sits in the same sentence, and is joined by a colon whose
only function is to elaborate it — the construction Task 8a settled when it kept "so the **day** had nowhere
nearer to stop" over the unchained "so there was nowhere nearer to stop". And where Tosa's stage 11 leaves a
*second* blank eleven words further on with no qualifier at all, this sentence has one blank, immediately
after the colon. **Restoring *marked* would undo a section-wide vocabulary decision on a case the corpus's
own precedent covers, and rewriting it would leave this stage drafted for a wording that is not false.** So
it is recorded rather than acted on, with Task 9a's own fix: **put the hedge back in the verb — "8.8 km with
nothing mapped on them" — the next time this text is opened.** 8.810 km and its third place in the
section-wide gap list are unchanged.

- **Judging it with Tosa's stage 11 is what its drafter asked for.** Task 7c cleared "the record almost
  stops" and asked that it be judged with Tosa's identical phrase; Task 8b and Task 8c landed the two on the
  same words, so they are still judged together and still say the same thing — including the same weakness.
- **"Six places to stay stand on the day … and seven public toilets" are two positive existence claims**
  over eight features the report does not drop, so neither is owed a hedge: 6 `type: accommodation` and 7
  `subtype: toilet` of the 26, re-derived here.
- **"three of them inside its last 700 m" holds under either measure** — 0.665 km across the three beds,
  0.675 km from the first of them to the day's end vertex.
- **"Karibaen, the last of the beds" and the whole reflection are carried word for word**, so Task 7c's

**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged. The height stays, and here is why.**

Re-measured: span vertex 6179 → vertex 6931 is **17.44556 km**, `snap`, the second-shortest of
the fourteen behind 13.050 ✓. 16 features carry `stageIndex: 6`, the section minimum, 12 of them
`bus_stop`, the other four `node/8374601817` 国民宿舎古岩屋荘 (168.779 / 9.2 m), one
`sacred_site`/`church`, Kumakogen and `temple-45` ✓. `temple-45` **171.377 / 7.2 m**,
`elevation` **560**, and exactly five of the section's 26 temple waypoints carry an `elevation`
(45, 58, 60, 64, 65) ✓. **Nothing of any type projects strictly between 171.377 and 180.946**,
so 9.569 km is exact and is second in the section-wide list ✓. Kumakogen **182.023 / 25.7 m**;
182.392 − 182.023 = **0.369 km** ✓.

- **The 560 m stands.** It is a point `elevation` on a temple waypoint, which the shared
  paragraph's Task 6c correction admits as an OSM tag on a temple — an enumerated kind — and it
  is stated as a height at that temple and nothing more. Three tests, all passed. The clause is
  attributed to the record, not the ground: "**its waypoint** is one of five in Iyo that carry a
  height, and **the number on it** is 560 m." It is separated from the kilometres by a semicolon
  and a change of subject, so the reader is not handed "6.4 km" and "560 m" as a ratio. And it
  names the count of five, so the twenty-one temples with no `elevation` read as unrecorded
  rather than lower. Nothing anywhere in the fourteen calls this day, or any day, a climb, hard,
  gentle, level or downhill — checked across all fourteen narratives, themes and reflections.
- **Nor should it come out.** The drafter's own honest test — delete both heights and no other
  sentence needs rewriting — is an argument from minimalism, not from truth, and this review
  strikes what is false, not what is optional. A grounded, admissible, true clause is not an
  ungrounded one, and deleting it would be authorship. A later editor who prefers the section
  described by spacing alone has a free hand; a reader does not.
- **Recorded: this is one of the three entries the distance convention moves.** "Iwaya-ji comes
  6.4 km in" is **6.446 km** from the day's boundary vertex, which is what the grounding declares
  and what the shipped slice starts from, and **6.456 km** from Karibaen's own projection, which
  would print **6.5**. Karibaen sits 9.81 m short of its vertex, and the rounding falls on that
  10 m. Both figures are true of what they measure; the section chooses the vertex, consistently.
- Noted: the narrative prints the day at **17.4 km** (the vertex span, 17.4456) while
  `stages.json` declares `distanceKm: 17.5` (anchor to anchor, 17.4713). A walker reading this
  package sees both. See the section blockquote; this is the convention's sharpest practical
  consequence and it is not a falsehood in either direction.

**Edited in Task 8c. The second collision, and the one that cost the most thought.**

```
theme:      The fewest places of any day in Iyo
narrative:  Sixteen places are mapped on this day, the fewest of any day here, and twelve of them are bus stops. Iwaya-ji comes 6.4 km in, 10.2 km on from Daihō-ji; the map gives it a height of 560 m, one of five heights it gives any temple in Iyo. Past the temple nothing is mapped for 9.6 km, the second-longest such run here. Then the town of Kumakogen, and just past it the guesthouse where the day ends — the shortest Iyo has cut but one.
reflection: Sixteen places mapped in 17.4 km, and nine and a half of those kilometres hold none of them. Is a day like that short, or long?
```

- **"its waypoint is one of five in Iyo that carry a height, and the number on it is 560 m" → "the map gives
  it a height of 560 m, one of five heights it gives any temple in Iyo".** *Waypoint* is on the out-list;
  Task 7c's clearance of this clause quotes it — *"The clause is attributed to the record, not the ground:
  '**its waypoint** is one of five in Iyo that carry a height, and **the number on it** is 560 m'"* — so the
  two rules meet head on, as they did at Tosa's stage 3. Resolved the way Tosa's was: reword, then reconcile
  the argument with the new text rather than let it detach in silence. **Task 7c named three tests and the
  new wording passes all three.** The subject is *the map*, so the clause is still attributed to the record
  and not to the ground. It still sits after a semicolon and a change of subject, so a reader is not handed
  "6.4 km" and "560 m" as a ratio. And it still **names the count of five**, which is the guard the shared
  paragraph demands: without it a reader could infer the twenty-one temples with no `elevation` are lower,
  and they are neither lower nor higher. Nothing anywhere in the fourteen calls this day a climb, and this
  pass added no such word.
- **"Sixteen places are recorded on this day, the fewest of the fourteen" → "Sixteen places are mapped on
  this day, the fewest of any day here".** One out-list word and one figure. The minimum Task 7c verified
  against all fourteen counts is unchanged.
- **"Past the temple the record marks nothing for 9.6 km" → "Past the temple nothing is mapped for 9.6
  km".** The hedge into the verb; 9.569 km and its second place in the section-wide list are unchanged.
- **"the town of Kumakogen at 182.0 km and, 0.4 km after it, the guesthouse where the day ends at 17.4 km" →
  "the town of Kumakogen, and just past it the guesthouse where the day ends".** One cumulative kilometre
  out, and 0.369 km becomes *just past it*, which names a range containing the measurement and is therefore
  the weaker form. **Removing "17.4 km" also removes the section's sharpest practical consequence of the
  distance convention** — Task 7c noted that the narrative printed 17.4 (the vertex span) where
  `stages.json` declares 17.5 (anchor to anchor), and that a walker sees both. The walker now sees one, the
  declared figure, and the convention question is untouched: this pass settles nothing about it, and the
  three entries the convention moves still move together.
- **"the shortest Iyo has cut but one" is carried word for word.**
- **Reflection: "Sixteen marked places in 17.4 km" → "Sixteen places mapped in 17.4 km".** One word, and the
  only reflection change on this stage; the rest of the sentence, including the "nine and a half" rounding
  Task 7c listed among the section's spoken-register roundings, is untouched.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands and the flag is cleared. The height's guard survived; one of Task 8c's
claims about its own effect did not.**

Task 7c named three tests for the 560 m and the rewritten clause passes all three. The subject is *the map*,
so the height is attributed to the record and not to the ground. It still sits after a semicolon and a
change of subject, so "6.4 km" and "560 m" are not handed to a reader as a ratio. And **it still names the
count of five**, which is the guard the shared paragraph demands: without it a reader could infer that the
twenty-one temples carrying no `elevation` are lower, and they are neither lower nor higher. Nothing in the
fourteen calls this day a climb, and this pass added no such word.

- **Recorded: the distance convention's sharpest consequence did not leave the package, only the narrative.**
  Task 8c writes that removing "17.4 km" means "the walker now sees one, the declared figure". The walker
  sees both. **The reflection still reads "Sixteen places mapped in 17.4 km"** — the vertex span — while

- **"Sixteen places are mapped on this day, the fewest of any day here" is verified** at 16 features
  carrying `stageIndex: 6`, the section minimum, 12 of them bus stops, none dropped.
- **"just past it" for 0.369 km names a range the measurement sits inside** and is therefore the weaker

narrative:  The day walks 14.7 km before it reaches a temple, and then six come inside 13.2 km.   7c's own condition**: the drop statement was defended as "the only form that stays honest when a count is

  reaches the day*, which is 49 of the 83 that reach and 49 of the 84 filed — a majority under both, so the
  claim cannot be falsified by the hospital under either reading, and it is strictly weaker than a counted

  **The hospital is not lost, only relocated to where a reviewer looks for it**: `node/1423738628`
  杏林会東明病院, 325 m by the report and 322.5 m measured, the farthest waypoint from the walked line
  anywhere in this section, is still named in the Places line and the grounding bullet above, and still

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands; the flag is cleared, and the sentence this pass was named for is

**The hospital sentence, verified.** "Eighty-four places are filed on the day, forty-nine of them bus stops;

**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged. This is the entry I came closest to striking, and the
reason I did not is recorded in full.**

Re-measured: span vertex 10169 → vertex 11158 is **31.65137 km**, `snap`; ゲストハウス BEKKU
projects **66.65 m short** of its own vertex, the widest Δ in the section and four times the next
✓. `temple-59` **277.2735 / 54.8 m**; 277.2735 − 262.590 = **14.683 km** ✓; steps **3.098,
2.973, 2.377, 6.236** ✓. Imabari **262.804 / 290.7 m** — inside the build's 300 m and not
dropped ✓ — 0.221 km past the vertex, and the last of the seven `type: town` features, with
nothing of that type in the remaining 102.9 km ✓. 88 features carry `stageIndex: 10`, 41
`bus_stop`, 5 with a `templeNumber` ✓. Independently counted, **52** of the 88 project before
Iyo Kokubun-ji and **36** from it onward, which reproduces the drafter's own figures ✓. No drops
✓.

- **"Yokomine-ji, the next temple, is 27.7 km further still" — recorded, not struck.** The base
  of "further" is ambiguous and the two readings differ by 17 km. From Iyo Kokubun-ji it is
  **27.745 km** and true, the figure the step list and the grounding bullet both declare. From
  "where the day stops", the position named immediately before it, it is **10.784 km** and the
  sentence is false. A reader who adds 17.0 and 27.7 gets 44.7 km for a leg that measures 27.7.
  I did not strike it, on the rule this file's second reader named: **strike what is false under
  every reading, record what is false only under one of them.** The four clauses struck before
  this — "the second-longest such stretch", "nearly twice the next day's count", "most of what
  the day holds arrives in its last six hundred metres", "ordinary" — had no true reading at all.
  This one has a true reading, it is the reading the grounding declares, and the clause is a
  grounded measurement rather than an ungrounded characterisation. Deleting it would remove a
  true fact the section preamble itself lists; replacing "further still" with "on from Iyo
  Kokubun-ji" would fix the ambiguity and is a rewrite, which is authorship and not review. **If
  one sentence in the fourteen is later re-opened, it should be this one**, and the fix is four
  words.
- **"Then nothing" means no temple, and the clause it leads carries the word.** "Then nothing:
  past Iyo Kokubun-ji the line runs 17.0 km to where the day stops, and **Yokomine-ji, the next
  temple**, is …" — the colon makes the following clause the definition of "nothing", and that
  clause is about temples. The theme ("none after") and the reflection ("none in the last
  seventeen") both repeat the sense. The stretch is not empty and the narrative never says it is:
  36 of the day's 88 places stand in it.
- **Recorded: this is the second of the three entries the distance convention moves.** "the line
  runs **17.0 km** to where the day stops" is 16.962 to the boundary vertex, which is where the
  shipped slice ends, and 16.895 to BEKKU's own projection, which would print **16.9**. BEKKU's
  −66.65 m is what puts the rounding on the boundary.
- **Two temples romanise as "Taisan-ji", and neither entry should say so.** `temple-52` 太山寺
  here on stage 8 and `temple-56` 泰山寺 on this day. Telling a walker on stage 10 that a temple
  on stage 8 shares its spelling would be a sentence about another day's package, which is
  precisely the cross-stage form the pilgrimage has not settled. Both narratives give the
  kilometre, which distinguishes them without a note.
- Noted: "Eighty-eight places on **31.6 km**" is `stages.json`'s declared `distanceKm`; the
  boundary span is 31.651 and would print 31.7. Both are grounded, and the section prints
  sometimes one and sometimes the other — see stage 12.

words.**

```
theme:      Five gates in the first fifteen kilometres, and none after
narrative:  Nankōbō opens this day and four more temples follow inside 14.7 km — Taisan-ji 3.1 km on, Eifuku-ji 3.0 km after that, Senyū-ji 2.4 km after that, and Iyo Kokubun-ji. Then nothing: Yokomine-ji, the next temple, is 27.7 km on from Iyo Kokubun-ji, and the day walks 17.0 km of that before it stops. Imabari stands 0.2 km into the day, the last town mapped in Iyo. Forty-one bus stops stand on it.
reflection: Five gates in the first fifteen kilometres and none in the last seventeen. Which half of the day will you remember having walked?
```

- **"and Yokomine-ji, the next temple, is 27.7 km further still" → "Yokomine-ji, the next temple, is 27.7 km
  on from Iyo Kokubun-ji, and the day walks 17.0 km of that before it stops".**
  Task 7c recorded this as the one clause in the fourteen that a final pass should look at again: the base
  of "further" is ambiguous, the two readings differ by 17 km, and under the reading a walker most naturally
  takes — from "where the day stops", the position named immediately before it — the sentence is false. Task
  7c did not strike it, on the rule that you strike what is false under every reading and record what is
  false only under one; it named the fix as four words, "on from Iyo Kokubun-ji", and said that applying
  them is authorship and not review. **This is that pass, and those are the four words**, adopted verbatim
  rather than reinvented.
  **Nothing is asserted that was not asserted before.** 27.745 km from `temple-59` is the figure the step
  list and the grounding bullet have always declared; what changes is that it now has one reading. The
  clause order is inverted so that the two figures sit in the relation the arithmetic actually holds — 17.0
  km is *part of* the 27.7, not added to it — which is precisely the inference the old wording destroyed.
  **Task 7c's other ruling on this sentence survives the inversion**: "Then nothing" is still defined by the

  does between Iyo Kokubun-ji and here, and nothing in the fourteen claims to. The alternative to
  the clause is silence about a measured number, which is not more honest, only quieter.

- **"its waypoint carries a height of 750 m — the highest of the five heights any temple in Iyo records" →

  temple itself" → "across 14.5 km only the temple and three public toilets are mapped".** Two cumulative
  kilometres and one redundant count out — *four things* was 3 + 1 stated twice — and *the record holds*

  temples still named and the run still given as "four temples inside 6.2 km". This is Awa's stage 2 move:

  four steps (8.652 → 1.497 → 1.438 → 3.276) **that the same sentence prints in full**", and the sentence no
  longer prints them. What it does print is the run those steps make — four temples inside 6.2 km, against

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands and the flag is cleared. "Then the spacing closes" lost the evidence Task
7c named for it, and here is the ruling.**

**The question.** Task 7c cleared the phrase as "a reading of four steps (8.652 → 1.497 → 1.438 → 3.276)
**that the same sentence prints in full**", and the sentence no longer prints them. Task 8c dropped them
under Awa's stage 2 rule — where a figure is a summary's evidence, drop the figure and keep the summary —
and recorded the trade rather than hiding it. It is the only place on this branch where an editing pass
reduced an argument's stated support instead of transferring it.

**The ruling: it stands, and the phrase claims nothing the page no longer prints.** Three reasons. **First,
the claim did not change.** "The spacing closes" says the gaps between temples get smaller, and the sentence
still prints the closing itself — "four temples inside 6.2 km" — which is the 6.210 km holding three of the
four steps, unchanged. **Second, the contrast that makes it a *closing* is in the sentence immediately
before**, which this pass also rewrote and which now reads "Little stands near it: across 14.5 km only the
temple and three public toilets are mapped". Re-measured here, **exactly four** features project between
296.848 and 311.372 km — `temple-60` at 305.019 and three unnamed toilets at 303.071, 304.916 and 305.461 —
so a walker meets one temple standing alone across 14.5 km and then four temples inside 6.2, which is the
closing and its evidence, one clause apart. **Third, what a reading of this kind goes wrong by is being a
characterisation with nothing behind it** — Awa's struck "ordinary" — and this one has a measured span with
a count in it on either side of it. The four steps remain in the grounding bullet above for a reader who
wants the sequence rather than the run.

**Neither alternative was an improvement.** Restoring the drafted clause verbatim would put back "Kōon-ji
**at 313.7 km**", a kilometre measured from the section's start and the one thing Task 8c removed from all
fourteen narratives; writing my own bridge would leave this stage drafted for a wording that is not false.

**Reviewed in Task 7c. Stands unchanged. The text is honest; the `warnings` question is not a
review's to answer.**

Re-measured: span vertex 12229 → vertex 13509 is **32.74572 km**, the longest of the fourteen,
**292.4 m** longer than stage 3 ✓, `snap`. **No `type: accommodation` feature projects strictly
between `temple-64` at 319.88102 and the day's end vertex at 352.62674** — checked against all 41
in the section, which run 316.551 and then 352.631 ✓. **No feature carrying a `templeNumber`
projects strictly inside that stretch either**; `temple-64` stands at the day's own start ✓.
365.677 − 319.881 = **45.796 km**, third in the step list behind 70.442 and 49.675 ✓, and 32.746
+ 13.050 = 45.796 exactly, so this day's figure and stage 13's tile the step ✓. 84 features carry
`stageIndex: 12`, **59** `bus_stop` — second only to stage 2's 62 ✓ — and of the 25 that are not,
6 `sacred_site` (all `church`), 4 `hospital` and 4 `convenience_store` make 14, so "most of what
is left" is 14 of 25 ✓. Iyo-Doi **348.598 / 113.9 m**, the day's only station, 4.029 km before
the end vertex ✓. No drops ✓.

- **"no place to stay at all" is honest, and the sentence's own subject is what makes it so.**
  The clause is "between Maegami-ji and the hotel it ends at **the record marks** no place to
  stay at all". The record is the grammatical subject; the claim is about what is marked. The
  reflection then makes the gap between record and road its explicit question — "How far do you
  trust a map to know where you can stop?" — and calls the absence "no bed **marked** between its
  ends". Awa's stage 3 needed its `warnings` entry because its narrative asserted the absence
  flatly; this one carries the qualification inside the sentence and again in the reflection.
  There is nothing here to strike.
- **The `warnings` question is outside a review's remit and I leave it open, as its drafter
  did.** Whether a 32.7 km day with no marked lodging should ship a `warnings` array is a change
  outside `interior`; recording that the text does not depend on one is the most this task can
  settle. It does not.
- **The reflection is not too clever.** It asks the reader to hold the same distinction the
  narrative just drew, which is the only way a data-derived absence can be stated without either
  overclaiming or going silent.
- **Found in the evidence, not the prose: the grounding bullet sources "32.8 km" to the spans.**
  It reads "'Iyo's longest day, 32.8 km' — the spans listed under stage 6; 32.746 is the
  largest", and 32.746 rounds to **32.7**. 32.8 is `stages.json`'s declared `distanceKm`, which
  is anchor to anchor (352.63107 − 319.88102 = 32.750). The **claim** is true under either
  measure — 32.8 is the largest declared figure and 32.746 the largest span — so the narrative
  does not move; the bullet cites the wrong one and is corrected here rather than rewritten.
- Noted: the same day is therefore given two lengths one clause apart — "Iyo's longest day,
  **32.8 km**" (declared) and "this day walks **32.7** of them" (span, and the figure that makes
  32.7 + 13.1 = 45.8 come out). Both are true of what they measure and a reader of the package
  sees both. It is the clearest instance of the section printing sometimes the declared figure
  and sometimes the span; see the section blockquote.
- **The end hotel is 290 m off the line and 4.33 m past its own boundary vertex**, confirmed —
  the farthest-off day-end anchor in the section, inside the 500 m the snap carries, and one of
  the six misfilings. The narrative calls it "the hotel it ends at" and claims nothing about its
  distance from the line, so nothing here is asserted that the measurement contradicts.

reflection: The longest day in the section, and no bed mapped between its ends. How far do you trust a map to know where you can stop?

- **"between Maegami-ji and the hotel it ends at **the record marks** no place to stay at all" → "between

## shikoku-88-sanuki — Sanuki (Temples 65-88, and the return to Temple 1)

> **Reviewed in Task 7a**, by the same reader who took Awa and who drafted neither. All six stages
> cleared; two clauses struck, on stages 0 and 3. Every figure was re-measured independently before

> superlative**: stage 0's "the second-longest such stretch in Sanuki" is third-longest, because

> temple positions including the `temple-73`/`temple-72` inversion, all 389 waypoint projections,
> the four-way exact co-projection at Zentsū-ji, and every cited node id — reproduces exactly as
> written, and the four near-miss drops were confirmed absent from the prose.

> **Edited in Task 8c**, in the same pass as `shikoku-88-iyo` and under the house style Task 8a set on Awa.
> All six narratives and three reflections were edited; no theme was, because none carried the dataset's
> vocabulary. **No measurement was re-opened and none was changed**, and neither of Task 7a's two struck
> clauses was restored.
>
> **Three of this section's standing decisions were treated as binding on the editor.** "Gate" and "bed"
> stay out of all six — the section's deliberate abstention, recorded above, and one this pass had every
> chance to break, since both words are the shortest way to say what stages 3, 4 and 5 are about; they are
> not used. The section-local bound on every superlative survives, including the 911 m, which is still
> ranked against this section's own seven heights and not across the eighty-eight. And the refusal of the
> `end.note`'s "closing return" survives: stage 5 gained none of *return, circle, close, complete, finish*
> or *nirvana*. **Two of those words were already in the shipped text and are left alone** — "Ryōzen-ji
> **closes** Sanuki", the same section-boundary verb Awa's stage 4 and Iyo's stage 13 use, and the
> reflection's "Who decides when a walk is **finished**?", which Task 7a cleared by name. Task 7a's scan
> read the day for the *reading* of the circuit rather than for the strings, and it was right that the
> reading is absent; this note records the strings so a later reader is not surprised by them.
>
> **Figures per narrative**, on Task 8b's convention: 14 · 17 · 15 · 13 · 19 · 13 before, mean 15.2 and
> worst 19 — the heaviest register of the four sections; **8 · 10 · 8 · 8 · 8 · 9** after, mean **8.5** and
> worst **10**, which is Tosa's worst. **Dataset vocabulary 25 tokens → 0.** Three cumulative section
> kilometres removed; the rest of this section's distances were already measured from the day's own start.
> Sanuki does not reach Awa's 7.2 or Tosa's 6.9 and the reason is structural rather than editorial: four of
> its six days are the figure-densest in the pilgrimage — nine temples on one, the last three of the
> eighty-eight on another, a day whose whole subject is a 321 m margin, and a closing stretch that *is* a
> sequence of measured blanks. Each is named in its own entry.

**Read in Task 9b**, by the same reader who read Iyo's fourteen, with no part in the drafting, no part in
any of the three editing passes, and no part in Task 9a's reading of Awa and Tosa. **All six stages stand;
nothing was restored and nothing was rewritten**, so all six lines are ticked again and all six flags are
cleared. Task 7a's grounding was not re-opened; what was re-opened is the six narratives' and three
reflections' changed wording, read clause by clause against the text Task 8c changed it from.

**No edit was found that strengthened a claim.** Twenty-five dataset tokens left these six narratives and
every absence keeps its hedge inside the clause, in the verb — "nothing is mapped in the 10.0 km before it"
(stage 0), "nothing on it is mapped as a town" (stage 0), "holding nothing mapped at all" (stage 1),
"nothing is mapped as a place to stay for 86.1 km" (stage 3), "past Ōkubo-ji nothing else is mapped as a
place to stay or a town" (stage 5). None of the six carries the shape Task 9a found in Tosa and Task 9b
found once in Iyo.

**Both places where a hedge was dropped rather than re-verbed are places where nothing is owed one**, and
both were checked rather than accepted: stage 1's "the narrowest step between temples in this section" and
its reflection's "The closest two temples in this section", where *records* goes because the temples are an
exhaustively known set of 23 `templeNumber` features from the official-site source, which is Task 9a's own
ruling on Tosa's "No temple has stood on the way for 61.7 km". **Task 7a's argued point on that clause was
the section bound, not the hedge, and "in this section" carries it word for word** — 0.117 km is the
narrowest step measured on any of the four lines and the sentence still does not say so. The same discipline
holds for the 911 m on stage 0: it still ranks *the seven heights the map gives this section's temples*, it
still names the sixteen that carry none, it still stops at Sanuki, and it still stands in its own sentence
between one carrying 20.5/10.0 km and one carrying a metre figure, so it cannot be read as a gradient.

**The closing return's refusal survives, and the inventory of it is corrected here.** Task 8c reports that
*two* of the words the drafter refused were already in the shipped text — stage 5's "Ryōzen-ji **closes**
Sanuki" and its reflection's "Who decides when a walk is **finished**?" — and that is exact **for stage 5**,
which is the day it was speaking of. Scanned across all six, the list's words stand in **four** places on
three stages: those two, plus stage 1's "Konzō-ji **closes** the day" and stage 4's "because the temple
sequence **completes** there". All four are pre-existing, none was introduced or removed by Task 8c, and all
four are right to stand. Three are the corpus's ordinary boundary verb, which closes a *day* or a *section*
and not a circuit — Awa's stage 4 and Iyo's stage 13 use it in the same words, and removing it from stage 5
would break a formula all four sections share. The fourth, stage 4's *completes*, is the most loaded of them
and is the one Task 7a cleared by argument rather than by silence: it is a verbatim restatement of that
stage's own committed `end.note` ("the temple sequence completes here"), it is what makes the eighty-eighth
temple read as an ending the cut *held* rather than one it chose, and it says the numbering completes and
not that the walk does. **What the refusal is for is the reading of the circuit, and no sentence in the six
makes it**: none of *return*, *circle*, *nirvana* appears anywhere, stage 5 gained none of the six words,
and the day is still given as two temples, a distance, and what is mapped between them in the order the line
passes it.

**One concern is recorded rather than acted on**, under stage 1, where "project onto the line" became "meet
the way at exactly the point Zentsū-ji does". The four-way co-projection was re-measured here and is exact
to fourteen decimal places — **63.80976512540966 km** for all four — but the three places stand **283.1**,
**295.9** and **102.8 m** off the line and Zentsū-ji itself **102.5**, so *meet* is doing work *project*
did not. It stands, and why is set out in its entry.

Temple 65 Sankaku-ji to Temple 88 Ōkubo-ji and on to Temple 1 Ryōzen-ji, **202.234 km** of
walked line (`ways/report.json` `walkedLine`, 9,020 points), cut into six days. Twenty-three
waypoints here carry a `templeNumber`, 66 through 88: Temple 65 is this section's start anchor,
taken verbatim from `overview.startPoint`, and its own numbered waypoint belongs to
`shikoku-88-iyo`. Temple 1 is this section's end anchor and its numbered waypoint belongs to
`shikoku-88-awa`, so **this is the only one of the four sections whose end anchor is a temple it
does not hold**. The day cut ends three of the six days at a temple — stages 1, 4 and 5 — and
the other three at a lodging or a town.

Temple positions on the line, which most of the text below is arithmetic on:

`temple-66` 20.547 · `temple-67` 30.321 · `temple-68` 39.073 · `temple-69` 39.190 ·
`temple-70` 43.966 · `temple-71` 55.704 · `temple-73` 59.678 · `temple-72` 60.135 ·
`temple-74` 62.310 · `temple-75` 63.810 · `temple-76` 67.618 · `temple-77` 71.661 ·
`temple-78` 78.843 · `temple-79` 84.744 · `temple-80` 91.827 · `temple-81` 98.526 ·
`temple-82` 103.438 · `temple-83` 115.788 · `temple-84` 130.371 · `temple-85` 135.922 ·
`temple-86` 142.837 · `temple-87` 149.711 · `temple-88` 162.432 km — and then the section's end
anchor at Temple 1, **202.234 km**. That list is in along-line order and **it is not in
numbering order**: `temple-73` projects 0.457 km before `temple-72`. See "One pair the line puts

The steps, in numbering order from the section's own 0.000 km at Temple 65: 20.547, 9.774,
8.752, 0.117, 4.776, 11.738, 4.431, −0.457, 2.631, 1.500, 3.808, 4.043, 7.182, 5.901, 7.083,
6.698, 4.912, 12.350, 14.583, 5.551, 6.916, 6.873, 12.721 km — 162.432 km to Temple 88 — and
then 39.802 km from Temple 88 to Temple 1. They sum to 202.234, the whole line.

Four properties of that list carry most of the narratives below:

- **One step runs over 30 km, and it is the closing return** — 39.802 km, Temple 88 to Temple 1.
  `metadata.json`'s `description` declares exactly that: "it is the only leg in this section
  longer than 30 km". The measurement holds; the widest of the other twenty-three is 20.547 km,
  Temple 65 to Temple 66.
- **The narrowest step is Temple 68 → 69 at 0.117 km**, which `description` declares as 0.11 km.
  It is the narrowest step this section records and the narrowest measured anywhere on this
  pilgrimage; the narrative below claims only the first, on the discipline recorded under
  "Superlatives".
- **Not one of the twenty-three temple-to-temple steps falls inside the 25–30 km band the day
  cut looks in.** The near misses are `temple-67` at 30.321 km, 321 m past the band measured
  from the section's start, and — measured from stage 1's end — `temple-80` 0.791 km short of it
  and `temple-81` 0.908 km past. Both near misses carry a narrative below.
- **Nine of the twenty-three temples stand on one day.** Stage 1 holds Temples 68 to 76 inside
  29.331 km. Temples per day, counting each day's own start out: **2, 9, 2, 7, 3, 0.**

**Places.** `waypoints.geojson` holds **389** features. Per stage, by `stageIndex`: 30, 75, 85,
163, 29, 7. Of the 389, **98 are bus stops**, 62 restaurants, 44 vending machines, 34 convenience
stores, 27 `sacred_site`/`church`, 25 toilets, 23 cafés, 23 temples, 13 train stations, 12
pharmacies, 9 accommodation (5 `guesthouse`, 3 `hotel`, 1 `hostel`), 6 towns (4 `city`, 2
`town`), 5 hospitals, 4 `sacred_site`/`wayside_shrine`, 2 viewpoints, 1 clinic and 1 fountain.
One of the nine accommodation features is unnamed — `node/1019988758` at 60.214 km — and it is
one of the two `metadata.json`'s `provenance` says the day-cut rule filters for itself.

**Coverage.** `ways/report.json`'s `places` block reports `placesPerStage: 9.3` with
`sparse: false` — the mean of `momentsBeyondEnds`, which runs 6, 18, 6, 22, 3, 1 across the six
days. No day is a zero, so nothing here needs Iyo's qualification. **Nine places to stay in
202.234 km** is the figure that shapes three of the six narratives, not the moment count.

### What makes this section's preamble different in kind

Awa's entry flagged that this one would be, and it is: **every other section of this pilgrimage
ends where the next one begins, and this one ends where the first one began.**
`shikoku-88-awa`'s `overview.startPoint` and this section's `overview.endPoint` are the same
literal value, `[134.503, 34.16, 15]` — the same three numbers in both files — so the
pilgrimage's line ends on the coordinate it started from. That is a fact about two files, and it
is the whole of what this document can say about it.

**What §6 does not admit is the arrival.** Nothing in `routes/shikoku-88-sanuki/` records a
pilgrim reaching Temple 1, what it is to have walked 1,140.9 km, or what the eighty-eighth temple
is for. The files hold a line, a distance, a temple at each end and seven marked places between
them. So stage 5's narrative describes the day and its reflection asks a question, and neither of
them tells the walker what the day means: the meaning is the one thing the walker brings and the
data does not. A reviewer should read stage 5's entry with that as the test — not "is this moving
enough" but "is any sentence here claiming something the files do not hold".

### What Sanuki refused, on top of the shared list above

Everything under "What was refused" binds here unchanged. Beyond it, this section offered nine
things the other three did not, or offered them differently.

- **"Nirvana" as what Sanuki means.** It sits verbatim in this section's own `description` —
  "Sanuki carries the fourth of the four stages of spiritual progression the pilgrimage maps onto
  Shikoku's four provinces: nirvana" — and again in `cultural.historicalSignificance`. Refused,
  on the reasoning that refused "awakening" for Awa, "discipline" for Tosa and "enlightenment"
  for Iyo: §6's two sentences must both hold, and a dōjō name's meaning passes the second and
  fails the first, and attaches to the section rather than to any day of it. **The refusal binds
  hardest here**, because the closing return is the one day of the forty where that word would
  look earned. It is refused there too, and stage 5's narrative does not contain it. **No
  narrative below names the dōjō or its meaning.**
- **"Highest temple on the pilgrimage."** `temple-66`'s `description` field reads "Cloud
  Monastery — highest temple on the pilgrimage at 911m." and `overview.elevationRange`'s
  `maxMetersNote` restates it. Both refused, and the first is the sharper case: it is a
  `description` on a temple waypoint, which is what Tosa refused for Cape Muroto and Iyo for the
  Cave Temple, and writing "the highest temple on the pilgrimage" would be lifting the refused
  field almost word for word. The **number** 911 is an `elevation` tag on a temple and is used;
  the superlative attached to it is not. See "The one height used".
- **`description` fields on five more temple waypoints.** `temple-68` and `temple-69` "Shares
  grounds with Temple 69/68.", `temple-75` "Birthplace of Kukai (Kobo Daishi). Darkness ritual in
  the Daishi Hall basement.", `temple-84` "Panoramic views of the Seto Inland Sea.", `temple-88`
  "Final temple. Pilgrims traditionally leave their walking staff here." All refused on the same
  precedent. The last two are the ones a drafter most wants: `temple-84`'s is a scenic
  characterisation with no node behind it, and `temple-88`'s is the staff practice the shared list
  already refuses, restated on the one temple where it would land hardest. **The 0.117 km between
  Temples 68 and 69 is a measurement and is used; "shares grounds" is not.**
- **`tags` on five temple waypoints** — `temple-66` `["temple","highest","phase-boundary"]`,
  `temple-81` and `temple-82` `["temple","mountain"]`, `temple-84` `["temple","viewpoint"]`,
  `temple-88` `["temple","end","completion"]`. Refused exactly as Tosa and Iyo refused the same
  field. `mountain` is terrain with no profile behind it, and `end` and `completion` are the
  reading of the closing return this section is not allowed to make. That `temple-84` carries the
  tag `viewpoint` while a separate `type: viewpoint` waypoint stands 234 m off the line 282 m
  before it is the clearest illustration of why the field is not evidence: one of the two is a
  node with a position, the other is a word.
- **Kūkai's birthday at Temple 75.** `cultural.events[0]` gives a date and names Zentsū-ji as
  Kūkai's birthplace, and the temple stands on stage 1. Refused twice over: it is a date, which
  §6 forbids outright, and it is the Kūkai claim the shared list refuses. Zentsū-ji is named below
  only as a temple at 63.810 km with three other places projecting onto the same point of the line.
- **The Utazu station.** `node/7825993173`, `train_station`, name "Utazu" 宇多津, at 77.843 km on
  stage 2 — **dropped by `build-ways` at 303 m**. The town named Utazu is a different node,
  `node/1012320127` at 79.117 km and 258 m off, and it *is* named. So the word "Utazu" appears
  once below, as a town, while a station of the same name on the same day never reaches the
  package. Recorded here so a reviewer checking the drop list does not read the name as a citation
  of the dropped node.
- **The 39.84 km figure for the closing return.** `osm.note` gives relation 13766029 as 39.84 km
  where the walked line measures **39.802**. Stage 5 uses 39.8, which is what `stages.json`
  declares and what the line measures. See "What the description declares, measured".
- **Six of the seven temple elevations.** `temple-71` 160, `temple-81` 250, `temple-82` 330,
  `temple-84` 280, `temple-85` 200 and `temple-88` 450 were available and are not used. Four of
  the six stand on stage 3 alone, and using them there would do exactly what Iyo's entry warns
  about: make a day of 59.611 km look as though it had a profile, when nothing in this repository
  says what the ground does between any two points on it.
- **Any account of the return beyond its geometry.** Stage 5's committed `end.note` calls the
  stretch "the circuit's closing return"; the narrative does not use the phrase, because
  "closing" does work the line does not do. The day is described as a temple at each end, a
  distance, and what the record marks between them.

### The one height used

`temple-66` 911 m appears in stage 0's narrative, phrased as a fact about the waypoint — "Its
waypoint carries a height of 911 m, the highest of the seven heights Sanuki's temples record; the
other sixteen carry none." Four things were done deliberately, and a reviewer should rule on each:

- **The sentence names the count of seven and the sixteen silent temples**, for Iyo's reason: a
  height given without that count lets a reader infer the sixteen are lower. They are not lower or
  higher; nothing is recorded for them.
- **The superlative stops at the section.** 911 is the largest of the eighteen heights the four
  sections' temple waypoints carry, and the narrative does not say so — because that claim needs
  three other sections' files, and because it is the sentence `temple-66`'s refused `description`
  already makes. A reviewer who thinks the pilgrimage-wide form is the honest one should say so;
  it is one clause's difference and it is deliberate.
- **The height stands nowhere near a distance that could be read as a gradient.** It sits in its
  own sentence, after the sentence carrying 20.5 km and 10.0 km and before the one carrying
  30.3 km. The failure mode is the one the shared paragraph warns about, and it is sharper here
  than anywhere in Iyo: 911 is a number a walker will *want* to read as a climb, and **nothing in
  this repository says what the ground does between any two of these points.**
- **Nothing else changed because of it.** Delete the sentence and no other sentence in the six
  needs rewriting — the same measure Iyo reports of its two.

### What the description declares, measured

Iyo found that `metadata.json`'s `description` quotes leg distances that are relation lengths
rather than walked ones — Temple 43 → 44 declared 70.3 km against a measured 70.442. **Sanuki's
description does not repeat that.** Its four numbers agree with the walked line to within 34 m,
and three of the four to within 7 m:

| declared in `description` | measured on `route.main.geojson` | difference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 202.2 km for the section | 202.234 km | +34 m |
| Temple 68 → 69, 0.11 km | 0.117 km | +7 m |
| Temple 72 → 73, 0.46 km | 0.457 km (separation) | −3 m |
| the closing return, 39.8 km | 39.802 km | +2 m |

Three things are recorded rather than smoothed:

- **The relation figure is present in this section too, and the `description` does not use it.**
  `osm.note` gives relation 13766029 as **39.84 km** against the walked line's 39.802 — a 38 m gap
  of exactly Iyo's kind. The `description` quotes 39.8, which is the walked figure. So both figures
  sit in one `metadata.json` and the description happens to have taken the right one. That is worth
  a reviewer's eye, because it is luck rather than a rule: nothing in the file says which of the
  two a description should quote, which is why Iyo's took the other.
- **"The temples stand closest together here" is true of the narrowest pair and not of the mean.**
  Temple 68 → 69 at 0.117 km is the narrowest step measured on any of the four lines (Iyo 0.882,
  Awa 1.111, Tosa 3.849). But mean step length runs **Awa 7.022 km over 22 steps against Sanuki
  7.062 km over 23** — Awa is fractionally tighter. No narrative below makes the section-wide
  claim; stage 1 claims only the pair.
- **Temple 72 → 73 is declared as a forward step and the walked line does not walk it forward.**

### One pair the line puts in the other order

`temple-73` Shusshaka-ji projects at **59.678 km** and `temple-72` Mandara-ji at **60.135**, so
along `route.main.geojson` the numbering runs backwards across that one pair and the step measures
**−0.457 km**. Two things about it:

- Both waypoints' coordinates are rounded to three decimals — `temple-72` `[133.75, 34.223]`,
  `temple-73` `[133.75, 34.219]`, about 445 m apart — and both project close to the line, 18 m and
  32 m. Nothing here establishes which one the walking reaches first. What *is* established is
  that they project 0.457 km apart, in that order, on the committed line.
- **So stage 1's narrative gives the separation and not the order**: "two of them, Mandara-ji and
  Shusshaka-ji, 0.46 km apart". The pair is named in numbering order and the sentence makes no
  claim about which comes first on the ground. A reviewer should decide whether that is the right
  handling or whether the pair should have stayed out of the prose altogether.

It is the only inversion in the pilgrimage: every other step in all four sections' numbering is
positive along its own line.

### The four that reach the enrichment corridor and not the stage

`ways/report.json`'s per-stage `dropped` arrays hold four entries between them — one on stage 1,
one on stage 2, two on stage 3 — and its top-level `dropped` is empty, so a reader checking only
the top-level list sees nothing and has to go stage by stage. **All four are ordinary near misses
of the 300 m limit**: the report's figures are 302, 303, 304 and 308 m. Nothing here behaves like
Tosa's Ashizuri cluster. The check still had to be run, because the two lines are further apart on
this section than on any other: enrichment measures against `route.geojson`, **371.094 km** across
9,677 points, and `build-ways` against `route.main.geojson`, **202.234 km** across 9,020 — a factor
of **1.835**. Measured with `projectOnLine` against `route.main.geojson`, **no waypoint in this
section stands more than 307 m from the walked line**, so on Sanuki the trap does not spring.

| waypoint | `report.json` | off `route.main.geojson` | off `route.geojson` | stage |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `node/2358488810` unnamed convenience store | 302 m | 297 m | 297 m | 1 |
| `node/7825993173` Utazu, train station | 303 m | 303 m | 297 m | 2 |
| `node/4806012099` 国分八幡宮 | 304 m | 304 m | 206 m | 3 |
| `node/4627855268` unnamed toilet | 308 m | 307 m | 280 m | 3 |

**None of the four is named in any narrative**, and the Utazu row is the one to watch — see "What
Sanuki refused" above.

### Where Sanuki's day-ends are filed

`metadata.json`'s `provenance` records that 12 of the pilgrimage's 36 mid-route day-end places are
filed on the day that *begins* there rather than the one that arrives. **None of the twelve is in
Sanuki**, checked on the committed line; the twelve are six in Tosa and six in Iyo.

| day-end | projects at | boundary vertex | Δ | filed on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `node/12953439381` Fujikawa Ryokan | 38.28657 km | 38.28680 km (vertex 1714) | −0.23 m | stage 0, the arriving day |
| `temple-76` Konzō-ji | 67.61793 km | 67.61793 km (vertex 2833) | 0.00 m | stage 1, the arriving day |
| `node/1012320253` Sakaide | 82.47898 km | 82.49094 km (vertex 3359) | −11.96 m | stage 2, the arriving day |
| `node/1012320229` Sanuki | 142.08986 km | 142.10150 km (vertex 6293) | −11.64 m | stage 3, the arriving day |
| `temple-88` Ōkubo-ji | 162.43169 km | 162.43169 km (vertex 7126) | 0.00 m | stage 4, the arriving day |

All seven boundaries snapped; none went proportional, and the first and last snapped to vertex 0
and vertex 9,019, so the stretches tile the line end to end. Every Δ is zero or negative, which is
why nothing here is misfiled: the sign of Δ decides every row across the pilgrimage, and a day-end
lands on the day that *begins* there only when it projects past its own boundary vertex.
Endpoints below are still read from `stages.json` rather than from any waypoint's `stageIndex`, as
in the other three sections.

### Superlatives, and two things this section did not re-open

**Every superlative in the six narratives below is section-local, with one exception**: "the
longest day of the forty" on stage 3 and "the day after it is the longest of the forty" on stage 2.
Those two are grounded in this section's own `metadata.json` `provenance`, which records that the
cut's realised range runs from 13.05 km to 59.61 km and names this section's stage 3 as the 59.61.
Every other comparison — longest, shortest, narrowest, busiest, fewest — is measured against
Sanuki's own six days or Sanuki's own 389 places, and says so. Where a pilgrimage-wide claim was
true and available it was still not made: `temple-66`'s 911 m and Temple 68 → 69's 0.117 km are
both corpus-wide maxima and both are stated section-locally.

**"Gate" and "bed".** Tosa's stage 0 note asks a reviewer to settle "gate" for a temple once for
the whole pilgrimage; Iyo inherits the question in nine of its fourteen entries, and "bed" for an
`accommodation` waypoint in three of them. **Sanuki uses neither word**, in any of its six
narratives or reflections — temples are called temples and lodgings are called "places to stay".
That is deliberate: whichever way the question is settled, these six entries do not change with it.

**Measurement.** As for Awa, Tosa and Iyo: every along-line kilometre and off-line metre below is
the perpendicular projection of the place's own coordinate onto the committed
`routes/shikoku-88-sanuki/route.main.geojson`, computed with `projectOnLine` from
`scripts/ways/geo.ts`; boundary vertices come from `stageBoundaries` on the same line. Distances
*into* a day are measured from the day's boundary vertex, which is why a figure here can sit a few
metres off the difference of two of the projections above. The twenty-three temple waypoints carry
no OSM id — they come from the Shikoku 88 official-site source `metadata.json` declares under
`provenance.sources` — and are cited as `temple-66` … `temple-88`. Every other id is an
OpenStreetMap node and resolves at `https://api.openstreetmap.org/api/0.6/node/<id>.json`. Each
**Distance** line gives the span between the two boundary vertices, the figure `stages.json`
declares, and `ways/report.json`'s `sliceKm`, `ratio` and `passedGate` for the slice actually
shipped.

**Romanisation.** As in Iyo, the temple waypoints' `name.en` strings carry no macrons — "Unpen-ji",
"Daiko-ji", "Zentsu-ji", "Konzo-ji", "Doryu-ji", "Gosho-ji", "Tenno-ji", "Okubo-ji" — and the
narratives macronise them. Two of `stages.json`'s own stage names are unmacronned in the same way
("Konzo-ji (Temple 76)", "Okubo-ji (Temple 88)") while the narratives say "Konzō-ji" and
"Ōkubo-ji", so title and text differ, exactly as they do on Iyo's stage 9. One reviewer decision
covers both sections.

### The long-day warning, and where it is inconsistent

Stage 3 carries a committed `warnings` entry; stage 5 does not. **This task added neither and
changed neither** — a `warnings` array is outside `interior`, and altering one would be a change
to a stage's declared data under cover of a drafting task. What the drafter found, for whoever
settles it:

- **The flagging is inconsistent across the pilgrimage as it stands.** `shikoku-88-awa` stage 3
  (52.947 km, no lodging and no town anywhere between its ends) carries a warning.
  `shikoku-88-sanuki` stage 3 (59.611 km, the same cause, and the longest day of the forty)
  carries one. `shikoku-88-iyo` stage 12 (32.746 km, whose own narrative says the record marks no
  bed on it) carries none — and neither does `shikoku-88-sanuki` stage 5, which runs 39.802 km
  with exactly one place to stay on it, 187 m from its start, and 24.668 km after that with
  nothing marked at all.
- **The consistent treatment is a stated threshold, not another warning.** The two warned days
  share a property nothing in the files names: *no named accommodation and no town anywhere
  between the day's two ends.* On that reading Iyo stage 12 (a hotel at its end) and Sanuki stage
  5 (a minshuku 187 m in) are both correctly unwarned, and the current set is already consistent —
  but only by accident, because no file states the rule. The honest fix is one sentence in
  `metadata.json`'s `provenance` giving the trigger, after which every flag can be checked rather
  than judged. Adding a warning to stage 5 without that sentence would make the set *look*
  intentional while leaving the next drafter the same guess.
- **If the threshold is set by distance instead, note that it decides four days at once.**
  59.611, 52.947, 39.802 and 38.287 are the four longest of the forty, and the last two are
  1.5 km apart: a 40 km line warns two days, a 38 km line warns four, and both of the extra two
  are in this section.

---

### shikoku-88-sanuki stage 0 — Sankaku-ji (Temple 65) to Fujikawa Ryokan

- **Start** Sankaku-ji (Temple 65) — section boundary, 69 m off the line, at 0.000 km. The
  anchor's coordinates and name are `metadata.json`'s `overview.startPoint`, and
  `shikoku-88-iyo` ends at the same point; its own numbered waypoint, `temple-65`, belongs to that
  section. What stands here in this section's file is the church-subtype twin
  `wp-osm-sacred_site-node4839325648` / `node/4839325648` (`sacred_site` / `church`, name
  "Sankaku-ji"), 31 m off, at 0.000 km — one of the twins `metadata.json`'s `provenance` warns
  about, and not counted as a temple by the cut or by this text
- **End** Fujikawa Ryokan — `wp-osm-accommodation-node12953439381` / `node/12953439381`
  (`accommodation` / `hotel`), 78 m off the line, at 38.287 km. Second arm: no temple falls
  25–30 km past 0.000 km
- **Distance** 38.287 km between the boundary vertices against **38.3 km** declared. Shipped slice
  37.827 km (`ways/report.json` `sliceKm`), ratio 0.9876, `passedGate: true`, `boundaryMode: snap`.
  Previously declared: **none** — this section's stages were cut in Task 5b and have never carried
  another figure
- **Places** 30 filed on the stage; `ways/report.json` drops none of them
- **Grounding the text cites**
  - "Two temples stand on this day" — two features projecting inside 0.000–38.287 km carry a
    `templeNumber`, `temple-66` and `temple-67`
  - "Unpen-ji comes 20.5 km in" — `temple-66`, 13 m off, at **20.547 km**
  - "the 10.0 km before it hold nothing the record marks" — the previous feature is the bus stop
    七田, `wp-osm-transport-node3152603105` / `node/3152603105`, 184 m off, at 10.550 km;
    20.547 − 10.550 = **9.997 km**. The drafted text continued "— the second-longest such stretch
    in Sanuki", and **Task 7a struck that clause as false**; the comparison the drafter recorded
    here ("The section's longest such run is stage 5's 24.668 km; the next after this one is
    stage 4's 8.685") is a comparison of *per-stage maxima*, which is not the same set. See the
    verdict below
  - "Its waypoint carries a height of 911 m, the highest of the seven heights Sanuki's temples
    record; the other sixteen carry none" — `temple-66` `elevation: 911`. Seven of this section's
    twenty-three `templeNumber` features carry an `elevation` — 66, 71, 81, 82, 84, 85, 88 — and
    911 is the largest. See "The one height used"
  - "A viewpoint and a café stand 118 m further along the line" —
    `wp-osm-viewpoint-node13189823602` / `node/13189823602` (`viewpoint`, 毘沙門天展望館), 83 m
    off, and `wp-osm-food-node13347352350` / `node/13347352350` (`food` / `cafe`, "Unpenji Cafe"),
    132 m off, both projecting at **20.665 km**; 20.665 − 20.547 = **0.118 km**. A church-subtype
    twin of the temple, `node/13457236116` (第66番札所 雲辺寺, 16 m off, 20.652 km), stands between
    them and is not cited
  - "Daikō-ji follows at 30.3 km — 321 m past the 25 to 30 km band the day cut looks in" —
    `temple-67`, 37 m off, at **30.321 km**, measured from the day's start at 0.000; the band's far
    edge is 30.000, so it misses by **0.321 km**, and no temple projects inside 25.000–30.000
  - "it runs on to Fujikawa Ryokan at 38.3 km" — the fallback arm's candidates are named
    accommodation and town waypoints; the nearest to 27.5 km is Fujikawa Ryokan at 38.287
    (10.787 km from the target), against `wp-osm-accommodation-node11413648269` /
    `node/11413648269` ("Ohenro House Moriso", `guesthouse`, 30 m off) at 5.803 km (21.697) and
    `node/5980700786` (本木ビジネスホテル, `hotel`, 198 m off) at 43.590 km (16.090)
  - "Thirty places are recorded on the whole of it, fourteen of them bus stops, and not one of them
    a town" — 30 features carry `stageIndex: 0`, of which 14 carry `subtype: bus_stop` and **none**
    carries `type: town`. The section's six towns sit at 51.732, 71.038, 75.439, 79.117, 82.479 and
    142.090 km

```
theme:      Two temples in thirty-eight kilometres
narrative:  Two temples stand on this day. Unpen-ji comes 20.5 km in, and the 10.0 km before it hold nothing the record marks — the second-longest such stretch in Sanuki. Its waypoint carries a height of 911 m, the highest of the seven heights Sanuki's temples record; the other sixteen carry none. A viewpoint and a café stand 118 m further along the line. Daikō-ji follows at 30.3 km — 321 m past the 25 to 30 km band the day cut looks in — so no temple ends this day and it runs on to Fujikawa Ryokan at 38.3 km. Thirty places are recorded on the whole of it, fourteen of them bus stops, and not one of them a town.
reflection: Daikō-ji stands three hundred and twenty-one metres past where a day of this length would have ended. How much of a day is decided by three hundred metres?
```

**Reviewed in Task 7a. One clause struck as false; the rest stands.**

Re-measured against `route.main.geojson` (9,020 points, 202.234 km): `temple-66` **20.547 /
13 m**, `temple-67` **30.321 / 37 m**, the viewpoint and the café both at **20.665** (118 m on),
七田 at **10.550 / 184 m**, span vertex 0 → vertex 1714 = **38.28680 km**, `snap`. Of the 30
features carrying `stageIndex: 0`, **14** are `bus_stop` and **none** is `type: town` ✓, and the
report drops none of them ✓.

- **Struck: "— the second-longest such stretch in Sanuki". It is the third.** Sorting every gap
  between consecutive projected features across all 389 of this section's waypoints gives
  **24.668, 12.597, 9.997, 8.685, 7.613, 6.648, 4.241, 4.088 …** km. Two runs are longer than
  9.997 and **both are on stage 5**: 162.619 → 187.287 (24.668) and 188.347 → 200.944 (12.597).
  The grounding bullet above compares this stage's 9.997 against *the largest run on each other
  day* — 24.668 (stage 5), 8.685 (stage 4), 6.648 (stage 1), 4.241 (stage 3), 2.097 (stage 2) —
  and that list is correct as a list of per-stage maxima, but it silently discards stage 5's
  **second** run, which is 12.597 km. Ranking a stretch against per-stage maxima is not ranking
  it against the section's stretches, and the narrative claimed the latter. The ranking is
  unchanged whether or not the four features beyond 300 m are counted. Deleting the clause
  leaves "Unpen-ji comes 20.5 km in, and the 10.0 km before it hold nothing the record marks."
  — grammatical, and true.
- **The 911 m superlative was rightly stopped at the section, but not for the reason given, and
  the recorded reason is wrong.** The drafter's ground was that the corpus-wide claim "needs
  three other sections' files". It does not: this section's own
  `overview.elevationRange.maxMetersNote` says "Temple 66 Unpen-ji, at 911 m, stands in this
  section and is the highest temple on the whole circuit", so the fact passes §6's second
  sentence through an `overview` field of exactly the class the drafters already cite
  (`overview.distanceKm` is quoted as "a 154.5 km section" on Awa's stage 0). The refusal
  survives on the **first** sentence instead, and on the same reasoning that refused the four
  dōjō names: a ranking of this temple's height against the pilgrimage's other temples is a fact
  about the pilgrimage, not about this section's temple or this day — it attaches to the corpus,
  as "nirvana" attaches to the section. A point height at a gate is a fact about the gate; its
  rank across 88 gates is not. Two further supports: `maxMetersNote` spends its own next sentence
  walking the superlative back ("Pilgrims anywhere on the circuit may cross higher mountain
  passes"), so quoting only the first half would be selective; and the corpus-wide form is
  word-for-word the refused `temple-66` `description`. **The reader does lose Sanuki's most
  striking fact, and that is the right trade** — 911 is the number, and the walker still gets it.
- **The seven/sixteen phrasing is exactly right and should be the corpus's template.** Seven of
  this section's 23 `templeNumber` features carry an `elevation` (66, 71, 81, 82, 84, 85, 88) and
  911 is the largest ✓; naming the count stops a reader inferring the other sixteen are lower.
  They are neither: nothing is recorded for them.
- **The height sits nowhere that could be read as a gradient** — its own sentence, between one
  carrying 20.5/10.0 km and one carrying 30.3 km — and no sentence in these eleven describes
  ascent, descent, a pass, hardness or flatness. Checked by scan across all eleven narratives,
  themes and reflections: zero hits.
- **The reflection stands.** Making the cut's 321 m tie-break the day's subject is a reading of
  a measurement the stage's own `end.note` already records, and it asserts nothing. It is the
  same move Tosa's stages 3, 6 and 12 make and should be settled with them; nothing here turns
  on the answer.

**Edited in Task 8c. The fourth collision: Task 7a called this sentence the corpus's template, and the
template says "waypoint".**

```
theme:      Two temples in thirty-eight kilometres
narrative:  Unpen-ji comes 20.5 km in, and nothing is mapped in the 10.0 km before it. The map gives it a height of 911 m, the highest of the seven it gives this section's temples; the other sixteen carry none. A viewpoint and a café stand just past it. Daikō-ji follows 321 m past the 30 km edge of the band the day cut ends days in, so no temple ends this day and it runs on to Fujikawa Ryokan. Fourteen bus stops stand on it, and nothing on it is mapped as a town.
reflection: Daikō-ji stands three hundred and twenty-one metres past where a day of this length would have ended. How much of a day is decided by three hundred metres?
```

- **"Its waypoint carries a height of 911 m, the highest of the seven heights Sanuki's temples record; the
  other sixteen carry none" → "The map gives it a height of 911 m, the highest of the seven it gives this
  section's temples; the other sixteen carry none".** *Waypoint* and *record* are both on the out-list, and
  Task 7a's clearance quotes the sentence whole and calls "the seven/sixteen phrasing exactly right and …
  the corpus's template". Resolved as Iyo's two heights were, in the same words, so that all three heights
  in this pilgrimage now read alike. **Every one of Task 7a's four tests still passes.** The subject is *the
  map*, so the height is still a fact about the record and not about the ground. **Both counts survive** —
  the seven and the sixteen — which is the guard against a reader inferring the silent sixteen are lower.
  **The superlative still stops at the section**: "this section's temples" is "Sanuki's temples" in the
  clause's new grammar, so it still does not reach across the eighty-eight, which is the refusal Task 7a
  rebuilt from the ground up and settled on §6's first sentence. And it still stands in its own sentence,
  between one carrying 20.5/10.0 km and one carrying a metre figure, so it cannot be read as a gradient.
- **"the 10.0 km before it hold nothing the record marks" → "nothing is mapped in the 10.0 km before it".**
  The hedge into the verb. Task 7a's struck clause — "the second-longest such stretch in Sanuki", which is
  the third — is **not** restored, and this pass makes no rank claim about the stretch at all.
- **"A viewpoint and a café stand 118 m further along the line" → "… stand just past it".** *Along the line*
  is the pipeline's word for `route.main.geojson`, which no walker has a definition for; *just past it*
  names a range containing the 118 m and is therefore the weaker form. The exact co-projection at 20.665 km
  stays in the grounding bullet above.
- **"Daikō-ji follows at 30.3 km — 321 m past the 25 to 30 km band the day cut looks in — so … it runs on to
  Fujikawa Ryokan at 38.3 km" → "Daikō-ji follows 321 m past the 30 km edge of the band the day cut ends
  days in, so … it runs on to Fujikawa Ryokan".** Three figures out and the two that matter kept. **"25 to
  30" comes down to "the 30 km edge" on Tosa's own split**: stages 3 and 12 could drop the band because
  nothing rested on it, and stage 6 had to keep the edge because "the day's whole subject is a 457 m margin
  against that edge". This day's whole subject is a 321 m margin against the same edge, so the edge stays
  and the near edge goes. "30.3 km" and "321 m past 30 km" are the same measurement said twice; the margin
  is the one the reflection is built on, so it is the one that stays.
- **"Two temples stand on this day" — cut**, and **"Thirty places are recorded on the whole of it, fourteen
  of them bus stops, and not one of them a town" → "Fourteen bus stops stand on it, and nothing on it is
  mapped as a town".** The opening count is the theme's own words and both temples are named in the next two
  sentences; the closing total is inventory. What replaces it is a positive existence claim over 14
  `bus_stop` features the report does not drop, plus an absence claim that keeps its hedge in the verb —
  because "no town here" is exactly the kind of claim an unmapped town would falsify, and *mapped* refuses
  it. The section's six towns still sit at 51.732 and beyond, in the grounding bullet.
- The reflection is untouched.

**Read in Task 9b. The edit stands; the flag is cleared.**

Task 7a called this height sentence "the corpus's template" and named four things done deliberately in it.
Every one survives the rewrite. The subject is *the map*, so the height stays a fact about the record and
not about the ground. **The count of seven and the sixteen silent temples both survive**, which is the guard
against a reader inferring the sixteen are lower; they are neither lower nor higher. The superlative still
stops at the section — "this section's temples" is "Sanuki's temples" in the clause's new grammar — so it
still does not reach across the eighty-eight, which is the refusal Task 7a rebuilt from the ground up on
§6's first sentence. And it still stands in its own sentence, between one carrying 20.5 and 10.0 km and one
carrying a metre figure, so it cannot be read as a gradient.

- **Task 7a's struck clause has not come back.** "the second-longest such stretch in Sanuki" — which is the
  third — is absent, and the rewritten sentence makes no rank claim about the 10.0 km at all.
- **"Fourteen bus stops stand on it, and nothing on it is mapped as a town" splits one sentence into a

  carrying `stageIndex: 0` are `bus_stop`, none is `type: town`, and the report drops none of them. "No town
  here" is exactly the claim an unmapped town would falsify, and *mapped* refuses it.

  far edge is 30.000, so the figure is exact and it is the only one this day turns on. Task 8b's split on

- **"stand just past it" for 118 m names a range containing the measurement** and is therefore the weaker
  form; the exact co-projection at 20.665 km stays in the grounding bullet.

**Reviewed in Task 7a. Stands unchanged.**

Re-measured: nine `templeNumber` features project inside 38.287–67.618 ✓ and the six days hold
**2, 9, 2, 7, 3, 0** counting each day's own start out ✓. Jinne-in **0.786** → 0.8 ✓,
Kannon-ji **0.117** ✓, Iyadani-ji **11.738** → 11.7 ✓, the run 45.084 → 51.732 Mitoyo

11.9 ✓. Of the 75 features, **17** are `food` (10 `restaurant` + 7 `cafe`) ✓. The one dropped
node, `node/2358488810` at 302 m, projects at 41.928 and is not named ✓. Span **29.33113**,
